```
1→import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
     2→import { ASPECT_RATIO_TO_RESOLUTION } from "./aspectRatios";
     3→import { nanoid } from "nanoid";
     4→import { AuthError, NetworkError, ValidationError } from "./errors";
     5→import { handleError } from '@/shared/lib/errorHandler';
     6→import { isAbortError } from '@/shared/lib/errorUtils';
     7→
     8→/**
     9→ * Default aspect ratio to use when project aspect ratio is not found
    10→ */
    11→const DEFAULT_ASPECT_RATIO = "1:1";
    12→
    13→/**
    14→ * Interface for project resolution lookup result
    15→ */
    16→interface ProjectResolutionResult {
    17→  resolution: string;
    18→  aspectRatio: string;
    19→}
    20→
    21→/**
    22→ * Resolves the resolution for a project, either using provided custom resolution
    23→ * or looking up the project's aspect ratio and mapping it to a standard resolution.
    24→ * 
    25→ * @param projectId - The project ID to look up
    26→ * @param customResolution - Optional custom resolution (e.g., "1024x768")
    27→ * @returns Promise resolving to the final resolution string
    28→ */
    29→export async function resolveProjectResolution(
    30→  projectId: string, 
    31→  customResolution?: string
    32→): Promise<ProjectResolutionResult> {
    33→  // If custom resolution is provided and valid, use it
    34→  if (customResolution?.trim()) {
    35→    return {
    36→      resolution: customResolution.trim(),
    37→      aspectRatio: "custom"
    38→    };
    39→  }
    40→
    41→  try {
    42→    // Fetch project aspect ratio from database
    43→    const { data: project } = await supabase
    44→      .from("projects")
    45→      .select("aspect_ratio")
    46→      .eq("id", projectId)
    47→      .single();
    48→
    49→    const aspectRatioKey = project?.aspect_ratio ?? DEFAULT_ASPECT_RATIO;
    50→    const resolution = ASPECT_RATIO_TO_RESOLUTION[aspectRatioKey] ?? ASPECT_RATIO_TO_RESOLUTION[DEFAULT_ASPECT_RATIO];
```

```
1→"""Persistent state management for desloppify findings (.desloppify/state.json)."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import fnmatch

    14→from .scoring import TIER_WEIGHTS

    18→class Finding(TypedDict):
    19→    """The central data structure — a normalized finding from any detector."""
    20→    id: str
    21→    detector: str
    22→    file: str
    23→    tier: int              # 1-4
    24→    confidence: str        # "high" | "medium" | "low"
    25→    summary: str
    26→    detail: dict
    27→    status: str            # "open" | "resolved" | "wontfix" | "false_positive"
    28→    note: str | None
    29→    first_seen: str
    30→    last_seen: str

    32→    reopen_count: int

    36→
    37→STATE_DIR = PROJECT_ROOT / ".desloppify"
    38→STATE_FILE = STATE_DIR / "state.json"
    39→
    40→CURRENT_VERSION = 1
    41→
    42→
    43→def _now() -> str:
    44→    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
    45→
    46→
    47→def _empty_state() -> dict:
    48→    return {
    49→        "version": CURRENT_VERSION,
    50→        "created": _now(),
    51→        "last_scan": None,
    52→        "scan_count": 0,
    53→        "config": {"ignore": []},
    54→        "score": 0,
    55→        "stats": {},
    56→        "findings": {},
    57→    }
    58→
    59→
    60→def load_state(path: Path | None = None) -> dict:
    61→    """Load state from disk, or return empty state.
    62→
    63→    Handles corruption gracefully: tries backup, then starts fresh.
    64→    """
    65→    p = path or STATE_FILE
    66→    if not p.exists():
    67→        return _empty_state()
    68→
    69→    try:
    70→        data = json.loads(p.read_text())
    71→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as e:
    72→        # Try backup
    73→        backup = p.with_suffix(".json.bak")
    74→        if backup.exists():
    75→            try:
    76→                data = json.loads(backup.read_text())
    77→                print(f"  \u26a0 State file corrupted ({e}), loaded from backup.", file=sys.stderr)
    78→                return data
    79→            except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError):
    80→                pass
    81→        print(f"  \u26a0 State file corrupted ({e}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    82→        try:
    83→            p.rename(p.with_suffix(".json.corrupted"))
    84→        except OSError:
    85→            pass
    86→        return _empty_state()
    87→
    88→    # Version check
    89→    version = data.get("version", 1)
    90→    if version > CURRENT_VERSION:
    91→        print(f"  \u26a0 State file version {version} is newer than supported ({CURRENT_VERSION}). "
    92→              f"Some features may not work correctly.", file=sys.stderr)
    93→    return data
    94→
    95→
    96→def _json_default(obj):
    97→    """JSON serializer that handles known types and rejects unknowns."""
    98→    if isinstance(obj, set):
    99→        return sorted(obj)
   100→    if isinstance(obj, Path):
   101→        return str(obj)
   102→    if hasattr(obj, "isoformat"):
   103→        return obj.isoformat()
   104→    raise TypeError(f"Object of type {type(obj).__name__} is not JSON serializable: {obj!r}")
   105→
   106→
   107→def save_state(state: dict, path: Path | None = None):
   108→    """Recompute stats/score and save to disk atomically."""
   109→    _recompute_stats(state)
   110→    p = path or STATE_FILE
   111→    p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   112→
   113→    content = json.dumps(state, indent=2, default=_json_default) + "\n"
   114→
   115→    # Atomic write: temp file + rename
   116→    try:
   117→        fd, tmp_path = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=str(p.parent), suffix=".tmp")
   118→        try:
   119→            os.write(fd, content.encode())
   120→            os.fsync(fd)
   121→        finally:
   122→            os.close(fd)
   123→
   124→        # Keep a backup of the previous state
   125→        if p.exists():
   126→            backup = p.with_suffix(".json.bak")
   127→            try:
   128→                import shutil
   129→                shutil.copy2(str(p), str(backup))
   130→            except OSError:
   131→                pass
   132→
   133→        os.replace(tmp_path, str(p))
   134→    except OSError:
   135→        # Fallback to direct write if atomic write fails
   136→        try:
   137→            os.unlink(tmp_path)
   138→        except (OSError, UnboundLocalError):
   139→            pass
   140→        try:
   141→            p.write_text(content)
   142→        except OSError as e:
   143→            import sys
   144→            print(f"  \u26a0 Could not save state: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
   145→
   146→

   148→_EMPTY_COUNTERS = ("open", "fixed", "auto_resolved", "wontfix", "false_positive")
   149→
   150→
   151→def _count_findings(findings: dict) -> tuple[dict[str, int], dict[int, dict[str, int]]]:
   152→    """Tally per-status counters and per-tier breakdowns."""
   153→    counters = dict.fromkeys(_EMPTY_COUNTERS, 0)
   154→    tier_stats: dict[int, dict[str, int]] = {}
   155→    for f in findings.values():
   156→        s, tier = f["status"], f.get("tier", 3)
   157→        counters[s] = counters.get(s, 0) + 1
   158→        ts = tier_stats.setdefault(tier, dict.fromkeys(_EMPTY_COUNTERS, 0))
   159→        ts[s] = ts.get(s, 0) + 1
   160→    return counters, tier_stats
   161→
   162→
   163→def _weighted_progress(findings: dict) -> tuple[float, float]:
   164→    """Compute weighted addressed% and strict-fixed%. Returns (score, strict_score)."""
   165→    total_w = addressed_w = fixed_w = 0
   166→    for f in findings.values():
   167→        w = TIER_WEIGHTS.get(f.get("tier", 3), 2)
   168→        total_w += w
   169→        if f["status"] != "open":
   170→            addressed_w += w
   171→        if f["status"] in ("fixed", "auto_resolved", "false_positive"):
   172→            fixed_w += w
   173→    if total_w == 0:
   174→        return 100.0, 100.0
   175→    return round((addressed_w / total_w) * 100, 1), round((fixed_w / total_w) * 100, 1)
   176→
   177→
   178→def _update_objective_health(state: dict, findings: dict):
   179→    """Compute dimension-based objective health scores from potentials."""
   180→    pots = state.get("potentials", {})
   181→    if not pots:
   182→        return
   183→    from .scoring import merge_potentials, compute_dimension_scores, compute_objective_score
   184→    merged = merge_potentials(pots)
   185→    if not merged:
   186→        return
   187→    ds = compute_dimension_scores(findings, merged, strict=False)
   188→    ss = compute_dimension_scores(findings, merged, strict=True)
   189→    state["dimension_scores"] = {
   190→        n: {"score": ds[n]["score"], "strict": ss[n]["score"], "checks": ds[n]["checks"],
   191→            "issues": ds[n]["issues"], "tier": ds[n]["tier"], "detectors": ds[n].get("detectors", {})}
   192→        for n in ds}
   193→    state["objective_score"] = round(compute_objective_score(ds), 1)
   194→    state["objective_strict"] = round(compute_objective_score(ss), 1)
   195→
   196→
   197→def _recompute_stats(state: dict):
   198→    """Recompute stats, progress scores, and objective health scores from findings."""
   199→    findings = state["findings"]
   200→    counters, tier_stats = _count_findings(findings)
   201→    score, strict_score = _weighted_progress(findings)
   202→    state["stats"] = {
   203→        "total": sum(counters.values()),
   204→        **counters,
   205→        "by_tier": {str(t): ts for t, ts in sorted(tier_stats.items())},
   206→    }
   207→    state["score"] = score
   208→    state["strict_score"] = strict_score
   209→    _update_objective_health(state, findings)
   210→
   211→
   212→def is_ignored(finding_id: str, file: str, ignore_patterns: list[str]) -> bool:
   213→    """Check if a finding matches any ignore pattern (glob, ID prefix, or file path)."""
   214→    for pat in ignore_patterns:
   215→        if "*" in pat:
   216→            target = finding_id if "::" in pat else file
   217→            if fnmatch.fnmatch(target, pat):
   218→                return True
   219→        elif "::" in pat:
   220→            if finding_id.startswith(pat):
   221→                return True
   222→        elif file == pat or file == rel(pat):
   223→            return True
   224→    return False
   225→
   226→
   227→def add_ignore(state: dict, pattern: str) -> int:
   228→    """Add an ignore pattern. Removes matching findings from state. Returns count removed."""
   229→    config = state.setdefault("config", {})
   230→    ignores = config.setdefault("ignore", [])
   231→    if pattern not in ignores:
   232→        ignores.append(pattern)
   233→
   234→    to_remove = [fid for fid, f in state["findings"].items()
   235→                 if is_ignored(fid, f["file"], [pattern])]
   236→    for fid in to_remove:
   237→        del state["findings"][fid]
   238→    return len(to_remove)
   239→
   240→
   241→def make_finding(detector: str, file: str, name: str, *,
   242→                 tier: int, confidence: str, summary: str,
   243→                 detail: dict | None = None) -> Finding:
   244→    """Create a normalized finding dict with a stable ID."""
   245→    rfile = rel(file)
   246→    fid = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{name}" if name else f"{detector}::{rfile}"
   247→    now = _now()
   248→    return {"id": fid, "detector": detector, "file": rfile, "tier": tier,
   249→            "confidence": confidence, "summary": summary, "detail": detail or {},
   250→            "status": "open", "note": None, "first_seen": now, "last_seen": now,
   251→            "resolved_at": None, "reopen_count": 0}
   252→
   253→
   254→def _find_suspect_detectors(
   255→    existing: dict, current_by_detector: dict[str, int], force_resolve: bool,
   256→    ran_detectors: set[str] | None = None,
   257→) -> set[str]:
   258→    """Detectors that had open findings but didn't actually run this scan.
   259→
   260→    A detector is suspect only if:
   261→    1. It had open findings before
   262→    2. It returned 0 findings now
   263→    3. It's NOT in ran_detectors (i.e., it didn't run — tool missing, skipped, errored)
   264→
   265→    If ran_detectors is provided (from potentials), a 0-finding result from a
   266→    detector that DID run is trusted — the user likely fixed everything.
   267→    """
   268→    if force_resolve:
   269→        return set()
   270→    prev: dict[str, int] = {}
   271→    for f in existing.values():
   272→        if f["status"] == "open":
   273→            det = f.get("detector", "unknown")
   274→            prev[det] = prev.get(det, 0) + 1
   275→
   276→    suspect = set()
   277→    for det, n in prev.items():
   278→        if current_by_detector.get(det, 0) > 0:
   279→            continue  # Detector produced findings — not suspect
   280→        if ran_detectors is not None and det in ran_detectors:
   281→            continue  # Detector ran and found nothing — legitimate
   282→        # Detector had findings before, returned 0 now, and didn't appear in
   283→        # potentials. Only flag if it had a meaningful number of findings.
   284→        if n >= 3:
   285→            suspect.add(det)
   286→    return suspect
   287→
   288→
   289→def _auto_resolve_disappeared(
   290→    existing: dict, current_ids: set[str], suspect_detectors: set[str],
   291→    now: str, *, lang: str | None, scan_path: str | None,
   292→    exclude: tuple[str, ...] = (),
   293→) -> tuple[int, int, int]:
   294→    """Auto-resolve open/wontfix findings absent from scan. Returns (resolved, skip_lang, skip_path)."""
   295→    resolved = skip_lang = skip_path = 0
   296→    for fid, old in existing.items():
   297→        if fid in current_ids or old["status"] not in ("open", "wontfix"):
   298→            continue
   299→        if lang and old.get("lang") and old["lang"] != lang:
   300→            skip_lang += 1; continue
   301→        if scan_path and not old["file"].startswith(scan_path.rstrip("/") + "/") and old["file"] != scan_path:
   302→            skip_path += 1; continue
   303→        if exclude and any(matches_exclusion(old["file"], ex) for ex in exclude):
   304→            continue
   305→        if old.get("detector", "unknown") in suspect_detectors:
   306→            continue
   307→        prev = old["status"]
   308→        old["status"] = "auto_resolved"
   309→        old["resolved_at"] = now
   310→        old["note"] = ("Fixed despite wontfix — disappeared from scan (was wontfix)"
   311→                       if prev == "wontfix" else "Disappeared from scan — likely fixed")
   312→        resolved += 1
   313→    return resolved, skip_lang, skip_path
   314→
   315→
   316→def _upsert_findings(
   317→    existing: dict, current_findings: list[dict], ignore: list[str],
   318→    now: str, *, lang: str | None,
   319→) -> tuple[set[str], int, int, dict[str, int]]:
   320→    """Insert new findings and update existing ones. Returns (ids, new, reopened, by_detector)."""
   321→    current_ids: set[str] = set()
   322→    new_count = reopened = 0
   323→    by_detector: dict[str, int] = {}
   324→    for f in current_findings:
   325→        fid = f["id"]
   326→        if is_ignored(fid, f["file"], ignore):
   327→            continue
   328→        current_ids.add(fid)
   329→        det = f.get("detector", "unknown")
   330→        by_detector[det] = by_detector.get(det, 0) + 1
   331→        if lang:
   332→            f["lang"] = lang
   333→        if fid in existing:
   334→            old = existing[fid]
   335→            old.update(last_seen=now, tier=f["tier"], confidence=f["confidence"],
   336→                       summary=f["summary"], detail=f.get("detail", {}))
   337→            if "zone" in f:
   338→                old["zone"] = f["zone"]
   339→            if lang and not old.get("lang"):
   340→                old["lang"] = lang
   341→            if old["status"] in ("fixed", "auto_resolved"):
   342→                prev = old["status"]
   343→                old["reopen_count"] = old.get("reopen_count", 0) + 1
   344→                old.update(status="open", resolved_at=None,
   345→                           note=f"Reopened (×{old['reopen_count']}) — reappeared in scan (was {prev})")
   346→                reopened += 1
   347→        else:
   348→            existing[fid] = f
   349→            new_count += 1
   350→    return current_ids, new_count, reopened, by_detector
   351→
   352→
   353→def merge_scan(state: dict, current_findings: list[dict], *,
   354→               lang: str | None = None, scan_path: str | None = None,
   355→               force_resolve: bool = False, exclude: tuple[str, ...] = (),
   356→               potentials: dict[str, int] | None = None,
   357→               codebase_metrics: dict | None = None,
   358→               include_slow: bool = True) -> dict:
   359→    """Merge a fresh scan into existing state. Returns diff summary."""
   360→    from .utils import compute_tool_hash
   361→    now = _now()
   362→    state["last_scan"] = now
   363→    state["scan_count"] = state.get("scan_count", 0) + 1
   364→    state["tool_hash"] = compute_tool_hash()
   365→    if potentials is not None and lang:
   366→        state.setdefault("potentials", {})[lang] = potentials
   367→    if codebase_metrics is not None and lang:
   368→        state.setdefault("codebase_metrics", {})[lang] = codebase_metrics
   369→    if lang:
   370→        state.setdefault("scan_completeness", {})[lang] = "full" if include_slow else "fast"
   371→
   372→    existing = state["findings"]
   373→    ignore = state.get("config", {}).get("ignore", [])
   374→    current_ids, new_count, reopened_count, current_by_detector = _upsert_findings(
   375→        existing, current_findings, ignore, now, lang=lang)
   376→    # Detectors that appear in potentials actually ran — trust their 0-finding results
   377→    ran_detectors = set(potentials.keys()) if potentials else None
   378→    suspect_detectors = _find_suspect_detectors(
   379→        existing, current_by_detector, force_resolve, ran_detectors)
   380→    auto_resolved, skipped_lang, skipped_path = _auto_resolve_disappeared(
   381→        existing, current_ids, suspect_detectors, now,
   382→        lang=lang, scan_path=scan_path, exclude=exclude)
   383→    _recompute_stats(state)
   384→
   385→    # Append scan history entry for trajectory tracking
   386→    history = state.setdefault("scan_history", [])
   387→    history.append({
   388→        "timestamp": now,
   389→        "lang": lang,
   390→        "objective_strict": state.get("objective_strict"),
   391→        "objective_score": state.get("objective_score"),
   392→        "open": state["stats"]["open"],
   393→        "diff_new": new_count,
   394→        "diff_resolved": auto_resolved,
   395→        "dimension_scores": {
   396→            name: {"score": ds["score"], "strict": ds.get("strict", ds["score"])}
   397→            for name, ds in state.get("dimension_scores", {}).items()
   398→        } if state.get("dimension_scores") else None,
   399→    })
   400→    if len(history) > 20:
   401→        state["scan_history"] = history[-20:]
   402→
   403→    # Detect chronic reopeners (findings that keep bouncing between resolved and open)
   404→    chronic = [f for f in existing.values()
   405→               if f.get("reopen_count", 0) >= 2 and f["status"] == "open"]
   406→
   407→    return {
   408→        "new": new_count, "auto_resolved": auto_resolved,
   409→        "reopened": reopened_count, "total_current": len(current_ids),
   410→        "suspect_detectors": sorted(suspect_detectors) if suspect_detectors else [],
   411→        "chronic_reopeners": chronic,
   412→        "skipped_other_lang": skipped_lang, "skipped_out_of_scope": skipped_path,
   413→    }
   414→
   415→
   416→def _matches_pattern(fid: str, f: dict, pattern: str) -> bool:
   417→    """Check if a finding matches: exact ID, glob, ID prefix, detector name, or file path."""
   418→    if fid == pattern:
   419→        return True
   420→    if "*" in pattern:
   421→        return fnmatch.fnmatch(fid, pattern)
   422→    if "::" in pattern:
   423→        return fid.startswith(pattern)
   424→    return f.get("detector") == pattern or f["file"] == pattern or f["file"].startswith(pattern.rstrip("/") + "/")
   425→
   426→
   427→def match_findings(state: dict, pattern: str, status_filter: str = "open") -> list[dict]:
   428→    """Return findings matching *pattern* with the given status."""
   429→    return [f for fid, f in state["findings"].items()
   430→            if (status_filter == "all" or f["status"] == status_filter)
   431→            and _matches_pattern(fid, f, pattern)]
   432→
   433→
   434→def resolve_findings(state: dict, pattern: str, status: str,
   435→                     note: str | None = None) -> list[str]:
   436→    """Resolve findings matching pattern. Returns list of resolved IDs."""
   437→    now = _now()
   438→    resolved = []
   439→    for f in match_findings(state, pattern, status_filter="open"):
   440→        f.update(status=status, note=note, resolved_at=now)
   441→        resolved.append(f["id"])
   442→    _recompute_stats(state)
   443→    return resolved
   444→
```

```
   226→    return parser
   227→
   228→
   229→def _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, state: dict):
   230→    """Merge CLI --exclude with persisted config.exclude, set on utils global."""
   231→    from .utils import set_exclusions, c
   232→
   233→    cli_exclusions = getattr(args, "exclude", None) or []
   234→    persisted = state.get("config", {}).get("exclude", [])
   235→    combined = list(cli_exclusions) + [e for e in persisted if e not in cli_exclusions]
   236→    if combined:
   237→        set_exclusions(combined)
   238→        import sys
   239→        if cli_exclusions:
   240→            print(c(f"  Excluding: {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
   241→        else:
   242→            print(c(f"  Excluding (from state): {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
   243→
   244→
   245→def main():
   246→    # Ensure Unicode output works on Windows terminals (cp1252 etc.)
   247→    for stream in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr):
   248→        if hasattr(stream, "reconfigure"):
   249→            try:
   250→                stream.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
   251→            except (AttributeError, OSError):
   252→                pass
   253→
   254→    parser = create_parser()
   255→    args = parser.parse_args()
   256→
   257→    # Resolve default path from language config if not explicitly provided
   258→    if getattr(args, "path", None) is None:
   259→        lang = _resolve_lang(args)
   260→        if lang:
   261→            args.path = str(PROJECT_ROOT / lang.default_src)
   262→        else:
   263→            args.path = str(DEFAULT_PATH)
   264→
   265→    # Load state once and apply exclusions before any command runs
   266→    sp = _state_path(args)
   267→    from .state import load_state
   268→    state = load_state(sp)
   269→    _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, state)
   270→    args._preloaded_state = state
   271→    args._state_path = sp
   272→
   273→    # Lazy-load command handlers from commands/
   274→    from .commands.scan import cmd_scan
   275→    from .commands.status import cmd_status
   276→    from .commands.show import cmd_show
   277→    from .commands.next import cmd_next
   278→    from .commands.resolve import cmd_resolve, cmd_ignore_pattern
   279→    from .commands.fix_cmd import cmd_fix
   280→    from .commands.plan_cmd import cmd_plan_output
   281→    from .commands.detect import cmd_detect
   282→
   283→    commands = {
   284→        "scan": cmd_scan,
   285→        "status": cmd_status,
   286→        "show": cmd_show,
   287→        "next": cmd_next,
   288→        "resolve": cmd_resolve,
   289→        "ignore": cmd_ignore_pattern,
   290→        "fix": cmd_fix,
   291→        "plan": cmd_plan_output,
   292→        "detect": cmd_detect,
   293→    }
   294→
   295→    # Lazy-loaded commands
   296→    if args.command == "tree":
   297→        from .visualize import cmd_tree
   298→        commands["tree"] = cmd_tree
   299→    elif args.command == "viz":
   300→        from .visualize import cmd_viz
   301→        commands["viz"] = cmd_viz
   302→    elif args.command == "move":
   303→        from .commands.move import cmd_move
   304→        commands["move"] = cmd_move
   305→    elif args.command == "zone":
   306→        from .commands.zone_cmd import cmd_zone
   307→        commands["zone"] = cmd_zone
   308→    elif args.command == "review":
   309→        from .commands.review_cmd import cmd_review
   310→        commands["review"] = cmd_review
   311→
   312→    try:
   313→        commands[args.command](args)
   314→    except KeyboardInterrupt:
   315→        print("\nInterrupted.")
   316→        sys.exit(1)
   317→
   318→
   319→if __name__ == "__main__":
   320→    main()
   321→
```

```
1→"""Objective dimension-based scoring system.
     2→
     3→Groups detectors into dimensions (coherent aspects of code quality),
     4→computes per-dimension pass rates from potentials, and produces a
     5→tier-weighted overall health score.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→from dataclasses import dataclass
    11→
    12→from .enums import Confidence, Tier
    13→from .zones import EXCLUDED_ZONE_VALUES
    14→
    15→
    16→@dataclass
    17→class Dimension:
    18→    name: str
    19→    tier: int
    20→    detectors: list[str]
    21→
    22→
    23→DIMENSIONS = [
    24→    Dimension("Import hygiene",      1, ["unused"]),
    25→    Dimension("Debug cleanliness",   1, ["logs"]),
    26→    Dimension("API surface",         2, ["exports", "deprecated"]),
    27→    Dimension("File health",         3, ["structural"]),
    28→    Dimension("Component design",    3, ["props"]),
    29→    Dimension("Coupling",            3, ["single_use", "coupling"]),
    30→    Dimension("Organization",        3, ["orphaned", "flat_dirs", "naming", "facade", "stale_exclude"]),
    31→    Dimension("Code quality",        3, ["smells", "react", "dict_keys"]),
    32→    Dimension("Duplication",         3, ["dupes"]),
    33→    Dimension("Pattern consistency", 3, ["patterns"]),
    34→    Dimension("Dependency health",   4, ["cycles"]),
    35→    Dimension("Test health",         4, ["test_coverage"]),
    36→    Dimension("Security",            4, ["security"]),
    37→    Dimension("Design quality",      4, ["review"]),
    38→]
    39→
    40→TIER_WEIGHTS = {Tier.AUTO_FIX: 1, Tier.QUICK_FIX: 2, Tier.JUDGMENT: 3, Tier.MAJOR_REFACTOR: 4}
    41→CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS = {Confidence.HIGH: 1.0, Confidence.MEDIUM: 0.7, Confidence.LOW: 0.3}
    42→
    43→# Minimum checks for full dimension weight — below this, weight is dampened
    44→# proportionally. Prevents small-sample dimensions from swinging the overall score.
    45→MIN_SAMPLE = 200
    46→
    47→# Detectors where potential = file count but findings are per-(file, sub-type).
    48→# Per-file weighted failures are capped at 1.0 to match the file-based denominator.
    49→_FILE_BASED_DETECTORS = {"smells", "dict_keys", "test_coverage", "security", "review"}
    50→
    51→# Zones excluded from scoring (imported from zones.py canonical source)
    52→_EXCLUDED_ZONES = EXCLUDED_ZONE_VALUES
    53→
    54→# Security findings are only excluded in generated/vendor zones (secrets in tests are real risks)
    55→_SECURITY_EXCLUDED_ZONES = {"generated", "vendor"}
    56→
    57→# Statuses that count as failures
    58→_LENIENT_FAILURES = {"open"}
    59→_STRICT_FAILURES = {"open", "wontfix"}
    60→
    61→
    62→def merge_potentials(potentials_by_lang: dict) -> dict[str, int]:
    63→    """Sum potentials across languages per detector."""
    64→    merged: dict[str, int] = {}
    65→    for lang_potentials in potentials_by_lang.values():
    66→        for detector, count in lang_potentials.items():
    67→            merged[detector] = merged.get(detector, 0) + count
    68→    return merged
    69→
    70→
    71→def _detector_pass_rate(
    72→    detector: str,
    73→    findings: dict,
    74→    potential: int,
    75→    *,
    76→    strict: bool = False,
    77→) -> tuple[float, int, float]:
    78→    """Pass rate for one detector.
    79→
    80→    Returns (pass_rate, issue_count, weighted_failures).
    81→    Zero potential -> (1.0, 0, 0.0).
    82→
    83→    For file-based detectors (potential = file count, multiple findings per file),
    84→    per-file weighted failures are capped at 1.0 to prevent unit mismatch.
    85→    """
    86→    if potential <= 0:
    87→        return 1.0, 0, 0.0
    88→
    89→    failure_set = _STRICT_FAILURES if strict else _LENIENT_FAILURES
    90→    issue_count = 0
    91→    excluded_zones = _SECURITY_EXCLUDED_ZONES if detector == "security" else _EXCLUDED_ZONES
    92→
    93→    if detector in _FILE_BASED_DETECTORS:
    94→        # Group by file, cap per-file weight at 1.0
    95→        by_file: dict[str, float] = {}
    96→        for f in findings.values():
    97→            if f.get("detector") != detector:
    98→                continue
    99→            if f.get("zone", "production") in excluded_zones:
   100→                continue
   101→            if f["status"] in failure_set:
   102→                weight = CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS.get(f.get("confidence", "medium"), 0.7)
   103→                file_key = f.get("file", "")
   104→                by_file[file_key] = by_file.get(file_key, 0) + weight
   105→                issue_count += 1
   106→        weighted_failures = sum(min(1.0, w) for w in by_file.values())
   107→    else:
   108→        weighted_failures = 0.0
   109→        for f in findings.values():
   110→            if f.get("detector") != detector:
   111→                continue
   112→            if f.get("zone", "production") in excluded_zones:
   113→                continue
   114→            if f["status"] in failure_set:
   115→                weight = CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS.get(f.get("confidence", "medium"), 0.7)
   116→                weighted_failures += weight
   117→                issue_count += 1
   118→
   119→    pass_rate = max(0.0, (potential - weighted_failures) / potential)
   120→    return pass_rate, issue_count, weighted_failures
   121→
   122→
   123→def compute_dimension_scores(
   124→    findings: dict,
   125→    potentials: dict[str, int],
   126→    *,
   127→    strict: bool = False,
   128→) -> dict[str, dict]:
   129→    """Compute per-dimension scores from findings and potentials.
   130→
   131→    Returns {dimension_name: {"score": float, "checks": int, "issues": int, "detectors": dict}}.
   132→    Dimensions with no active detectors (all potentials = 0 or missing) are excluded.
   133→    """
   134→    results: dict[str, dict] = {}
   135→
   136→    for dim in DIMENSIONS:
   137→        total_checks = 0
   138→        total_issues = 0
   139→        total_weighted_failures = 0.0
   140→        detector_detail = {}
   141→
   142→        for det in dim.detectors:
   143→            pot = potentials.get(det, 0)
   144→            if pot <= 0:
   145→                continue
   146→            rate, issues, weighted = _detector_pass_rate(
   147→                det, findings, pot, strict=strict)
   148→            total_checks += pot
   149→            total_issues += issues
   150→            total_weighted_failures += weighted
   151→            detector_detail[det] = {
   152→                "potential": pot, "pass_rate": rate,
   153→                "issues": issues, "weighted_failures": weighted,
   154→            }
   155→
   156→        if total_checks <= 0:
   157→            continue
   158→
   159→        # Potential-weighted pass rate: treats the dimension as one big pool
   160→        dim_score = max(0.0, (total_checks - total_weighted_failures) / total_checks) * 100
   161→
   162→        results[dim.name] = {
   163→            "score": round(dim_score, 1),
   164→            "tier": dim.tier,
   165→            "checks": total_checks,
   166→            "issues": total_issues,
   167→            "detectors": detector_detail,
   168→        }
   169→
   170→    return results
   171→
   172→
   173→def compute_objective_score(dimension_scores: dict) -> float:
   174→    """Tier-weighted average of dimension scores, dampened by sample size.
   175→
   176→    Dimensions with fewer than MIN_SAMPLE checks get proportionally reduced
   177→    weight, preventing small-sample dimensions from swinging the overall score.
   178→    """
   179→    if not dimension_scores:
   180→        return 100.0
   181→
   182→    weighted_sum = 0.0
   183→    weight_total = 0.0
   184→    for name, data in dimension_scores.items():
   185→        tier = data["tier"]
   186→        w = TIER_WEIGHTS.get(tier, 2)
   187→        # Dampen weight for small-sample dimensions
   188→        sample_factor = min(1.0, data.get("checks", 0) / MIN_SAMPLE)
   189→        effective_weight = w * sample_factor
   190→        weighted_sum += data["score"] * effective_weight
   191→        weight_total += effective_weight
   192→
   193→    if weight_total == 0:
   194→        return 100.0
   195→    return round(weighted_sum / weight_total, 1)
   196→
   197→
   198→def compute_score_impact(
   199→    dimension_scores: dict,
   200→    potentials: dict[str, int],
   201→    detector: str,
   202→    issues_to_fix: int,
   203→) -> float:
   204→    """Estimate score improvement from fixing N issues in a detector.
   205→
   206→    Returns estimated point increase in the objective score.
   207→    """
   208→    # Find which dimension this detector belongs to
   209→    target_dim = None
   210→    for dim in DIMENSIONS:
   211→        if detector in dim.detectors:
   212→            target_dim = dim
   213→            break
   214→    if target_dim is None or target_dim.name not in dimension_scores:
   215→        return 0.0
   216→
   217→    pot = potentials.get(detector, 0)
   218→    if pot <= 0:
   219→        return 0.0
   220→
   221→    dim_data = dimension_scores[target_dim.name]
   222→    old_score = compute_objective_score(dimension_scores)
   223→
   224→    # Simulate fixing: reduce weighted failures by issues_to_fix * avg_weight
   225→    det_data = dim_data["detectors"].get(detector)
   226→    if not det_data:
   227→        return 0.0
   228→
   229→    old_weighted = det_data["weighted_failures"]
   230→    # Assume fixes are high-confidence (weight=1.0) — conservative estimate
   231→    new_weighted = max(0.0, old_weighted - issues_to_fix * 1.0)
   232→
   233→    # Recompute dimension score with potential-weighted averaging
   234→    total_pot = 0
   235→    total_new_wf = 0.0
   236→    for det in target_dim.detectors:
   237→        d = dim_data["detectors"].get(det)
   238→        if not d:
   239→            continue
   240→        total_pot += d["potential"]
   241→        if det == detector:
   242→            total_new_wf += new_weighted
   243→        else:
   244→            total_new_wf += d["weighted_failures"]
   245→    if total_pot <= 0:
   246→        return 0.0
   247→    new_dim_score = max(0.0, (total_pot - total_new_wf) / total_pot) * 100
   248→
   249→    # Recompute overall with the new dimension score
   250→    simulated = {k: dict(v) for k, v in dimension_scores.items()}
   251→    simulated[target_dim.name] = {**dim_data, "score": round(new_dim_score, 1)}
   252→    new_score = compute_objective_score(simulated)
   253→
   254→    return round(new_score - old_score, 1)
   255→
   256→
   257→def get_dimension_for_detector(detector: str) -> Dimension | None:
   258→    """Look up which dimension a detector belongs to."""
   259→    for dim in DIMENSIONS:
   260→        if detector in dim.detectors:
   261→            return dim
   262→    return None
   263→
```

```
1→"""Finding generation (detector → findings), tier assignment, plan output.
     2→
     3→Runs all detectors, converts raw results into normalized findings with stable IDs,
     4→assigns tiers, and generates prioritized plans.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import sys
    10→from collections import defaultdict
    11→from datetime import date
    12→from pathlib import Path
    13→
    14→from .utils import c
    15→
    16→TIER_LABELS = {
    17→    1: "Auto-fixable (imports, logs, dead deprecated)",
    18→    2: "Quick fixes (unused vars, dead exports, exact dupes, orphaned files, cross-tool imports)",
    19→    3: "Needs judgment (smells, near-dupes, single-use, small cycles, state sync)",
    20→    4: "Major refactors (structural decomposition, large import cycles)",
    21→}
    22→
    23→CONFIDENCE_ORDER = {"high": 0, "medium": 1, "low": 2}
    24→
    25→
    26→def generate_findings(
    27→    path: Path, *, include_slow: bool = True, lang=None,
    28→    zone_overrides: dict[str, str] | None = None,
    29→) -> tuple[list[dict], dict[str, int]]:
    30→    """Run all detectors and convert results to normalized findings.
    31→
    32→    Dispatches through the LangConfig phase pipeline.
    33→    Auto-detects language when none is specified.
    34→    Returns (findings, potentials) where potentials maps detector names to checked counts.
    35→    """
    36→    if lang is None:
    37→        from .lang import get_lang, auto_detect_lang
    38→        from .utils import PROJECT_ROOT
    39→        detected = auto_detect_lang(PROJECT_ROOT)
    40→        lang = get_lang(detected or "typescript")
    41→    return _generate_findings_from_lang(path, lang, include_slow=include_slow,
    42→                                         zone_overrides=zone_overrides)
    43→
    44→
    45→def _generate_findings_from_lang(
    46→    path: Path, lang, *, include_slow: bool = True,
    47→    zone_overrides: dict[str, str] | None = None,
    48→) -> tuple[list[dict], dict[str, int]]:
    49→    """Run detector phases from a LangConfig."""
    50→    stderr = lambda msg: print(c(msg, "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
    51→
    52→    # Build zone map if language has zone rules
    53→    if lang.zone_rules and lang.file_finder:
    54→        from .zones import FileZoneMap, ZONE_POLICIES
    55→        from .utils import rel
    56→        files = lang.file_finder(path)
    57→        lang._zone_map = FileZoneMap(files, lang.zone_rules, rel_fn=rel,
    58→                                      overrides=zone_overrides)
    59→        counts = lang._zone_map.counts()
    60→        zone_str = ", ".join(f"{z}: {n}" for z, n in sorted(counts.items()) if n > 0)
    61→        stderr(f"  Zones: {zone_str}")
    62→
    63→    phases = lang.phases
    64→    if not include_slow:
    65→        phases = [p for p in phases if not p.slow]
    66→
    67→    findings: list[dict] = []
    68→    all_potentials: dict[str, int] = {}
    69→    total = len(phases)
    70→    for i, phase in enumerate(phases):
    71→        stderr(f"  [{i+1}/{total}] {phase.label}...")
    72→        phase_findings, phase_potentials = phase.run(path, lang)
    73→        all_potentials.update(phase_potentials)
    74→        findings += phase_findings
    75→
    76→    # Stamp language and zone on all findings
    77→    for f in findings:
    78→        f["lang"] = lang.name
    79→        if lang._zone_map is not None:
    80→            zone = lang._zone_map.get(f.get("file", ""))
    81→            f["zone"] = zone.value
    82→            # Apply zone policy confidence downgrades
    83→            policy = ZONE_POLICIES.get(zone)
    84→            if policy and f.get("detector") in policy.downgrade_detectors:
    85→                f["confidence"] = "low"
    86→
    87→    stderr(f"\n  Total: {len(findings)} findings")
    88→    return findings, all_potentials
    89→
    90→
    91→def _plan_header(state: dict, stats: dict) -> list[str]:
    92→    """Build the plan header: title, score line, and codebase metrics."""
    93→    score = state.get("score", 0)
    94→
    95→    # Use objective score if available, fall back to progress score
    96→    obj_score = state.get("objective_score")
    97→    obj_strict = state.get("objective_strict")
    98→    if obj_score is not None:
    99→        header_score = f"**Health: {obj_score:.1f}/100** (strict: {obj_strict:.1f})"
   100→    else:
   101→        header_score = f"**Score: {score}/100**"
   102→
   103→    # Codebase metrics
   104→    metrics = state.get("codebase_metrics", {})
   105→    total_files = sum(m.get("total_files", 0) for m in metrics.values())
   106→    total_loc = sum(m.get("total_loc", 0) for m in metrics.values())
   107→    total_dirs = sum(m.get("total_directories", 0) for m in metrics.values())
   108→
   109→    lines = [
   110→        f"# Desloppify Plan — {date.today().isoformat()}",
   111→        "",
   112→        f"{header_score} | "
   113→        f"{stats.get('open', 0)} open | "
   114→        f"{stats.get('fixed', 0)} fixed | "
   115→        f"{stats.get('wontfix', 0)} wontfix | "
   116→        f"{stats.get('auto_resolved', 0)} auto-resolved",
   117→        "",
   118→    ]
   119→    if total_files:
   120→        from .utils import LOC_COMPACT_THRESHOLD
   121→        loc_str = f"{total_loc:,}" if total_loc < LOC_COMPACT_THRESHOLD else f"{total_loc // 1000}K"
   122→        lines.append(f"\n{total_files} files · {loc_str} LOC · {total_dirs} directories\n")
   123→
   124→    return lines
   125→
   126→
   127→def _plan_dimension_table(state: dict) -> list[str]:
   128→    """Build the dimension health table rows (empty list when no data)."""
   129→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
   130→    if not dim_scores:
   131→        return []
   132→
   133→    lines = [
   134→        "## Health by Dimension",
   135→        "",
   136→        "| Dimension | Tier | Checks | Issues | Health | Strict |",
   137→        "|-----------|------|--------|--------|--------|--------|",
   138→    ]
   139→    from .scoring import DIMENSIONS
   140→    for dim in DIMENSIONS:
   141→        ds = dim_scores.get(dim.name)
   142→        if not ds:
   143→            continue
   144→        checks = ds.get("checks", 0)
   145→        issues = ds.get("issues", 0)
   146→        score_val = ds.get("score", 100)
   147→        strict_val = ds.get("strict", score_val)
   148→        bold = "**" if score_val < 93 else ""
   149→        lines.append(
   150→            f"| {bold}{dim.name}{bold} | T{dim.tier} | "
   151→            f"{checks:,} | {issues} | {score_val:.1f}% | {strict_val:.1f}% |"
   152→        )
   153→    lines.append("")
   154→    return lines
   155→
   156→
   157→def _plan_tier_sections(findings: dict) -> list[str]:
   158→    """Build per-tier sections listing open findings grouped by file."""
   159→    open_findings = [f for f in findings.values() if f["status"] == "open"]
   160→    by_tier_file: dict[int, dict[str, list]] = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(list))
   161→    for f in open_findings:
   162→        by_tier_file[f["tier"]][f["file"]].append(f)
   163→
   164→    lines: list[str] = []
   165→    for tier_num in [1, 2, 3, 4]:
   166→        tier_files = by_tier_file.get(tier_num, {})
   167→        if not tier_files:
   168→            continue
   169→        label = TIER_LABELS.get(tier_num, f"Tier {tier_num}")
   170→        tier_count = sum(len(fs) for fs in tier_files.values())
   171→        lines.extend([
   172→            "---",
   173→            f"## Tier {tier_num}: {label} ({tier_count} open)",
   174→            "",
   175→        ])
   176→
   177→        # Sort files by finding count (most findings first)
   178→        sorted_files = sorted(tier_files.items(), key=lambda x: -len(x[1]))
   179→        for filepath, file_findings in sorted_files:
   180→            # Sort findings within file: high confidence first
   181→            file_findings.sort(key=lambda f: (CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(f["confidence"], 9), f["id"]))
   182→            lines.append(f"### `{filepath}` ({len(file_findings)} findings)")
   183→            lines.append("")
   184→            for f in file_findings:
   185→                conf_badge = f"[{f['confidence']}]"
   186→                lines.append(f"- [ ] {conf_badge} {f['summary']}")
   187→                lines.append(f"      `{f['id']}`")
   188→            lines.append("")
   189→
   190→    return lines
   191→
   192→
   193→def generate_plan_md(state: dict) -> str:
   194→    """Generate a prioritized markdown plan from state."""
   195→    findings = state["findings"]
   196→    stats = state.get("stats", {})
   197→
   198→    lines = _plan_header(state, stats)
   199→    lines.extend(_plan_dimension_table(state))
   200→
   201→    # Tier breakdown summary
   202→    by_tier = stats.get("by_tier", {})
   203→    for tier_num in [1, 2, 3, 4]:
   204→        ts = by_tier.get(str(tier_num), {})
   205→        t_open = ts.get("open", 0)
   206→        t_total = sum(ts.values())
   207→        t_addressed = t_total - t_open
   208→        pct = round(t_addressed / t_total * 100) if t_total else 100
   209→        label = TIER_LABELS.get(tier_num, f"Tier {tier_num}")
   210→        lines.append(f"- **Tier {tier_num}** ({label}): {t_open} open / {t_total} total ({pct}% addressed)")
   211→    lines.append("")
   212→
   213→    # Per-tier open finding sections
   214→    lines.extend(_plan_tier_sections(findings))
   215→
   216→    # Addressed findings summary
   217→    addressed = [f for f in findings.values() if f["status"] != "open"]
   218→    if addressed:
   219→        by_status: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
   220→        for f in addressed:
   221→            by_status[f["status"]] += 1
   222→        lines.extend([
   223→            "---",
   224→            "## Addressed",
   225→            "",
   226→        ])
   227→        for status, count in sorted(by_status.items()):
   228→            lines.append(f"- **{status}**: {count}")
   229→
   230→        # Show wontfix items with their reasons
   231→        wontfix = [f for f in addressed if f["status"] == "wontfix" and f.get("note")]
   232→        if wontfix:
   233→            lines.extend(["", "### Wontfix (with explanations)", ""])
   234→            for f in wontfix[:30]:
   235→                lines.append(f"- `{f['id']}` — {f['note']}")
   236→        lines.append("")
   237→
   238→    return "\n".join(lines)
   239→
   240→
   241→def get_next_item(state: dict, tier: int | None = None) -> dict | None:
   242→    """Get the highest-priority open finding."""
   243→    items = get_next_items(state, tier, 1)
   244→    return items[0] if items else None
   245→
   246→
   247→def get_next_items(state: dict, tier: int | None = None, count: int = 1) -> list[dict]:
   248→    """Get the N highest-priority open findings.
   249→
   250→    Priority: tier (ascending) → confidence (high first) → detail count.
   251→    """
   252→    open_findings = [f for f in state["findings"].values() if f["status"] == "open"]
   253→    if tier is not None:
   254→        open_findings = [f for f in open_findings if f["tier"] == tier]
   255→    if not open_findings:
   256→        return []
   257→
   258→    open_findings.sort(key=lambda f: (
   259→        f["tier"],
   260→        CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(f["confidence"], 9),
   261→        -f.get("detail", {}).get("count", 0),
   262→        f["id"],
   263→    ))
   264→    return open_findings[:count]
   265→
```

```
   105→def make_unused_findings(entries: list[dict], stderr_fn) -> list[Finding]:
   106→    """Transform raw unused-detector entries into normalized findings.
   107→
   108→    Shared by both Python and TypeScript unused phases.
   109→    """
   110→    results = []
   111→    for e in entries:
   112→        tier = 1 if e["category"] == "imports" else 2
   113→        results.append(make_finding(
   114→            "unused", e["file"], e["name"],
   115→            tier=tier, confidence="high",
   116→            summary=f"Unused {e['category']}: {e['name']}",
   117→            detail={"line": e["line"], "category": e["category"]},
   118→        ))
   119→    stderr_fn(f"         {len(entries)} instances -> {len(results)} findings")
   120→    return results
   121→
   122→
   123→def make_dupe_findings(entries: list[dict], stderr_fn) -> list[Finding]:
   124→    """Transform clustered duplicate entries into normalized findings.
   125→
   126→    Each entry represents a cluster of similar functions. One finding per cluster.
   127→    """
   128→    results = []
   129→    for e in entries:
   130→        a, b = e["fn_a"], e["fn_b"]
   131→        if a["loc"] < 10 and b["loc"] < 10:
   132→            continue
   133→        cluster_size = e.get("cluster_size", 2)
   134→        pair = sorted([(a["file"], a["name"]), (b["file"], b["name"])])
   135→        name = f"{pair[0][1]}::{rel(pair[1][0])}::{pair[1][1]}"
   136→        tier = 2 if e["kind"] == "exact" else 3
   137→        conf = "high" if e["kind"] == "exact" else "low"
   138→        kind_label = "Exact" if e["kind"] == "exact" else "Near"
   139→        if cluster_size > 2:
   140→            summary = (f"{kind_label} dupe cluster ({cluster_size} functions, "
   141→                       f"{e['similarity']:.0%} similar): "
   142→                       f"{a['name']} ({rel(a['file'])}:{a['line']}), "
   143→                       f"{b['name']} ({rel(b['file'])}:{b['line']}), ...")
   144→        else:
   145→            summary = (f"{kind_label} dupe: "
   146→                       f"{a['name']} ({rel(a['file'])}:{a['line']}) <-> "
   147→                       f"{b['name']} ({rel(b['file'])}:{b['line']}) [{e['similarity']:.0%}]")
   148→        results.append(make_finding(
   149→            "dupes", pair[0][0], name,
   150→            tier=tier, confidence=conf,
   151→            summary=summary,
   152→            detail={"fn_a": a, "fn_b": b,
   153→                    "similarity": e["similarity"], "kind": e["kind"],
   154→                    "cluster_size": cluster_size,
   155→                    "cluster": e.get("cluster", [a, b])},
   156→        ))
   157→    suppressed = sum(1 for e in entries
   158→                     if e["fn_a"]["loc"] < 10 and e["fn_b"]["loc"] < 10)
   159→    stderr_fn(f"         {len(entries)} clusters, {suppressed} suppressed (<10 LOC)")
   160→    return results
   161→
   162→
   163→def add_structural_signal(structural: dict, file: str, signal: str, detail: dict):
   164→    """Add a complexity signal to the per-file structural dict.
   165→
   166→    Accumulates signals per file so they can be merged into tiered findings.
   167→    """
   168→    f = resolve_path(file)
   169→    structural.setdefault(f, {"signals": [], "detail": {}})
   170→    structural[f]["signals"].append(signal)
   171→    structural[f]["detail"].update(detail)
   172→
   173→
   174→def merge_structural_signals(structural: dict, stderr_fn,
   175→                              *, complexity_only_min: int = 35) -> list[Finding]:
   176→    """Convert per-file structural signals into tiered findings.
   177→
   178→    3+ signals -> T4/high (needs decomposition).
   179→    1-2 signals -> T3/medium.
   180→    Complexity-only files (no large/god signals) need score >= complexity_only_min
   181→    to be flagged — lower complexity in small files is normal, not decomposition-worthy.
   182→    """
   183→    results = []
   184→    suppressed = 0
   185→    for filepath, data in structural.items():
   186→        if "loc" not in data["detail"]:
   187→            try:
   188→                p = Path(filepath) if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else PROJECT_ROOT / filepath
   189→                data["detail"]["loc"] = len(p.read_text().splitlines())
   190→            except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError):
   191→                data["detail"]["loc"] = 0
   192→
   193→        # Suppress complexity-only findings below the elevated threshold
   194→        signals = data["signals"]
   195→        is_complexity_only = all(s.startswith("complexity") for s in signals)
   196→        if is_complexity_only:
   197→            score = data["detail"].get("complexity_score", 0)
   198→            if score < complexity_only_min:
   199→                suppressed += 1
   200→                continue
   201→
   202→        signal_count = len(signals)
   203→        tier = 4 if signal_count >= 3 else 3
   204→        confidence = "high" if signal_count >= 3 else "medium"
   205→        summary = "Needs decomposition: " + " / ".join(signals)
   206→        results.append(make_finding(
   207→            "structural", filepath, "",
   208→            tier=tier, confidence=confidence,
   209→            summary=summary,
   210→            detail=data["detail"],
   211→        ))
   212→    if suppressed:
   213→        stderr_fn(f"         {suppressed} complexity-only files below threshold (< {complexity_only_min})")
   214→    stderr_fn(f"         -> {len(results)} structural findings")
   215→    return results
   216→
   217→
   218→def make_single_use_findings(
   219→    entries: list[dict],
   220→    get_area,
   221→    *,
   222→    loc_range: tuple[int, int] = (50, 200),
   223→    suppress_colocated: bool = True,
   224→    skip_dir_names: set[str] | None = None,
   225→    stderr_fn,
   226→) -> list[Finding]:
   227→    """Filter and normalize single-use entries into findings.
   228→
   229→    Suppresses entries within the LOC range (they're appropriately-sized abstractions),
   230→    entries co-located with their sole importer, and entries in skip_dir_names
   231→    directories (e.g., commands/ — CLI modules are single-use by design).
   232→    """
   233→    results = []
   234→    colocated_suppressed = 0
   235→    lo, hi = loc_range
   236→    for e in entries:
   237→        if lo <= e["loc"] <= hi:
   238→            continue
   239→        # Skip files in directories that are single-use by design (e.g., commands/)
   240→        if skip_dir_names:
   241→            parts = Path(e["file"]).parts
   242→            if any(p in skip_dir_names for p in parts):
   243→                continue
   244→        if suppress_colocated and get_area:
   245→            src_area = get_area(rel(e["file"]))
   246→            imp_area = get_area(e["sole_importer"])
   247→            if src_area == imp_area:
   248→                colocated_suppressed += 1
   249→                continue
   250→        results.append(make_finding(
   251→            "single_use", e["file"], "",
   252→            tier=3, confidence="medium",
   253→            summary=f"Single-use ({e['loc']} LOC): only imported by {e['sole_importer']}",
   254→            detail={"loc": e["loc"], "sole_importer": e["sole_importer"]},
   255→        ))
   256→    suppressed = len(entries) - len(results)
   257→    coloc_note = f", {colocated_suppressed} co-located" if colocated_suppressed else ""
   258→    stderr_fn(f"         single-use: {len(entries)} found, {suppressed} suppressed "
   259→              f"({lo}-{hi} LOC{coloc_note})")
   260→    return results
   261→
   262→
   263→def make_cycle_findings(entries: list[dict], stderr_fn) -> list[Finding]:
   264→    """Normalize import cycles into findings."""
   265→    results = []
   266→    for cy in entries:
   267→        cycle_files = [rel(f) for f in cy["files"]]
   268→        name = "::".join(cycle_files[:4])
   269→        if len(cycle_files) > 4:
   270→            name += f"::+{len(cycle_files) - 4}"
   271→        tier = 3 if cy["length"] <= 3 else 4
   272→        results.append(make_finding(
   273→            "cycles", cy["files"][0], name,
   274→            tier=tier, confidence="high",
   275→            summary=f"Import cycle ({cy['length']} files): "
   276→                    + " -> ".join(cycle_files[:5])
   277→                    + (f" -> +{len(cycle_files) - 5}" if len(cycle_files) > 5 else ""),
   278→            detail={"files": cycle_files, "length": cy["length"]},
   279→        ))
   280→    if entries:
   281→        stderr_fn(f"         cycles: {len(entries)} import cycles")
   282→    return results
   283→
   284→
   285→def make_orphaned_findings(entries: list[dict], stderr_fn) -> list[Finding]:
   286→    """Normalize orphaned file entries into findings."""
   287→    results = []
   288→    for e in entries:
   289→        results.append(make_finding(
   290→            "orphaned", e["file"], "",
   291→            tier=3, confidence="medium",
   292→            summary=f"Orphaned file ({e['loc']} LOC): zero importers, not an entry point",
   293→            detail={"loc": e["loc"]},
   294→        ))
   295→    if entries:
   296→        stderr_fn(f"         orphaned: {len(entries)} files with zero importers")
   297→    return results
   298→
   299→
   300→SMELL_TIER_MAP = {"high": Tier.QUICK_FIX, "medium": Tier.JUDGMENT, "low": Tier.JUDGMENT}
   301→
   302→
   303→def make_smell_findings(entries: list[dict], stderr_fn) -> list[Finding]:
   304→    """Group smell entries by file and assign tiers from severity.
   305→
   306→    Input: list of smell dicts from detect_smells, each with id/label/severity/matches.
   307→    Output: findings grouped per (file, smell_id).
   308→    """
   309→    from collections import defaultdict
   310→    results = []
   311→    for e in entries:
   312→        by_file: dict[str, list] = defaultdict(list)
   313→        for m in e["matches"]:
   314→            by_file[m["file"]].append(m)
   315→        for file, matches in by_file.items():
   316→            conf = "medium" if e["severity"] != "low" else "low"
   317→            tier = SMELL_TIER_MAP.get(e["severity"], 3)
   318→            results.append(make_finding(
   319→                "smells", file, e["id"],
   320→                tier=tier, confidence=conf,
   321→                summary=f"{len(matches)}x {e['label']}",
   322→                detail={"smell_id": e["id"], "severity": e["severity"],
   323→                        "count": len(matches),
   324→                        "lines": [m["line"] for m in matches[:10]]},
   325→            ))
   326→    stderr_fn(f"         -> {len(results)} smell findings")
   327→    return results
   328→
   329→
   330→def phase_dupes(path: Path, lang: LangConfig) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
   331→    """Shared phase runner: detect duplicate functions via lang.extract_functions.
   332→
   333→    When a zone map is available, filters out functions from zone-excluded files
   334→    before the O(n^2) comparison to avoid test/config/generated false positives.
   335→    """
   336→    from ..detectors.dupes import detect_duplicates
   337→    from ..utils import log
   338→    functions = lang.extract_functions(path)
   339→
   340→    # Filter out functions from zone-excluded files
   341→    if lang._zone_map is not None:
   342→        from ..zones import EXCLUDED_ZONES
   343→        before = len(functions)
   344→        functions = [f for f in functions
   345→                     if lang._zone_map.get(getattr(f, "file", "")) not in EXCLUDED_ZONES]
   346→        excluded = before - len(functions)
   347→        if excluded:
   348→            log(f"         zones: {excluded} functions excluded (non-production)")
   349→
   350→    entries, total_functions = detect_duplicates(functions)
   351→    findings = make_dupe_findings(entries, log)
   352→    return findings, {"dupes": total_functions}
   353→
   354→
   355→def make_passthrough_findings(
   356→    entries: list[dict],
   357→    name_key: str,
   358→    total_key: str,
   359→    stderr_fn,
   360→) -> list[Finding]:
   361→    """Normalize passthrough detection results into findings."""
   362→    results = []
   363→    for e in entries:
   364→        label = e[name_key]
   365→        results.append(make_finding(
   366→            "props", e["file"], f"passthrough::{label}",
   367→            tier=e["tier"], confidence=e["confidence"],
   368→            summary=f"Passthrough: {label} "
   369→                    f"({e['passthrough']}/{e[total_key]} forwarded, {e['ratio']:.0%})",
   370→            detail={k: v for k, v in e.items() if k != "file"},
   371→        ))
   372→    if entries:
   373→        stderr_fn(f"         passthrough: {len(entries)} findings")
   374→    return results
   375→
   376→
   377→def make_facade_findings(entries: list[dict], stderr_fn) -> list[Finding]:
   378→    """Normalize re-export facade entries into findings."""
   379→    results = []
   380→    for e in entries:
   381→        kind = e["kind"]
   382→        if kind == "directory":
   383→            summary = (f"Facade directory ({e['loc']} LOC, {e.get('file_count', '?')} files): "
   384→                       f"all modules are re-exports ({e['importers']} importers)")
   385→        else:
   386→            from_str = ", ".join(e["imports_from"][:3])
   387→            if len(e["imports_from"]) > 3:
   388→                from_str += f", +{len(e['imports_from']) - 3}"
   389→            summary = (f"Re-export facade ({e['loc']} LOC): "
   390→                       f"imports from {from_str} ({e['importers']} importers)")
   391→        results.append(make_finding(
   392→            "facade", e["file"], "",
   393→            tier=2, confidence="high" if e["importers"] == 0 else "medium",
   394→            summary=summary,
   395→            detail={"loc": e["loc"], "importers": e["importers"],
   396→                    "imports_from": e["imports_from"], "kind": kind},
   397→        ))
   398→    if entries:
   399→        stderr_fn(f"         facades: {len(entries)} re-export facade findings")
   400→    return results
   401→
   402→
   403→# ── Shared phase runners ──────────────────────────────────────
   404→
   405→
   406→def find_external_test_files(path: Path, lang_name: str) -> set[str]:
   407→    """Find test files in standard locations outside the scanned path."""
   408→    import os
   409→    extra = set()
   410→    test_dirs = ["tests", "test"]
   411→    if lang_name != "python":
   412→        test_dirs.append("__tests__")
   413→    for test_dir in test_dirs:
   414→        d = PROJECT_ROOT / test_dir
   415→        if not d.is_dir():
   416→            continue
   417→        try:
   418→            d.resolve().relative_to(path.resolve())
   419→            continue  # test_dir is inside scanned path, zone_map already has it
   420→        except ValueError:
   421→            pass
   422→        ext = ".py" if lang_name == "python" else (".ts", ".tsx")
   423→        for root, _, files in os.walk(d):
   424→            for f in files:
   425→                if isinstance(ext, tuple):
   426→                    if any(f.endswith(e) for e in ext):
   427→                        extra.add(os.path.join(root, f))
   428→                elif f.endswith(ext):
   429→                    extra.add(os.path.join(root, f))
   430→    return extra
   431→
   432→
   433→def phase_security(path: Path, lang: LangConfig) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
   434→    """Shared phase: detect security issues (cross-language + lang-specific)."""
   435→    from ..detectors.security import detect_security_issues
   436→    from ..zones import filter_entries
   437→
   438→    zm = lang._zone_map
   439→    files = lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else []
   440→    entries, potential = detect_security_issues(files, zm, lang.name)
   441→
   442→    # Also call lang-specific security detectors if available
   443→    if hasattr(lang, 'detect_lang_security'):
   444→        lang_entries, _ = lang.detect_lang_security(files, zm)
   445→        entries.extend(lang_entries)
   446→
   447→    entries = filter_entries(zm, entries, "security")
   448→
   449→    results = []
   450→    for e in entries:
   451→        finding = make_finding(
   452→            "security", e["file"], e["name"],
   453→            tier=e["tier"], confidence=e["confidence"],
   454→            summary=e["summary"], detail=e["detail"],
   455→        )
   456→        # Stamp zone so scoring zone override works
   457→        if zm is not None:
   458→            finding["zone"] = zm.get(e["file"]).value
   459→        results.append(finding)
   460→
   461→    if results:
   462→        log(f"         security: {len(results)} findings ({potential} files scanned)")
   463→    else:
   464→        log(f"         security: clean ({potential} files scanned)")
   465→
   466→    return results, {"security": potential}
   467→
   468→
   469→def phase_test_coverage(path: Path, lang: LangConfig) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
   470→    """Shared phase: detect test coverage gaps."""
   471→    from ..detectors.test_coverage import detect_test_coverage
   472→    from ..zones import filter_entries
   473→
   474→    zm = lang._zone_map
   475→    if zm is None:
   476→        return [], {}
   477→
   478→    graph = lang._dep_graph or lang.build_dep_graph(path)
   479→    extra = find_external_test_files(path, lang.name)
   480→    entries, potential = detect_test_coverage(graph, zm, lang.name,
   481→                                              extra_test_files=extra or None)
   482→    entries = filter_entries(zm, entries, "test_coverage")
   483→
   484→    results = []
   485→    for e in entries:
   486→        results.append(make_finding(
   487→            "test_coverage", e["file"], e.get("name", ""),
   488→            tier=e["tier"], confidence=e["confidence"],
   489→            summary=e["summary"], detail=e.get("detail", {}),
   490→        ))
   491→
   492→    if results:
   493→        log(f"         test coverage: {len(results)} findings ({potential} production files)")
   494→    else:
   495→        log(f"         test coverage: clean ({potential} production files)")
   496→
   497→    return results, {"test_coverage": potential}
   498→
```

```
1→"""Narrative orchestrator — compute_narrative() entry point."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from ._constants import STRUCTURAL_MERGE
     6→
     7→from .phase import _detect_phase, _detect_milestone
     8→from .dimensions import _analyze_dimensions, _analyze_debt
     9→from .actions import _compute_actions, _compute_tools
    10→from .headline import _compute_headline
    11→from .reminders import _compute_reminders
    12→from .strategy import _compute_strategy
    13→
    14→
    15→def _count_open_by_detector(findings: dict) -> dict[str, int]:
    16→    """Count open findings by detector, merging structural sub-detectors."""
    17→    by_det: dict[str, int] = {}
    18→    for f in findings.values():
    19→        if f["status"] != "open":
    20→            continue
    21→        det = f.get("detector", "unknown")
    22→        if det in STRUCTURAL_MERGE:
    23→            det = "structural"
    24→        by_det[det] = by_det.get(det, 0) + 1
    25→    return by_det
    26→
    27→
    28→def _compute_badge_status() -> dict:
    29→    """Check if scorecard.png exists and whether README references it."""
    30→    from ..utils import PROJECT_ROOT
    31→
    32→    scorecard_path = PROJECT_ROOT / "scorecard.png"
    33→    generated = scorecard_path.exists()
    34→
    35→    in_readme = False
    36→    if generated:
    37→        for readme_name in ("README.md", "readme.md", "README.MD"):
    38→            readme_path = PROJECT_ROOT / readme_name
    39→            if readme_path.exists():
    40→                try:
    41→                    in_readme = "scorecard.png" in readme_path.read_text()
    42→                except OSError:
    43→                    pass
    44→                break
    45→
    46→    recommendation = None
    47→    if generated and not in_readme:
    48→        recommendation = 'Add to README: <img src="scorecard.png" width="400">'
    49→
    50→    return {
    51→        "generated": generated,
    52→        "in_readme": in_readme,
    53→        "path": "scorecard.png",
    54→        "recommendation": recommendation,
    55→    }
    56→
    57→
    58→def compute_narrative(state: dict, *, diff: dict | None = None,
    59→                      lang: str | None = None,
    60→                      command: str | None = None) -> dict:
    61→    """Compute structured narrative context from state data.
    62→
    63→    Returns a dict with: phase, headline, dimensions, actions, tools, debt, milestone.
    64→
    65→    Args:
    66→        state: Current state dict.
    67→        diff: Scan diff (only present after a scan).
    68→        lang: Language name (e.g. "python", "typescript").
    69→        command: The command that triggered this (e.g. "scan", "fix", "resolve").
    70→    """
    71→    raw_history = state.get("scan_history", [])
    72→    # Filter history to current language to avoid mixing trajectories.
    73→    # Include entries without a lang field (pre-date this feature) for backward compat.
    74→    history = ([h for h in raw_history if h.get("lang") in (lang, None)]
    75→               if lang else raw_history)
    76→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
    77→    stats = state.get("stats", {})
    78→    obj_strict = state.get("objective_strict")
    79→    obj_score = state.get("objective_score")
    80→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
    81→
    82→    by_det = _count_open_by_detector(findings)
    83→    badge = _compute_badge_status()
    84→
    85→    phase = _detect_phase(history, obj_strict)
    86→    dimensions = _analyze_dimensions(dim_scores, history, state)
    87→    debt = _analyze_debt(dim_scores, findings, history)
    88→    milestone = _detect_milestone(state, diff, history)
    89→    actions = _compute_actions(by_det, dim_scores, state, debt, lang)
    90→    strategy = _compute_strategy(findings, by_det, actions, phase, lang)
    91→    tools = _compute_tools(by_det, lang, badge)
    92→    headline = _compute_headline(phase, dimensions, debt, milestone, diff,
    93→                                 obj_strict, obj_score, stats, history,
    94→                                 open_by_detector=by_det)
    95→    reminders, updated_reminder_history = _compute_reminders(
    96→        state, lang, phase, debt, actions, dimensions, badge, command)
    97→
    98→    return {
    99→        "phase": phase,
   100→        "headline": headline,
   101→        "dimensions": dimensions,
   102→        "actions": actions,
   103→        "strategy": strategy,
   104→        "tools": tools,
   105→        "debt": debt,
   106→        "milestone": milestone,
   107→        "reminders": reminders,
   108→        "reminder_history": updated_reminder_history,
   109→    }
   110→
```
