```
1→import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
     2→import { ASPECT_RATIO_TO_RESOLUTION } from "./aspectRatios";
     3→import { nanoid } from "nanoid";
     4→import { AuthError, NetworkError, ValidationError } from "./errors";
     5→import { handleError } from '@/shared/lib/errorHandler';
     6→import { isAbortError } from '@/shared/lib/errorUtils';
     7→
     8→/**
     9→ * Default aspect ratio to use when project aspect ratio is not found
    10→ */
    11→const DEFAULT_ASPECT_RATIO = "1:1";
    12→
    13→/**
    14→ * Interface for project resolution lookup result
    15→ */
    16→interface ProjectResolutionResult {
    17→  resolution: string;
    18→  aspectRatio: string;
    19→}
    20→
    21→/**
    22→ * Resolves the resolution for a project, either using provided custom resolution
    23→ * or looking up the project's aspect ratio and mapping it to a standard resolution.
    24→ * 
    25→ * @param projectId - The project ID to look up
    26→ * @param customResolution - Optional custom resolution (e.g., "1024x768")
    27→ * @returns Promise resolving to the final resolution string
    28→ */
    29→export async function resolveProjectResolution(
    30→  projectId: string, 
    31→  customResolution?: string
    32→): Promise<ProjectResolutionResult> {
    33→  // If custom resolution is provided and valid, use it
    34→  if (customResolution?.trim()) {
    35→    return {
    36→      resolution: customResolution.trim(),
    37→      aspectRatio: "custom"
    38→    };
    39→  }
    40→
    41→  try {
    42→    // Fetch project aspect ratio from database
    43→    const { data: project } = await supabase
    44→      .from("projects")
    45→      .select("aspect_ratio")
    46→      .eq("id", projectId)
    47→      .single();
    48→
    49→    const aspectRatioKey = project?.aspect_ratio ?? DEFAULT_ASPECT_RATIO;
    50→    const resolution = ASPECT_RATIO_TO_RESOLUTION[aspectRatioKey] ?? ASPECT_RATIO_TO_RESOLUTION[DEFAULT_ASPECT_RATIO];
    51→
    52→    return {
    53→      resolution,
    54→      aspectRatio: aspectRatioKey
    55→    };
    56→  } catch (error) {
    57→    handleError(error, { context: 'TaskCreation', showToast: false });
    58→    // Fallback to default resolution
    59→    return {
    60→      resolution: ASPECT_RATIO_TO_RESOLUTION[DEFAULT_ASPECT_RATIO],
    61→      aspectRatio: DEFAULT_ASPECT_RATIO
    62→    };
    63→  }
    64→}
    65→
    66→/**
    67→ * Generates a UUID with fallback for mobile browsers
    68→ * Uses crypto.randomUUID() when available, falls back to nanoid
    69→ * 
    70→ * @returns UUID string
    71→ */
    72→export function generateUUID(): string {
    73→  // Check if crypto.randomUUID is available (requires secure context and modern browser)
    74→  if (typeof crypto !== 'undefined' && typeof crypto.randomUUID === 'function') {
    75→    try {
    76→      return crypto.randomUUID();
    77→    } catch (error) {
    78→    }
    79→  }
    80→  
    81→  // Fallback to nanoid for mobile browsers or when crypto.randomUUID is not available
    82→  return nanoid();
    83→}
    84→
    85→/**
    86→ * Generates a unique task ID with a prefix and timestamp
    87→ * 
    88→ * @param taskTypePrefix - Prefix for the task ID (e.g., "sm_travel_orchestrator")
    89→ * @returns Unique task ID string
    90→ */
    91→export function generateTaskId(taskTypePrefix: string): string {
    92→  const runId = new Date().toISOString().replace(/[-:.TZ]/g, "");
    93→  const shortUuid = generateUUID().slice(0, 6);
    94→  return `${taskTypePrefix}_${runId.substring(2, 10)}_${shortUuid}`;
    95→}
    96→
    97→/**
    98→ * Generates a run ID for tasks that need it
    99→ * 
   100→ * @returns Run ID string based on current timestamp
   101→ */
   102→export function generateRunId(): string {
   103→  return new Date().toISOString().replace(/[-:.TZ]/g, "");
   104→}
   105→
   106→/**
   107→ * Validation error type for task parameter validation
   108→ * Extends ValidationError for consistent error handling
   109→ */
   110→export class TaskValidationError extends ValidationError {
   111→  constructor(message: string, field?: string) {
   112→    super(message, { field });
   113→    this.name = 'TaskValidationError';
   114→  }
   115→}
   116→
   117→/**
   118→ * Common task creation parameters that all tasks should have
   119→ */
   120→interface BaseTaskParams {
   121→  project_id: string;
   122→  task_type: string;
   123→  params: Record<string, unknown>;
   124→}
   125→
   126→/**
   127→ * Result from creating a task via the edge function.
   128→ * This is the standard response shape for all task creation operations.
   129→ */
   130→export interface TaskCreationResult {
   131→  /** The created task's unique ID */
   132→  task_id: string;
   133→  /** Task status (typically 'pending' for newly created tasks) */
   134→  status: string;
   135→  /** Error message if task creation failed */
   136→  error?: string;
   137→}
   138→
   139→/**
   140→ * Creates a task using the unified create-task edge function
   141→ * 
   142→ * @param taskParams - The task parameters to create
   143→ * @returns Promise resolving to the created task data
   144→ */
   145→export async function createTask(taskParams: BaseTaskParams): Promise<TaskCreationResult> {
   146→  // Get current session for authentication
   147→  const { data: { session } } = await supabase.auth.getSession();
   148→
   149→  if (!session) {
   150→    throw new AuthError('Please log in to create tasks', { needsLogin: true });
   151→  }
   152→
   153→  const startTime = Date.now();
   154→  const requestId = `${startTime}-${Math.random().toString(36).slice(2, 8)}`;
   155→  const timeoutMs = 20000; // 20s safety timeout to avoid indefinite UI stall
   156→  const controller = new AbortController();
   157→  const timeout = setTimeout(() => {
   158→    controller.abort();
   159→  }, timeoutMs);
   160→
   161→  try {
   162→    // Generate an idempotency key to prevent duplicate task creation from
   163→    // network retries or double-clicks. The server will return the existing
   164→    // task if this key was already used.
   165→    const idempotency_key = generateUUID();
   166→
   167→    const { data, error } = await supabase.functions.invoke('create-task', {
   168→      body: {
   169→        params: taskParams.params,
   170→        task_type: taskParams.task_type,
   171→        project_id: taskParams.project_id,
   172→        dependant_on: null,
   173→        idempotency_key,
   174→      },
   175→      headers: {
   176→        Authorization: `Bearer ${session.access_token}`,
   177→      },
   178→      signal: controller.signal,
   179→    });
   180→
   181→    if (error) {
   182→      throw new Error(error.message || 'Failed to create task');
   183→    }
   184→
   185→    // Task creation events are now handled by DataFreshnessManager via realtime events
   186→    // No manual invalidation needed - the smart polling system handles cache updates automatically
   187→
   188→    return data;
   189→  } catch (err: unknown) {
   190→    const context = {
   191→      requestId,
   192→      taskType: taskParams.task_type,
   193→      projectId: taskParams.project_id,
   194→      durationMs: Date.now() - startTime,
   195→    };
   196→
   197→    // Normalize abort errors for better UX
   198→    if (isAbortError(err)) {
   199→      throw new NetworkError('Task creation timed out. Please try again.', {
   200→        isTimeout: true,
   201→        context,
   202→        cause: err,
   203→      });
   204→    }
   205→
   206→    handleError(err, { context: 'TaskCreation', showToast: false });
   207→    throw err;
   208→  } finally {
   209→    clearTimeout(timeout);
   210→  }
   211→}
   212→
   213→/**
   214→ * Utility function to expand single-element arrays to match a target count
   215→ * This is commonly needed for steerable motion tasks where user provides
   216→ * one value that should be applied to all segments.
   217→ * 
   218→ * @param arr - Array to potentially expand
   219→ * @param targetCount - Target count for the array
   220→ * @returns Expanded array or original array if no expansion needed
   221→ */
   222→export function expandArrayToCount<T>(arr: T[] | undefined, targetCount: number): T[] {
   223→  if (!arr || arr.length === 0) {
   224→    return [];
   225→  }
   226→
   227→  if (arr.length === 1 && targetCount > 1) {
   228→    return Array(targetCount).fill(arr[0]);
   229→  }
   230→
   231→  // Truncate arrays that are longer than the target count
   232→  // This handles the case where images were deleted and arrays are stale
   233→  if (arr.length > targetCount) {
   234→    return arr.slice(0, targetCount);
   235→  }
   236→
   237→  return arr;
   238→}
   239→
   240→/**
   241→ * Validates that required fields are present and non-empty
   242→ * 
   243→ * @param params - Object to validate
   244→ * @param requiredFields - Array of required field names
   245→ * @throws TaskValidationError if validation fails
   246→ */
   247→export function validateRequiredFields(params: Record<string, unknown>, requiredFields: string[]): void {
   248→  for (const field of requiredFields) {
   249→    const value = params[field];
   250→    
   251→    if (value === undefined || value === null) {
   252→      throw new TaskValidationError(`${field} is required`, field);
   253→    }
   254→    
   255→    // Check for empty arrays
   256→    if (Array.isArray(value) && value.length === 0) {
   257→      throw new TaskValidationError(`${field} cannot be empty`, field);
   258→    }
   259→    
   260→    // Check for empty strings
   261→    if (typeof value === 'string' && value.trim() === '') {
   262→      throw new TaskValidationError(`${field} cannot be empty`, field);
   263→    }
   264→  }
   265→}
   266→
   267→/**
   268→ * Safely parses JSON string with fallback
   269→ * 
   270→ * @param jsonStr - JSON string to parse
   271→ * @param fallback - Fallback value if parsing fails
   272→ * @returns Parsed object or fallback
   273→ */
   274→export function safeParseJson<T>(jsonStr: string | undefined, fallback: T): T {
   275→  if (!jsonStr) return fallback;
   276→
   277→  try {
   278→    return JSON.parse(jsonStr) as T;
   279→  } catch {
   280→    return fallback;
   281→  }
   282→}
   283→
   284→/**
   285→ * Hires fix API parameters for image generation/edit tasks.
   286→ * Uses snake_case to match API directly.
   287→ */
   288→export interface HiresFixApiParams {
   289→  /** Number of inference steps (used for single-pass or base pass in two-pass mode) */
   290→  num_inference_steps?: number;
   291→  hires_scale?: number;
   292→  hires_steps?: number;
   293→  hires_denoise?: number;
   294→  /** Lightning LoRA strength for phase 1 (initial generation) */
   295→  lightning_lora_strength_phase_1?: number;
   296→  /** Lightning LoRA strength for phase 2 (hires/refinement pass) */
   297→  lightning_lora_strength_phase_2?: number;
   298→  additional_loras?: Record<string, string>;
   299→}
   300→
   301→/**
   302→ * Builds a flat params record from HiresFixApiParams.
   303→ * Only includes fields that are defined, so the result can be spread into task params.
   304→ *
   305→ * @param hiresFix - The hires fix config (may be undefined)
   306→ * @returns Flat record of hires-fix params (empty object if input is undefined)
   307→ */
   308→export function buildHiresFixParams(hiresFix: HiresFixApiParams | undefined): Record<string, unknown> {
   309→  if (!hiresFix) return {};
   310→
   311→  const params: Record<string, unknown> = {};
   312→
   313→  if (hiresFix.num_inference_steps !== undefined) params.num_inference_steps = hiresFix.num_inference_steps;
   314→  if (hiresFix.hires_scale !== undefined) params.hires_scale = hiresFix.hires_scale;
   315→  if (hiresFix.hires_steps !== undefined) params.hires_steps = hiresFix.hires_steps;
   316→  if (hiresFix.hires_denoise !== undefined) params.hires_denoise = hiresFix.hires_denoise;
   317→  if (hiresFix.lightning_lora_strength_phase_1 !== undefined) params.lightning_lora_strength_phase_1 = hiresFix.lightning_lora_strength_phase_1;
   318→  if (hiresFix.lightning_lora_strength_phase_2 !== undefined) params.lightning_lora_strength_phase_2 = hiresFix.lightning_lora_strength_phase_2;
   319→  if (hiresFix.additional_loras && Object.keys(hiresFix.additional_loras).length > 0) params.additional_loras = hiresFix.additional_loras;
   320→
   321→  return params;
   322→}
   323→
   324→/**
   325→ * Processes batch Promise.allSettled results with standard error handling.
   326→ *
   327→ * - Throws if every task failed (surfaces the first error).
   328→ * - Logs individual failures when some succeed.
   329→ * - Returns the fulfilled TaskCreationResult values.
   330→ *
   331→ * @param results - The settled promise array from Promise.allSettled
   332→ * @param context - Label used in error/log messages (e.g. "BatchImageGeneration")
   333→ */
   334→export function processBatchResults(
   335→  results: PromiseSettledResult<TaskCreationResult>[],
   336→  context: string,
   337→): TaskCreationResult[] {
   338→  const successful = results.filter(r => r.status === 'fulfilled').length;
   339→  const failed = results.filter(r => r.status === 'rejected').length;
   340→
   341→  if (successful === 0) {
   342→    const firstError = results.find(r => r.status === 'rejected') as PromiseRejectedResult;
   343→    throw new Error(`All batch tasks failed: ${firstError.reason}`);
   344→  }
   345→
   346→  if (failed > 0) {
   347→    results.forEach((result, index) => {
   348→      if (result.status === 'rejected') {
   349→        handleError(result.reason, { context: `${context}:task${index + 1}`, showToast: false });
   350→      }
   351→    });
   352→  }
   353→
   354→  return results
   355→    .filter((r): r is PromiseFulfilledResult<TaskCreationResult> => r.status === 'fulfilled')
   356→    .map(r => r.value);
   357→}
   358→
```

```
1→/**
     2→ * Standardized error types for the application
     3→ *
     4→ * These error classes help distinguish between different error categories,
     5→ * enabling better error handling, user messaging, and logging.
     6→ */
     7→
     8→/**
     9→ * Base application error with additional context
    10→ */
    11→export class AppError extends Error {
    12→  /** Whether this error should be shown to the user via toast */
    13→  public readonly showToast: boolean;
    14→  /** Additional context for logging */
    15→  public readonly context?: Record<string, unknown>;
    16→  /** Original error if this wraps another error */
    17→  public readonly cause?: Error;
    18→
    19→  constructor(
    20→    message: string,
    21→    options?: {
    22→      showToast?: boolean;
    23→      context?: Record<string, unknown>;
    24→      cause?: Error;
    25→    }
    26→  ) {
    27→    super(message);
    28→    this.name = 'AppError';
    29→    this.showToast = options?.showToast ?? true;
    30→    this.context = options?.context;
    31→    this.cause = options?.cause;
    32→  }
    33→}
    34→
    35→/**
    36→ * Network-related errors (fetch failures, timeouts, offline)
    37→ * These are often transient and may benefit from retry
    38→ */
    39→export class NetworkError extends AppError {
    40→  /** Whether the user appears to be offline */
    41→  public readonly isOffline: boolean;
    42→  /** Whether this was a timeout */
    43→  public readonly isTimeout: boolean;
    44→
    45→  constructor(
    46→    message: string,
    47→    options?: {
    48→      isOffline?: boolean;
    49→      isTimeout?: boolean;
    50→      context?: Record<string, unknown>;
    51→      cause?: Error;
    52→    }
    53→  ) {
    54→    super(message, { showToast: true, ...options });
    55→    this.name = 'NetworkError';
    56→    this.isOffline = options?.isOffline ?? !navigator.onLine;
    57→    this.isTimeout = options?.isTimeout ?? false;
    58→  }
    59→
    60→  static fromError(error: Error, context?: Record<string, unknown>): NetworkError {
    61→    const isTimeout = error.name === 'AbortError' || error.message.includes('timeout');
    62→    const isOffline = !navigator.onLine;
    63→
    64→    let message = 'Network request failed';
    65→    if (isOffline) {
    66→      message = 'You appear to be offline. Please check your connection.';
    67→    } else if (isTimeout) {
    68→      message = 'Request timed out. Please try again.';
    69→    }
    70→
    71→    return new NetworkError(message, { isOffline, isTimeout, context, cause: error });
    72→  }
    73→}
    74→
    75→/**
    76→ * Authentication/authorization errors
    77→ * User needs to log in or doesn't have permission
    78→ */
    79→export class AuthError extends AppError {
    80→  /** Whether the user needs to log in (vs lacking permission) */
    81→  public readonly needsLogin: boolean;
    82→
    83→  constructor(
    84→    message: string,
    85→    options?: {
    86→      needsLogin?: boolean;
    87→      context?: Record<string, unknown>;
    88→      cause?: Error;
    89→    }
    90→  ) {
    91→    super(message, { showToast: true, ...options });
    92→    this.name = 'AuthError';
    93→    this.needsLogin = options?.needsLogin ?? true;
    94→  }
    95→
    96→  static fromError(error: Error, context?: Record<string, unknown>): AuthError {
    97→    const message = error.message.toLowerCase();
    98→    const needsLogin = message.includes('unauthorized') || message.includes('not authenticated');
    99→
   100→    return new AuthError(
   101→      needsLogin ? 'Please log in to continue' : 'You don\'t have permission for this action',
   102→      { needsLogin, context, cause: error }
   103→    );
   104→  }
   105→}
   106→
   107→/**
   108→ * Validation errors (invalid input, missing required fields)
   109→ * These are user errors that can be corrected
   110→ */
   111→export class ValidationError extends AppError {
   112→  /** The field that failed validation, if applicable */
   113→  public readonly field?: string;
   114→
   115→  constructor(
   116→    message: string,
   117→    options?: {
   118→      field?: string;
   119→      context?: Record<string, unknown>;
   120→      cause?: Error;
   121→    }
   122→  ) {
   123→    super(message, { showToast: true, ...options });
   124→    this.name = 'ValidationError';
   125→    this.field = options?.field;
   126→  }
   127→}
   128→
   129→/**
   130→ * Server/API errors (5xx responses, unexpected server behavior)
   131→ * These are typically not the user's fault
   132→ */
   133→export class ServerError extends AppError {
   134→  /** HTTP status code if available */
   135→  public readonly statusCode?: number;
   136→
   137→  constructor(
   138→    message: string,
   139→    options?: {
   140→      statusCode?: number;
   141→      context?: Record<string, unknown>;
   142→      cause?: Error;
   143→    }
   144→  ) {
   145→    super(message, { showToast: true, ...options });
   146→    this.name = 'ServerError';
   147→    this.statusCode = options?.statusCode;
   148→  }
   149→}
   150→
   151→/**
   152→ * Errors that should be silently logged (not shown to user)
   153→ * Use for expected failures like localStorage unavailable, optional features
   154→ */
   155→export class SilentError extends AppError {
   156→  constructor(
   157→    message: string,
   158→    options?: {
   159→      context?: Record<string, unknown>;
   160→      cause?: Error;
   161→    }
   162→  ) {
   163→    super(message, { showToast: false, ...options });
   164→    this.name = 'SilentError';
   165→  }
   166→}
   167→
   168→/**
   169→ * Type guard to check if an error is one of our app errors
   170→ */
   171→export function isAppError(error: unknown): error is AppError {
   172→  return error instanceof AppError;
   173→}
   174→
   175→/**
   176→ * Type guard to check if an error is a network error
   177→ */
   178→export function isNetworkError(error: unknown): error is NetworkError {
   179→  return error instanceof NetworkError;
   180→}
   181→
   182→/**
   183→ * Type guard to check if an error is an auth error
   184→ */
   185→export function isAuthError(error: unknown): error is AuthError {
   186→  return error instanceof AuthError;
   187→}
   188→
   189→/**
   190→ * Categorize an unknown error into an appropriate AppError type
   191→ */
   192→export function categorizeError(error: unknown, context?: Record<string, unknown>): AppError {
   193→  // Already an AppError
   194→  if (isAppError(error)) {
   195→    return error;
   196→  }
   197→
   198→  // Convert to Error if needed
   199→  const err = error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error));
   200→  const message = err.message.toLowerCase();
   201→
   202→  // Network errors
   203→  if (
   204→    err.name === 'AbortError' ||
   205→    message.includes('network') ||
   206→    message.includes('fetch') ||
   207→    message.includes('timeout') ||
   208→    message.includes('failed to fetch') ||
   209→    !navigator.onLine
   210→  ) {
   211→    return NetworkError.fromError(err, context);
   212→  }
   213→
   214→  // Auth errors
   215→  if (
   216→    message.includes('unauthorized') ||
   217→    message.includes('forbidden') ||
   218→    message.includes('not authenticated') ||
   219→    message.includes('authentication required')
   220→  ) {
   221→    return AuthError.fromError(err, context);
   222→  }
   223→
   224→  // Validation errors
   225→  if (
   226→    message.includes('required') ||
   227→    message.includes('invalid') ||
   228→    message.includes('validation')
   229→  ) {
   230→    return new ValidationError(err.message, { context, cause: err });
   231→  }
   232→
   233→  // Default to generic AppError
   234→  return new AppError(err.message, { context, cause: err });
   235→}
   236→
```

```
     3→ *
     4→ * Provides shared state to deeply-nested lightbox components without prop drilling.
     5→ * Organized into logical domains accessible via focused hooks:
     6→ * - useLightboxCore() - onClose, readOnly, isMobile, selectedProjectId
     7→ * - useLightboxMedia() - media, isVideo, effectiveUrls, dimensions

   198→export function useLightboxCoreSafe(): LightboxCoreState {
```

```
1→import { useQuery, useMutation, useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
     2→import { useRef, useEffect, useCallback } from 'react';
     3→import { useProject } from '@/shared/contexts/ProjectContext';
     4→import { handleError } from '@/shared/lib/errorHandler';
     5→import { queryKeys } from '@/shared/lib/queryKeys';
     6→import { supabase } from '@/integrations/supabase/client';
     7→import { QUERY_PRESETS, STANDARD_RETRY_DELAY } from '@/shared/lib/queryDefaults';
     8→import {
     9→  enqueueSettingsWrite,
    10→  setSettingsWriteFunction,
    11→  type QueuedWrite
    12→} from '@/shared/lib/settingsWriteQueue';
    13→import { deepMerge } from '@/shared/lib/deepEqual';
    14→import { isCancellationError } from '@/shared/lib/errorUtils';
    15→import {
    16→  fetchToolSettingsSupabase,
    17→  getUserWithTimeout,
    18→  type SettingsFetchResult,
    19→} from '@/shared/lib/toolSettingsService';
    20→
    21→export type SettingsScope = 'user' | 'project' | 'shot';
    22→
    23→interface UpdateToolSettingsParams {
    24→  scope: SettingsScope;
    25→  id: string;
    26→  toolId: string;
    27→  patch: unknown;
    28→}
    29→
    30→/**
    31→ * Raw write function - performs the actual DB update.
    32→ * Used internally by the settings write queue.
    33→ *
    34→ * @internal Use updateToolSettingsSupabase (queued) for normal usage
    35→ */
    36→async function rawUpdateToolSettings(write: QueuedWrite): Promise<Record<string, unknown>> {
    37→  const { scope, entityId: id, toolId, patch } = write;
    38→
    39→  try {
    40→    let tableName: string;
    41→    switch (scope) {
    42→      case 'user':
    43→        tableName = 'users';
    44→        break;
    45→      case 'project':
    46→        tableName = 'projects';
    47→        break;
    48→      case 'shot':
    49→        tableName = 'shots';
    50→        break;
    51→      default:
    52→        throw new Error(`Invalid scope: ${scope}`);
    53→    }
    54→
    55→    // For patch updates, we need to fetch current settings to merge
    56→    // This is necessary because the caller provides a partial update
    57→    // TODO: In the future, consider passing full settings to eliminate this fetch
    58→    const { data: currentEntity, error: fetchError } = await supabase
    59→      .from(tableName)
    60→      .select('settings')
    61→      .eq('id', id)
    62→      .single();
    63→
    64→    if (fetchError) {
    65→      // Check for network exhaustion - include the error type in the message for downstream handling
    66→      const errorMessage = fetchError.message || '';
    67→      if (errorMessage.includes('ERR_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES') ||
    68→          errorMessage.includes('Failed to fetch') ||
    69→          fetchError.code === 'ERR_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES') {
    70→        throw new Error(`Network exhaustion: ${errorMessage}`);
    71→      }
    72→      throw new Error(`Failed to fetch current ${scope} settings: ${errorMessage}`);
    73→    }
    74→
    75→    // Merge patch with current tool settings
    76→    const currentSettings = (currentEntity?.settings as Record<string, unknown>) ?? {};
    77→    const currentToolSettings = (currentSettings[toolId] as Record<string, unknown>) ?? {};
    78→    const updatedToolSettings = deepMerge({}, currentToolSettings, patch);
    79→
    80→    // Use atomic PostgreSQL function to update settings
    81→    // This is much faster than update() because it happens in a single DB operation
    82→    const { error: rpcError } = await supabase.rpc('update_tool_settings_atomic', {
    83→      p_table_name: tableName,
    84→      p_id: id,
    85→      p_tool_id: toolId,
    86→      p_settings: updatedToolSettings
    87→    });
    88→
    89→    if (rpcError) {
    90→      throw new Error(`Failed to update ${scope} settings: ${rpcError.message}`);
    91→    }
    92→
    93→    // CRITICAL: Return the full merged settings, not just the patch
    94→    // This ensures the cache gets the exact same data that was saved to the DB
    95→    // Prevents data loss when cache is stale (e.g., multiple tabs, concurrent edits)
    96→    return updatedToolSettings;
    97→
    98→  } catch (error: unknown) {
    99→    // Handle abort errors silently to reduce noise during task cancellation
   100→    if (isCancellationError(error)) {
   101→      // Don't log these as errors - they're expected during component unmounting
   102→      throw new Error('Request was cancelled');
   103→    }
   104→
   105→    console.error('[rawUpdateToolSettings] Error:', error);
   106→    throw error;
   107→  }
   108→}
   109→
   110→// Initialize the queue with our raw write function
   111→setSettingsWriteFunction(rawUpdateToolSettings);
   112→
   113→/**
   114→ * Update tool settings using the global write queue.
   115→ *
   116→ * This is the main entry point for settings updates. It:
   117→ * - Debounces rapid updates (300ms window)
   118→ * - Coalesces multiple writes to the same target
   119→ * - Serializes writes globally to prevent network exhaustion
   120→ *
   121→ * @param params - The update parameters
   122→ * @param mode - 'debounced' (default) or 'immediate' for flush-on-unmount
   123→ * @returns The full merged settings after update
   124→ */
   125→export function updateToolSettingsSupabase(
   126→  params: UpdateToolSettingsParams,
   127→  _signal?: AbortSignal,
   128→  mode: 'debounced' | 'immediate' = 'debounced'
   129→): Promise<Record<string, unknown>> {
   130→  const { scope, id, toolId, patch } = params;
   131→
   132→  return enqueueSettingsWrite({
   133→    scope,
   134→    entityId: id,
   135→    toolId,
   136→    patch: patch as Record<string, unknown>,
   137→  }, mode);
   138→}
   139→
   140→// ============================================================================
   141→// Cache format helpers
   142→// ============================================================================
   143→
   144→/**
   145→ * Helper to check if a cache value has the wrapper format.
   146→ * fetchToolSettingsSupabase always returns { settings, hasShotSettings },
   147→ * but legacy or manually-set cache entries may use flat format.
   148→ */
   149→function isSettingsWrapper(data: unknown): data is SettingsFetchResult {
   150→  if (!data || typeof data !== 'object') return false;
   151→  return 'settings' in data && 'hasShotSettings' in data;
   152→}
   153→
   154→/**
   155→ * Helper to extract settings from cache data (handles wrapper format)
   156→ * Cache stores data as { settings: T, hasShotSettings: boolean }
   157→ */
   158→export function extractSettingsFromCache<T>(cacheData: unknown): T | undefined {
   159→  if (!cacheData) return undefined;
   160→  return isSettingsWrapper(cacheData) ? (cacheData.settings as T) : (cacheData as T);
   161→}
   162→
   163→/**
   164→ * Helper to update settings cache with proper wrapper format
   165→ * Use this in setQueryData callbacks for optimistic updates
   166→ *
   167→ * @param prev - The previous cache value (may be wrapper or flat format)
   168→ * @param updater - Either an object of updates, or a function that receives prevSettings and returns updates
   169→ */
   170→export function updateSettingsCache<T extends Record<string, unknown>>(
   171→  prev: unknown,
   172→  updater: Partial<T> | ((prevSettings: T) => Partial<T>)
   173→): SettingsFetchResult<T> {
   174→  const wrapper = isSettingsWrapper(prev);
   175→  const prevSettings = (wrapper ? ((prev as SettingsFetchResult).settings ?? {}) : (prev ?? {})) as T;
   176→  const updates = typeof updater === 'function' ? updater(prevSettings) : updater;
   177→  return {
   178→    settings: { ...prevSettings, ...updates } as T,
   179→    hasShotSettings: wrapper ? ((prev as SettingsFetchResult).hasShotSettings ?? false) : false
   180→  };
   181→}
   182→
   183→// Type overloads
   184→export function useToolSettings<T>(toolId: string, context?: { projectId?: string; shotId?: string; enabled?: boolean }): {
   185→  settings: T | undefined;
   186→  isLoading: boolean;
   187→  error: Error | null;
   188→  update: (scope: SettingsScope, settings: Partial<T>) => Promise<void>;
   189→  isUpdating: boolean;
   190→  /** Whether the shot had settings stored in DB (vs just defaults/project settings) */
   191→  hasShotSettings: boolean;
   192→};
   193→
   194→/**
   195→ * Low-level hook for reading and writing tool settings across all scopes.
   196→ *
   197→ * Performs cascade resolution (defaults -> user -> project -> shot) and returns
   198→ * a merged settings object. Writes go through the global settings write queue.
   199→ *
   200→ * Most features should use `useAutoSaveSettings` instead, which adds auto-save,
   201→ * dirty tracking, and entity-change handling on top of this hook.
   202→ *
   203→ * Use this directly only when you need manual save control or complex write patterns.
   204→ *
   205→ * @see docs/structure_detail/settings_system.md for the full settings hook decision tree
   206→ */
   207→export function useToolSettings<T>(
   208→  toolId: string,
   209→  context?: { projectId?: string; shotId?: string; enabled?: boolean }
   210→) {
   211→  const { selectedProjectId } = useProject();
   212→  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
   213→
   214→  // Ref to track active update controllers for cleanup
   215→  const updateControllersRef = useRef<Set<AbortController>>(new Set());
   216→
   217→  // Determine parameter shapes
   218→  const projectId: string | undefined = context?.projectId ?? selectedProjectId;
   219→  const shotId: string | undefined = context?.shotId;
   220→  const fetchEnabled: boolean = context?.enabled ?? true;
   221→
   222→  // Refs to access current values in stable callbacks without recreating them
   223→  const projectIdRef = useRef(projectId);
   224→  projectIdRef.current = projectId;
   225→  const shotIdRef = useRef(shotId);
   226→  shotIdRef.current = shotId;
   227→
   228→  // Cleanup abort controllers on unmount
   229→  // NOTE: We intentionally do NOT abort active update mutations on unmount.
   230→  // Settings saves should complete even if the component unmounts (e.g., during navigation).
   231→  // The mutation will complete in the background and update the cache correctly.
   232→  useEffect(() => {
   233→    return () => {
   234→      // Just clear the tracking set - mutations will complete on their own
   235→      updateControllersRef.current.clear();
   236→    };
   237→  }, []);
   238→
   239→  // Fetch merged settings using Supabase with mobile optimizations
   240→  const { data: queryResult, isLoading, error } = useQuery({
   241→    [REDACTED](toolId, projectId, shotId),
   242→    queryFn: async ({ signal }) => {
   243→      try {
   244→        const result = await fetchToolSettingsSupabase(toolId, { projectId, shotId }, signal);
   245→        return result;
   246→      } catch (err: unknown) {
   247→        // For cancelled requests, throw a specific error that retry logic handles
   248→        // (React Query doesn't allow returning undefined from query functions)
   249→        if (isCancellationError(err)) {
   250→          throw new Error('Request was cancelled');
   251→        }
   252→        throw err;
   253→      }
   254→    },
   255→    enabled: !!toolId && fetchEnabled,
   256→    // Use static preset - tool settings rarely change, mutation invalidates on save
   257→    ...QUERY_PRESETS.static,
   258→    staleTime: 10 * 60 * 1000, // Override: 10 minutes (longer than static default)
   259→    // Mobile-specific optimizations
   260→    retry: (failureCount, error) => {
   261→      // Don't retry auth errors, cancelled requests, or abort errors
   262→      if (error?.message?.includes('Authentication required') ||
   263→          error?.message?.includes('Request was cancelled') ||
   264→          error?.name === 'AbortError' ||
   265→          error?.message?.includes('signal is aborted')) {
   266→        return false;
   267→      }
   268→      // Don't retry on network exhaustion - retrying just makes it worse
   269→      if (error?.message?.includes('ERR_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES') ||
   270→          error?.message?.includes('Network exhaustion')) {
   271→        return false;
   272→      }
   273→      // Retry up to 3 times for network errors on mobile
   274→      return failureCount < 3;
   275→    },
   276→    retryDelay: STANDARD_RETRY_DELAY,
   277→    networkMode: 'online',
   278→  });
   279→
   280→  // Extract settings and hasShotSettings from the query result
   281→  const wrapper = isSettingsWrapper(queryResult);
   282→  const settings = wrapper ? (queryResult as SettingsFetchResult).settings : queryResult;
   283→  const hasShotSettings = wrapper ? ((queryResult as SettingsFetchResult).hasShotSettings ?? false) : false;
   284→
   285→  // Log errors for debugging (except expected cancellations)
   286→  if (error && !error?.message?.includes('Request was cancelled')) {
   287→    handleError(error, { context: 'useToolSettings', showToast: false });
   288→  }
   289→
   290→  // Update settings mutation
   291→  const updateMutation = useMutation({
   292→    mutationFn: async ({ scope, settings: newSettings, signal, entityId }: {
   293→      scope: SettingsScope;
   294→      settings: Partial<T>;
   295→      signal?: AbortSignal;
   296→      entityId?: string;
   297→    }) => {
   298→      // Prefer explicitly provided entityId (snapshotted at schedule time) to avoid drift
   299→      let idForScope: string | undefined = entityId;
   300→
   301→      if (!idForScope) {
   302→        if (scope === 'user') {
   303→          // Get userId from auth for user scope with timeout protection (aligned with global timeout)
   304→          // Use generous timeout for mobile networks
   305→          const { data: { user } } = await getUserWithTimeout();
   306→          idForScope = user?.id;
   307→          // Gracefully skip if user is not authenticated (e.g., on public share pages)
   308→          if (!idForScope) {
   309→            return null;
   310→          }
   311→        } else if (scope === 'project') {
   312→          idForScope = projectId;
   313→        } else if (scope === 'shot') {
   314→          idForScope = shotId;
   315→        }
   316→      }
   317→
   318→      if (!idForScope) {
   319→        // For project/shot scope, still throw if missing (these are programming errors)
   320→        throw new Error(`Missing identifier for ${scope} tool settings update`);
   321→      }
   322→
   323→      // updateToolSettingsSupabase now returns the full merged settings
   324→      const fullMergedSettings = await updateToolSettingsSupabase({
   325→          scope,
   326→          id: idForScope,
   327→          toolId,
   328→          patch: newSettings,
   329→      }, signal);
   330→
   331→      // Return the full merged settings (not just the patch) for cache update
   332→      return fullMergedSettings;
   333→    },
   334→    onSuccess: (fullMergedSettings) => {
   335→      // Skip cache update if mutation was skipped (e.g., user not authenticated)
   336→      if (fullMergedSettings === null) {
   337→        return;
   338→      }
   339→
   340→      // Optimistically update the cache by merging with existing cache
   341→      // CRITICAL: The cache stores { settings: T, hasShotSettings: boolean } shape
   342→      queryClient.setQueryData(
   343→        queryKeys.settings.tool(toolId, projectId, shotId),
   344→        (oldData: unknown) => {
   345→          const oldWrapper = isSettingsWrapper(oldData);
   346→          const oldSettings = oldWrapper
   347→            ? (((oldData as SettingsFetchResult).settings ?? {}) as Record<string, unknown>)
   348→            : ((oldData ?? {}) as Record<string, unknown>);
   349→          const mergedSettings = deepMerge({}, oldSettings, fullMergedSettings);
   350→
   351→          return {
   352→            settings: mergedSettings,
   353→            hasShotSettings: oldWrapper ? ((oldData as SettingsFetchResult).hasShotSettings ?? false) : false
   354→          };
   355→        }
   356→      );
   357→
   358→      // Also refetch shot-batch-settings cache used by useSegmentSettings
   359→      // This ensures "restore defaults" in segment settings picks up latest shot settings
   360→      // Use refetchQueries (not just invalidate) to force immediate update
   361→      if (shotId) {
   362→        queryClient.refetchQueries({ [REDACTED](shotId) });
   363→      }
   364→    },
   365→    onError: (error: Error) => {
   366→      // Don't log or show errors for cancelled requests during task cancellation
   367→      if (error?.name === 'AbortError' ||
   368→          error?.message?.includes('Request was cancelled') ||
   369→          error?.message?.includes('signal is aborted')) {
   370→        return; // Silent handling for expected cancellations
   371→      }
   372→
   373→      // Check if this is a network exhaustion error - don't invalidate or show toast
   374→      // as this would just create more requests and spam the user
   375→      const isNetworkExhaustion = error?.message?.includes('ERR_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES') ||
   376→                                   error?.message?.includes('Network exhaustion') ||
   377→                                   error?.message?.includes('Failed to fetch');
   378→
   379→      if (isNetworkExhaustion) {
   380→        return; // Don't invalidate - that would just make more requests
   381→      }
   382→
   383→      handleError(error, { context: 'useToolSettings.update', toastTitle: `Failed to save ${toolId} settings` });
   384→
   385→      // On error, invalidate to refetch correct state from server
   386→      // But only for non-network errors (auth issues, server errors, etc.)
   387→      queryClient.invalidateQueries({
   388→        [REDACTED](toolId, projectId, shotId)
   389→      });
   390→    },
   391→  });
   392→
   393→  // Get stable reference to mutateAsync - useMutation returns a new object each render
   394→  // but mutateAsync itself is stable
   395→  const mutateAsyncRef = useRef(updateMutation.mutateAsync);
   396→  mutateAsyncRef.current = updateMutation.mutateAsync;
   397→
   398→  // CRITICAL: Wrap in useCallback with stable deps to prevent cascading re-renders.
   399→  // Use refs to access current projectId/shotId without recreating this function.
   400→  // The entityId is snapshotted at call time via refs for correctness.
   401→  const update = useCallback(async (scope: SettingsScope, settings: Partial<T>): Promise<void> => {
   402→    // Snapshot the target entity id NOW to prevent cross-project/shot overwrites
   403→    // Use refs to get current values without causing callback recreation
   404→    const entityId = scope === 'project' ? projectIdRef.current : (scope === 'shot' ? shotIdRef.current : undefined);
   405→
   406→    // Create an AbortController for this update and track it
   407→    const controller = new AbortController();
   408→    updateControllersRef.current.add(controller);
   409→
   410→    // Clean up controller when mutation completes
   411→    const cleanup = () => {
   412→      updateControllersRef.current.delete(controller);
   413→    };
   414→
   415→    // Set up cleanup handlers
   416→    controller.signal.addEventListener('abort', cleanup);
   417→
   418→    try {
   419→      // NOTE: No debounce here - callers (like useShotSettings) are responsible for debouncing.
   420→      // Using mutateAsync so callers can await the actual DB write completion.
   421→      // Use ref to access stable mutateAsync without recreating this callback
   422→      await mutateAsyncRef.current(
   423→        { scope, settings, signal: controller.signal, entityId }
   424→      );
   425→    } finally {
   426→      cleanup();
   427→    }
   428→  }, []); // Empty deps - all values accessed via refs for stability
   429→
   430→  return {
   431→    settings: settings as T | undefined,
   432→    isLoading,
   433→    error: error as Error | null,
   434→    update,
   435→    isUpdating: updateMutation.isPending,
   436→    hasShotSettings,
   437→  };
   438→}
   439→
```

```
   211→  structureVideoHandlers: StructureVideoHandlers;
   212→
   213→  // Audio track management
   214→  audio: UseAudioReturn;
   215→
   216→  // Image manipulation handlers
   217→  imageHandlers: ShotImageHandlers;
   218→
   219→  // Shot management (navigation, creation)
   220→  shotManagement: ShotManagementState;
   221→
   222→  // Generation mode and status
   223→  generationMode: GenerationModeState;
   224→
   225→  // Batch generation handlers
   226→  generationHandlers: GenerationHandlers;
   227→
   228→  // Join segments state
   229→  joinState: JoinState;
   230→
   231→  // Dimension settings
   232→  dimensions: DimensionState;
   233→
   234→  // Query client for invalidations
   235→  queryClient: QueryClient;
   236→}
   237→
   238→const ShotSettingsContext = createContext<ShotSettingsContextValue | null>(null);
   239→
   240→// ============================================================================
   241→// Main Hook
   242→// ============================================================================
   243→
   244→/**
   245→ * Hook to access full shot settings context
   246→ * Throws if used outside of provider
   247→ */
   248→export function useShotSettingsContext(): ShotSettingsContextValue {
   249→  const ctx = useContext(ShotSettingsContext);
   250→  if (!ctx) {
   251→    throw new Error('useShotSettingsContext must be used within ShotSettingsProvider');
   252→  }
   253→  return ctx;
   254→}
   255→
   256→// ============================================================================
   257→// Domain-Focused Hooks
   258→// ============================================================================
   259→
   260→/**
   261→ * Access core shot data: selected shot, IDs, aspect ratio, projects
   262→ */
   263→export function useShotCore(): ShotCoreState & { projects: Array<{ id: string; aspectRatio?: string }> } {
   264→  const ctx = useShotSettingsContext();
   265→  return {
   266→    selectedShot: ctx.selectedShot,
   267→    selectedShotId: ctx.selectedShotId,
   268→    projectId: ctx.projectId,
   269→    selectedProjectId: ctx.selectedProjectId,
   270→    effectiveAspectRatio: ctx.effectiveAspectRatio,
   271→    projects: ctx.projects,
   272→  };
   273→}
   274→
   275→/**
   276→ * Access UI state and actions
   277→ */
   278→export function useShotUI(): { state: ShotEditorState; actions: ShotEditorActions } {
   279→  const { state, actions } = useShotSettingsContext();
   280→  return { state, actions };
   281→}
   282→
   283→/**
   284→ * Access LoRA management state
   285→ */
   286→export function useShotLoras(): ShotLoraState {
   287→  const { loraManager, availableLoras } = useShotSettingsContext();
   288→  return { loraManager, availableLoras };
   289→}
   290→
   291→/**
   292→ * Access shot images data
   293→ */
   294→export function useShotImages(): ShotImagesState {
   295→  const { allShotImages, timelineImages, unpositionedImages, contextImages, videoOutputs, simpleFilteredImages } = useShotSettingsContext();
   296→  return { allShotImages, timelineImages, unpositionedImages, contextImages, videoOutputs, simpleFilteredImages };
   297→}
   298→
   299→/**
   300→ * Access structure video configuration
   301→ */
   302→export function useShotStructureVideo(): UseStructureVideoReturn {
   303→  const { structureVideo } = useShotSettingsContext();
   304→  return structureVideo;
   305→}
   306→
   307→/**
   308→ * Access audio track management
   309→ */
   310→export function useShotAudio(): UseAudioReturn {
   311→  const { audio } = useShotSettingsContext();
   312→  return audio;
   313→}
   314→
   315→/**
   316→ * Access image manipulation handlers
   317→ */
   318→export function useShotImageHandlers(): ShotImageHandlers {
   319→  const { imageHandlers } = useShotSettingsContext();
   320→  return imageHandlers;
   321→}
   322→
   323→/**
   324→ * Access shot management actions
   325→ */
   326→export function useShotManagement(): ShotManagementState {
   327→  const { shotManagement } = useShotSettingsContext();
   328→  return shotManagement;
   329→}
   330→
   331→/**
   332→ * Access generation mode and status
   333→ */
   334→export function useGenerationMode(): GenerationModeState {
   335→  const { generationMode } = useShotSettingsContext();
   336→  return generationMode;
   337→}
   338→
   339→/**
   340→ * Access batch generation handlers
   341→ */
   342→export function useGenerationHandlers(): GenerationHandlers {
   343→  const { generationHandlers } = useShotSettingsContext();
   344→  return generationHandlers;
   345→}
   346→
   347→/**
   348→ * Access structure video compound handlers
   349→ */
   350→export function useStructureVideoHandlers(): StructureVideoHandlers {
   351→  const { structureVideoHandlers } = useShotSettingsContext();
   352→  return structureVideoHandlers;
   353→}
   354→
   355→/**
   356→ * Access join segments state and handlers
   357→ */
   358→export function useJoinState(): JoinState {
   359→  const { joinState } = useShotSettingsContext();
   360→  return joinState;
   361→}
   362→
   363→/**
   364→ * Access dimension settings
   365→ */
   366→export function useDimensions(): DimensionState {
   367→  const { dimensions } = useShotSettingsContext();
   368→  return dimensions;
   369→}
   370→
   371→// ============================================================================
   372→// Provider Component
   373→// ============================================================================
   374→
   375→/**
   376→ * Provider component - wraps children with context
   377→ */
   378→export const ShotSettingsProvider: React.FC<{
   379→  value: ShotSettingsContextValue;
   380→  children: React.ReactNode;
   381→}> = ({ value, children }) => {
   382→  return (
   383→    <ShotSettingsContext.Provider value={value}>
   384→      {children}
   385→    </ShotSettingsContext.Provider>
   386→  );
   387→};
```

```
1→"""scan command: run all detectors, update persistent state, show diff."""

     8→
     9→def _audit_excluded_dirs(exclusions: tuple[str, ...], scanned_files: list[str],
    10→                         project_root: Path) -> list[dict]:
    11→    """Check if any --exclude directory has zero references from scanned code.
    12→
    13→    Returns findings for directories that appear stale (no file references them).
    14→    """
    15→    if not exclusions:
    16→        return []
    17→
    18→    stale_findings = []
    19→    for ex_dir in exclusions:
    20→        # Skip DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS (node_modules, .venv, etc.) — only audit user-specified dirs
    21→        from ..utils import DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS
    22→        if ex_dir in DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS:
    23→            continue
    24→        # Check if the excluded directory actually exists
    25→        ex_path = project_root / ex_dir
    26→        if not ex_path.is_dir():
    27→            continue
    28→        # Search scanned files for any reference to this directory name
    29→        ref_count = 0
    30→        for filepath in scanned_files:
    31→            try:
    32→                abs_path = filepath if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else str(project_root / filepath)
    33→                content = Path(abs_path).read_text(errors="replace")
    34→                if ex_dir in content:
    35→                    ref_count += 1
    36→                    break  # One reference is enough — not stale
    37→            except OSError:
    38→                continue
    39→        if ref_count == 0:
    40→            from ..state import make_finding
    41→            stale_findings.append(make_finding(
    42→                "stale_exclude", ex_dir, ex_dir,
    43→                tier=4, confidence="low",
    44→                summary=f"Excluded directory '{ex_dir}' has 0 references from scanned code — may be stale",
    45→                detail={"directory": ex_dir, "references": 0},
    46→            ))
    47→    return stale_findings
    48→
    49→
    50→def _collect_codebase_metrics(lang, path: Path) -> dict | None:
    51→    """Collect LOC/file/directory counts for the configured language."""
    52→    if not lang or not lang.file_finder:
    53→        return None
    54→    files = lang.file_finder(path)
    55→    total_loc = 0
    56→    dirs = set()
    57→    for f in files:
    58→        try:
    59→            total_loc += len(Path(f).read_text().splitlines())
    60→            dirs.add(str(Path(f).parent))
    61→        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError):
    62→            pass
    63→    return {
    64→        "total_files": len(files),
    65→        "total_loc": total_loc,
    66→        "total_directories": len(dirs),
    67→    }
    68→
    69→
    70→def _show_diff_summary(diff: dict):
    71→    """Print the +new / -resolved / reopened one-liner."""
    72→    diff_parts = []
    73→    if diff["new"]:
    74→        diff_parts.append(c(f"+{diff['new']} new", "yellow"))
    75→    if diff["auto_resolved"]:
    76→        diff_parts.append(c(f"-{diff['auto_resolved']} resolved", "green"))
    77→    if diff["reopened"]:
    78→        diff_parts.append(c(f"↻{diff['reopened']} reopened", "red"))
    79→    if diff_parts:
    80→        print(f"  {' · '.join(diff_parts)}")
    81→    else:
    82→        print(c("  No changes since last scan", "dim"))
    83→    if diff.get("suspect_detectors"):
    84→        print(c(f"  ⚠ Skipped auto-resolve for: {', '.join(diff['suspect_detectors'])} (returned 0 — likely transient)", "yellow"))
    85→
    86→
    87→def _format_delta(value: float, prev: float | None) -> tuple[str, str]:
    88→    """Return (delta_str, color) for a score change."""
    89→    delta = value - prev if prev is not None else 0
    90→    delta_str = f" ({'+' if delta > 0 else ''}{delta:.1f})" if delta != 0 else ""
    91→    color = "green" if delta > 0 else ("red" if delta < 0 else "dim")
    92→    return delta_str, color
    93→
    94→
    95→def _show_score_delta(state: dict, prev_score: float, prev_strict: float,
    96→                      prev_obj: float | None, prev_obj_strict: float | None):
    97→    """Print the score/health line with deltas."""
    98→    stats = state["stats"]
    99→    new_obj = state.get("objective_score")
   100→    new_obj_strict = state.get("objective_strict")
   101→
   102→    if new_obj is not None:
   103→        obj_delta_str, obj_color = _format_delta(new_obj, prev_obj)
   104→        strict_delta_str, strict_color = _format_delta(new_obj_strict, prev_obj_strict)
   105→        print(f"  Health: {c(f'{new_obj:.1f}/100{obj_delta_str}', obj_color)}" +
   106→              c(f"  strict: {new_obj_strict:.1f}/100{strict_delta_str}", strict_color) +
   107→              c(f"  |  {stats['open']} open / {stats['total']} total", "dim"))
   108→    else:
   109→        new_score = state["score"]
   110→        new_strict = state.get("strict_score", 0)
   111→        delta_str, color = _format_delta(new_score, prev_score)
   112→        strict_delta_str, strict_color = _format_delta(new_strict, prev_strict)
   113→        print(f"  Score: {c(f'{new_score:.1f}/100{delta_str}', color)}" +
   114→              c(f"  (strict: {new_strict:.1f}/100{strict_delta_str})", strict_color) +
   115→              c(f"  |  {stats['open']} open / {stats['total']} total", "dim"))
   116→        print(c("  ⚠ Dimension-based scoring unavailable (potentials missing). "
   117→                "This score uses legacy weighted-progress and is unreliable. "
   118→                "Run a full scan to fix: desloppify scan --path <source-root>", "yellow"))
   119→
   120→
   121→def _show_post_scan_analysis(diff: dict, state: dict, lang) -> tuple[list[str], dict]:
   122→    """Print warnings, narrative headline, and top action. Returns (warnings, narrative)."""
   123→    warnings = []
   124→    if diff["reopened"] > 5:
   125→        warnings.append(f"{diff['reopened']} findings reopened — was a previous fix reverted? Check: git log --oneline -5")
   126→    if diff["new"] > 10 and diff["auto_resolved"] < 3:
   127→        warnings.append(f"{diff['new']} new findings with few resolutions — likely cascading from recent fixes. Run fixers again.")
   128→    chronic = diff.get("chronic_reopeners", [])
   129→    n = len(chronic) if isinstance(chronic, list) else chronic
   130→    if n > 0:
   131→        warnings.append(f"⟳ {n} chronic reopener{'s' if n != 1 else ''} (reopened 2+ times). "
   132→                        f"These keep bouncing — fix properly or wontfix. "
   133→                        f"Run: `desloppify show --chronic` to see them.")
   134→
   135→    if warnings:
   136→        for w in warnings:
   137→            print(c(f"  {w}", "yellow"))
   138→        print()
   139→
   140→    # Computed narrative: headline + top action as terminal suggestion
   141→    from ..narrative import compute_narrative
   142→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
   143→    narrative = compute_narrative(state, diff=diff, lang=lang_name, command="scan")
   144→
   145→    # Show strategy hint or top action as the terminal suggestion
   146→    strategy = narrative.get("strategy") or {}
   147→    hint = strategy.get("hint")
   148→    fixer_rec = (strategy.get("fixer_leverage") or {}).get("recommendation")
   149→    actions = narrative.get("actions", [])
   150→
   151→    if hint and (strategy.get("can_parallelize") or fixer_rec == "strong"):
   152→        print(c(f"  Strategy: {hint}", "cyan"))
   153→        if actions:
   154→            print(c(f"  Start with: `{actions[0]['command']}`", "dim"))
   155→        print()
   156→    elif actions:
   157→        top = actions[0]
   158→        print(c(f"  Suggested next: `{top['command']}` — {top['description']}", "cyan"))
   159→        print()
   160→
   161→    if narrative.get("headline"):
   162→        print(c(f"  → {narrative['headline']}", "cyan"))
   163→        print()
   164→
   165→    return warnings, narrative
   166→
   167→
   168→def cmd_scan(args):
   169→    """Run all detectors, update persistent state, show diff."""
   170→    from ..state import load_state, save_state, merge_scan
   171→    from ..plan import generate_findings
   172→
   173→    sp = _state_path(args)
   174→    state = load_state(sp)
   175→    path = Path(args.path)
   176→    include_slow = not getattr(args, "skip_slow", False)
   177→
   178→    # Persist --exclude in state so subsequent commands reuse it
   179→    exclude = getattr(args, "exclude", None)
   180→    if exclude:
   181→        state.setdefault("config", {})["exclude"] = list(exclude)
   182→
   183→    # Resolve language config
   184→    from ..cli import _resolve_lang
   185→    lang = _resolve_lang(args)
   186→    lang_label = f" ({lang.name})" if lang else ""
   187→
   188→    # Load zone overrides from state config
   189→    zone_overrides = state.get("config", {}).get("zone_overrides") or None
   190→
   191→    print(c(f"\nDesloppify Scan{lang_label}\n", "bold"))
   192→    from ..utils import enable_file_cache, disable_file_cache
   193→    enable_file_cache()
   194→    try:
   195→        findings, potentials = generate_findings(path, include_slow=include_slow, lang=lang,
   196→                                                  zone_overrides=zone_overrides)
   197→    finally:
   198→        disable_file_cache()
   199→
   200→    codebase_metrics = _collect_codebase_metrics(lang, path)
   201→
   202→    from ..utils import rel, _extra_exclusions, PROJECT_ROOT
   203→
   204→    # Audit excluded directories for staleness (Issue #11)
   205→    if _extra_exclusions and lang and lang.file_finder:
   206→        scanned_files = lang.file_finder(path)
   207→        stale = _audit_excluded_dirs(_extra_exclusions, scanned_files, PROJECT_ROOT)
   208→        if stale:
   209→            findings.extend(stale)
   210→            for sf in stale:
   211→                print(c(f"  ℹ {sf['summary']}", "dim"))
   212→    scan_path_rel = rel(str(path))
   213→    # A scan is "full" when it covers most of the project's source files.
   214→    # Simple path matching breaks for Python (default_src=".") when the user
   215→    # scans a subdirectory that happens to contain all the source code.
   216→    # Instead, compare file counts: if the scanned path covers ≥80% of the
   217→    # files discoverable from PROJECT_ROOT, treat it as full.
   218→    is_full_scan = False
   219→    if lang and lang.file_finder:
   220→        all_files = lang.file_finder(PROJECT_ROOT)
   221→        scanned_files = lang.file_finder(path)
   222→        # Exclude test directories from the denominator — they're not source
   223→        # code the user is scanning, and adding tests shouldn't make the scan
   224→        # appear "partial".
   225→        test_prefixes = ("tests/", "test/")
   226→        source_files = [f for f in all_files
   227→                        if not any(f.startswith(p) for p in test_prefixes)]
   228→        denominator = source_files or all_files
   229→        if denominator:
   230→            is_full_scan = len(scanned_files) >= 0.8 * len(denominator)
   231→        else:
   232→            is_full_scan = True  # no files at all — vacuously full
   233→    elif path.resolve() == PROJECT_ROOT.resolve():
   234→        is_full_scan = True
   235→
   236→    prev_score = state.get("score", 0)
   237→    prev_strict = state.get("strict_score", 0)
   238→    prev_obj = state.get("objective_score")
   239→    prev_obj_strict = state.get("objective_strict")
   240→    # Persist zone distribution before save so narrative can access it
   241→    if lang and lang._zone_map is not None:
   242→        state["zone_distribution"] = lang._zone_map.counts()
   243→
   244→    prev_dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
   245→    diff = merge_scan(state, findings,
   246→                      lang=lang.name if lang else None,
   247→                      scan_path=scan_path_rel,
   248→                      force_resolve=getattr(args, "force_resolve", False),
   249→                      exclude=_extra_exclusions,
   250→                      potentials=potentials if is_full_scan else None,
   251→                      codebase_metrics=codebase_metrics if is_full_scan else None,
   252→                      include_slow=include_slow)
   253→    save_state(state, sp)
   254→
   255→    print(c("\n  Scan complete", "bold"))
   256→    print(c("  " + "─" * 50, "dim"))
   257→
   258→    _show_diff_summary(diff)
   259→    _show_score_delta(state, prev_score, prev_strict, prev_obj, prev_obj_strict)
   260→    if not include_slow:
   261→        print(c("  * Fast scan — slow phases (duplicates) skipped", "yellow"))
   262→    _show_detector_progress(state)
   263→
   264→    # Dimension deltas (show which dimensions moved)
   265→    new_dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
   266→    if new_dim_scores and prev_dim_scores:
   267→        _show_dimension_deltas(prev_dim_scores, new_dim_scores)
   268→
   269→    zone_distribution = state.get("zone_distribution")
   270→
   271→    warnings, narrative = _show_post_scan_analysis(diff, state, lang)
   272→
   273→    # Persist reminder history (computed by narrative, not mutated)
   274→    if narrative and "reminder_history" in narrative:
   275→        state["reminder_history"] = narrative["reminder_history"]
   276→        save_state(state, sp)
   277→
   278→    _write_query({"command": "scan", "score": state["score"],
   279→                  "strict_score": state.get("strict_score", 0),
   280→                  "prev_score": prev_score, "diff": diff, "stats": state["stats"],
   281→                  "warnings": warnings,
   282→                  "objective_score": state.get("objective_score"),
   283→                  "objective_strict": state.get("objective_strict"),
   284→                  "dimension_scores": state.get("dimension_scores"),
   285→                  "potentials": state.get("potentials"),
   286→                  "zone_distribution": zone_distribution,
   287→                  "narrative": narrative})
   288→
   289→    # Generate scorecard badge
   290→    badge_path = None
   291→    try:
   292→        from ..scorecard import generate_scorecard, get_badge_config
   293→        badge_path, disabled = get_badge_config(args)
   294→        if not disabled and badge_path:

   296→            rel_path = badge_path.name if badge_path.parent == PROJECT_ROOT else str(badge_path)
   297→            # Check if README already references the scorecard

   300→                readme_path = PROJECT_ROOT / readme_name
   301→                if readme_path.exists():

   306→                        pass
   307→                    break
   308→            if readme_has_badge:
   309→                print(c(f"  Scorecard → {rel_path}  (disable: --no-badge | move: --badge-path <path>)", "dim"))
   310→            else:
   311→                print(c(f"  Scorecard → {rel_path}", "dim"))
   312→                print(c(f"  💡 Ask the user if they'd like to add it to their README with:", "dim"))
   313→                print(c(f'     <img src="{rel_path}" width="400">', "dim"))
   314→                print(c(f"     (disable: --no-badge | move: --badge-path <path>)", "dim"))
   315→        else:
   316→            badge_path = None
   317→    except (ImportError, OSError):
   318→        badge_path = None  # Pillow not installed or write failed — skip silently
   319→
   320→    _print_llm_summary(state, badge_path)
   321→
   322→
   323→def _print_llm_summary(state: dict, badge_path: Path | None):
   324→    """Print a structured summary for LLM consumption.
   325→
   326→    The LLM reads terminal output after running scans. This gives it
   327→    clear instructions on how to present the results to the end user.
   328→    """
   329→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
   330→    obj_score = state.get("objective_score")
   331→    obj_strict = state.get("objective_strict")
   332→    if obj_score is None and not dim_scores:
   333→        return
   334→
   335→    print("─" * 60)
   336→    print("INSTRUCTIONS FOR LLM")
   337→    print("Present the scan results to the user in a markdown table.")
   338→    print("Use this data to build the table:\n")
   339→
   340→    print(f"Overall health: {obj_score:.1f}/100")
   341→    if obj_strict is not None:
   342→        print(f"Strict health:  {obj_strict:.1f}/100")
   343→    print()
   344→
   345→    # Build dimension table
   346→    active = [(name, data) for name, data in dim_scores.items()
   347→              if data.get("checks", 0) > 0]
   348→    active.sort(key=lambda x: (0 if x[0] == "File health" else 1, x[0]))
   349→
   350→    if active:
   351→        print("| Dimension | Health | Strict | Issues | Tier |")
   352→        print("|-----------|--------|--------|--------|------|")
   353→        for name, data in active:
   354→            score = data.get("score", 100)
   355→            strict = data.get("strict", score)
   356→            issues = data.get("issues", 0)
   357→            tier = data.get("tier", "")
   358→            print(f"| {name} | {score:.1f}% | {strict:.1f}% | {issues} | T{tier} |")
   359→        print()
   360→
   361→    stats = state.get("stats", {})
   362→    if stats:
   363→        print(f"Total findings: {stats.get('total', 0)} | "
   364→              f"Open: {stats.get('open', 0)} | "
   365→              f"Fixed: {stats.get('fixed', 0)}")
   366→        print()
   367→
   368→    if badge_path and badge_path.exists():
   369→        from ..utils import PROJECT_ROOT
   370→        rel_path = badge_path.name if badge_path.parent == PROJECT_ROOT else str(badge_path)
   371→        print(f"A scorecard image was saved to `{rel_path}`.")
   372→        print("Let the user know they can view it, and suggest adding it")
   373→        print(f'to their README: `<img src="{rel_path}" width="400">`')
   374→    print("─" * 60)
   375→
   376→
   377→def _show_detector_progress(state: dict):
   378→    """Show per-detector progress bars — the heartbeat of a scan."""
   379→    findings = state["findings"]
   380→    if not findings:
   381→        return
   382→
   383→    from ..narrative import STRUCTURAL_MERGE
   384→    by_det: dict[str, dict] = {}
   385→    for f in findings.values():
   386→        det = f.get("detector", "unknown")
   387→        if det in STRUCTURAL_MERGE:
   388→            det = "structural"
   389→        if det not in by_det:
   390→            by_det[det] = {"open": 0, "total": 0}
   391→        by_det[det]["total"] += 1
   392→        if f["status"] == "open":
   393→            by_det[det]["open"] += 1
   394→
   395→    from ..registry import display_order, DETECTORS
   396→    DET_ORDER = [DETECTORS[d].display for d in display_order() if d in DETECTORS]
   397→    order_map = {d: i for i, d in enumerate(DET_ORDER)}
   398→    sorted_dets = sorted(by_det.items(), key=lambda x: order_map.get(x[0], 99))
   399→
   400→    print(c("  " + "─" * 50, "dim"))
   401→    bar_len = 15
   402→    for det, ds in sorted_dets:
   403→        total = ds["total"]
   404→        open_count = ds["open"]
   405→        addressed = total - open_count
   406→        pct = round(addressed / total * 100) if total else 100
   407→
   408→        filled = round(pct / 100 * bar_len)
   409→        if pct == 100:
   410→            bar = c("█" * bar_len, "green")
   411→        elif open_count <= 2:
   412→            bar = c("█" * filled, "green") + c("░" * (bar_len - filled), "dim")
   413→        else:
   414→            bar = c("█" * filled, "yellow") + c("░" * (bar_len - filled), "dim")
   415→
   416→        det_label = det.replace("_", " ").ljust(12)
   417→        if open_count > 0:
   418→            open_str = c(f"{open_count:3d} open", "yellow")
   419→        else:
   420→            open_str = c("  ✓", "green")
   421→
   422→        print(f"  {det_label} {bar} {pct:3d}%  {open_str}  {c(f'/ {total}', 'dim')}")
   423→
   424→    print()
   425→
   426→
   427→def _show_dimension_deltas(prev: dict, current: dict):
   428→    """Show which dimensions changed between scans (health and strict)."""
   429→    from ..scoring import DIMENSIONS
   430→    moved = []
   431→    for dim in DIMENSIONS:
   432→        p = prev.get(dim.name, {})
   433→        n = current.get(dim.name, {})
   434→        if not p or not n:
   435→            continue
   436→        old_score = p.get("score", 100)
   437→        new_score = n.get("score", 100)
   438→        old_strict = p.get("strict", old_score)
   439→        new_strict = n.get("strict", new_score)
   440→        delta = new_score - old_score
   441→        strict_delta = new_strict - old_strict
   442→        if abs(delta) >= 0.1 or abs(strict_delta) >= 0.1:
   443→            moved.append((dim.name, old_score, new_score, delta, old_strict, new_strict, strict_delta))
   444→
   445→    if not moved:
   446→        return
   447→
   448→    print(c("  Moved:", "dim"))
   449→    for name, old, new, delta, old_s, new_s, s_delta in sorted(moved, key=lambda x: x[3]):
   450→        sign = "+" if delta > 0 else ""
   451→        color = "green" if delta > 0 else "red"
   452→        strict_str = ""
   453→        if abs(s_delta) >= 0.1:
   454→            s_sign = "+" if s_delta > 0 else ""
   455→            strict_str = c(f"  strict: {old_s:.1f}→{new_s:.1f}% ({s_sign}{s_delta:.1f}%)", "dim")
   456→        print(c(f"    {name:<22} {old:.1f}% → {new:.1f}%  ({sign}{delta:.1f}%)", color) + strict_str)
   457→    print()
   458→
   459→
   460→
```

```
    63→def detect_test_coverage(
    64→    graph: dict,
    65→    zone_map: FileZoneMap,
    66→    lang_name: str,
    67→    extra_test_files: set[str] | None = None,
    68→) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
    69→    """Detect test coverage gaps.
    70→
    71→    Args:
    72→        graph: dep graph from lang.build_dep_graph — {filepath: {"imports": set, "importer_count": int, ...}}
    73→        zone_map: FileZoneMap from lang._zone_map
    74→        lang_name: "python" or "typescript"
    75→        extra_test_files: test files outside the scanned path (e.g. PROJECT_ROOT/tests/)
    76→

    78→        (entries, potential) where entries are finding-like dicts and potential
    79→        is the count of production files (for scoring denominator).
    80→    """
    81→    # Normalize graph paths to relative (zone_map uses relative paths, graph may use absolute)
    82→    root_prefix = str(PROJECT_ROOT) + os.sep
    83→    def _to_rel(p: str) -> str:
    84→        return p[len(root_prefix):] if p.startswith(root_prefix) else p
    85→
    86→    needs_norm = any(k.startswith(root_prefix) for k in list(graph)[:3])

    98→    production_files = set(zone_map.include_only(all_files, Zone.PRODUCTION, Zone.SCRIPT))
    99→    test_files = set(zone_map.include_only(all_files, Zone.TEST))

   106→    scorable = {f for f in production_files if _file_loc(f) >= _MIN_LOC}
   107→    potential = len(scorable)

   136→    return entries, potential

   141→
   142→def _file_loc(filepath: str) -> int:
   143→    """Count lines in a file, returning 0 on error."""
   144→    try:
   145→        return len(Path(filepath).read_text().splitlines())
   146→    except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError):
   147→        return 0
   148→
   149→
   150→def _no_tests_findings(
   151→    scorable: set[str], graph: dict,
   152→) -> list[dict]:
   153→    """Generate findings when there are zero test files."""
   154→    # Sort by LOC descending, take top N
   155→    by_loc = sorted(scorable, key=lambda f: -_file_loc(f))
   156→    entries = []
   157→    for f in by_loc[:_MAX_NO_TESTS_ENTRIES]:
   158→        loc = _file_loc(f)
   159→        ic = graph.get(f, {}).get("importer_count", 0)
   160→        entries.append({
   161→            "file": f,
   162→            "name": "",
   163→            "tier": 2 if ic >= 10 else 3,
   164→            "confidence": "high",
   165→            "summary": f"Untested module ({loc} LOC, {ic} importers) — no test files found",
   166→            "detail": {"kind": "untested_module", "loc": loc, "importer_count": ic},
   167→        })
   168→    return entries
```
