```
1→"""Zone classification system — deterministic file intent classification.
     2→
     3→Classifies files into zones (production, test, config, generated, script, vendor)
     4→based on path patterns. Zone metadata flows through findings, scoring, and the LLM.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import os
    10→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    11→from enum import Enum
    12→
    13→
    14→class Zone(str, Enum):
    15→    """File intent zone — determines scoring and detection policy."""
    16→    PRODUCTION = "production"
    17→    TEST = "test"
    18→    CONFIG = "config"
    19→    GENERATED = "generated"
    20→    SCRIPT = "script"
    21→    VENDOR = "vendor"
    22→
    23→
    24→# Zones excluded from the health score
    25→EXCLUDED_ZONES = {Zone.TEST, Zone.CONFIG, Zone.GENERATED, Zone.VENDOR}
    26→
    27→# String values for quick lookup in scoring (findings store zone as string)
    28→EXCLUDED_ZONE_VALUES = {z.value for z in EXCLUDED_ZONES}
    29→
    30→
    31→@dataclass
    32→class ZoneRule:
    33→    """A classification rule: zone + list of path patterns.
    34→
    35→    Patterns are matched against relative file paths. First matching rule wins.
    36→    Pattern types (auto-detected from shape):
    37→      - "/dir/"   → substring match on full path (directory marker)
    38→      - ".ext"    → basename ends-with (suffix/extension, e.g. ".d.ts", ".test.")
    39→      - "prefix_" → basename starts-with (trailing underscore)
    40→      - "name.py" → basename exact match (has extension, no /)
    41→      - fallback  → substring on full path
    42→    """
    43→    zone: Zone
    44→    patterns: list[str]
    45→
    46→
    47→def _match_pattern(rel_path: str, pattern: str) -> bool:
    48→    """Match a zone pattern against a relative file path.
    49→
    50→    See ZoneRule docstring for pattern type conventions.
    51→    """
    52→    basename = os.path.basename(rel_path)
    53→
    54→    # Directory pattern: "/dir/" → substring on padded path
    55→    if pattern.startswith("/") and pattern.endswith("/"):
    56→        return pattern in ("/" + rel_path + "/")
    57→
    58→    # Suffix/extension pattern: starts with "." → contains on basename
    59→    if pattern.startswith("."):
    60→        return pattern in basename
    61→
    62→    # Prefix pattern: ends with "_" → basename starts-with
    63→    if pattern.endswith("_"):
    64→        return basename.startswith(pattern)
    65→
    66→    # Suffix pattern: starts with "_" → basename ends-with (_test.py, _pb2.py)
    67→    if pattern.startswith("_"):
    68→        return basename.endswith(pattern)
    69→
    70→    # Exact basename: has a proper file extension (1-5 chars after last dot),
    71→    # no "/" → exact basename match (config.py, setup.py, conftest.py)
    72→    if "/" not in pattern and "." in pattern:
    73→        ext = pattern.rsplit(".", 1)[-1]
    74→        if ext and len(ext) <= 5 and ext.isalnum():
    75→            return basename == pattern
    76→
    77→    # Fallback: substring on full path (vite.config, tsconfig, eslint, etc.)
    78→    return pattern in rel_path
    79→
    80→
    81→# ── Common zone rules (shared across languages) ──────────
    82→
    83→COMMON_ZONE_RULES = [
    84→    ZoneRule(Zone.VENDOR, ["/vendor/", "/third_party/", "/vendored/"]),
    85→    ZoneRule(Zone.GENERATED, ["/generated/", "/__generated__/"]),
    86→    ZoneRule(Zone.TEST, ["/tests/", "/test/", "/fixtures/"]),
    87→    ZoneRule(Zone.SCRIPT, ["/scripts/", "/bin/"]),
    88→]
    89→
    90→
    91→def classify_file(rel_path: str, rules: list[ZoneRule],
    92→                  overrides: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> Zone:
    93→    """Classify a file by its relative path. Overrides take priority."""
    94→    if overrides:
    95→        override = overrides.get(rel_path)
    96→        if override:
    97→            try:
    98→                return Zone(override)
    99→            except ValueError:
   100→                pass  # Invalid zone value — fall through to rules
   101→    for rule in rules:
   102→        for pattern in rule.patterns:
   103→            if _match_pattern(rel_path, pattern):
   104→                return rule.zone
   105→    return Zone.PRODUCTION
   106→
   107→
   108→class FileZoneMap:
   109→    """Cached zone classification for a set of files.
   110→
   111→    Built once per scan from file list + zone rules.
   112→    """
   113→
   114→    def __init__(self, files: list[str], rules: list[ZoneRule],
   115→                 rel_fn=None, overrides: dict[str, str] | None = None):
   116→        """Build zone map.
   117→
   118→        Args:
   119→            files: List of file paths (absolute or relative).
   120→            rules: Ordered zone rules (first match wins).
   121→            rel_fn: Optional function to convert paths to relative.
   122→            overrides: Manual zone overrides {rel_path: zone_value}.
   123→        """
   124→        self._map: dict[str, Zone] = {}
   125→        self._overrides = overrides
   126→        for f in files:
   127→            rp = rel_fn(f) if rel_fn else f
   128→            self._map[f] = classify_file(rp, rules, overrides)
   129→
   130→    def get(self, path: str) -> Zone:
   131→        """Get zone for a file path. Returns PRODUCTION if not classified."""
   132→        return self._map.get(path, Zone.PRODUCTION)
   133→
   134→    def exclude(self, files: list[str], *zones: Zone) -> list[str]:
   135→        """Return files NOT in the given zones."""
   136→        zone_set = set(zones)
   137→        return [f for f in files if self._map.get(f, Zone.PRODUCTION) not in zone_set]
   138→
   139→    def include_only(self, files: list[str], *zones: Zone) -> list[str]:
   140→        """Return files that ARE in the given zones."""
   141→        zone_set = set(zones)
   142→        return [f for f in files if self._map.get(f, Zone.PRODUCTION) in zone_set]
   143→
   144→    def counts(self) -> dict[str, int]:
   145→        """Return file count per zone."""
   146→        counts: dict[str, int] = {}
   147→        for zone in self._map.values():
   148→            counts[zone.value] = counts.get(zone.value, 0) + 1
   149→        return counts
   150→
   151→    def production_count(self) -> int:
   152→        """Count files classified as production."""
   153→        return len(self._map) - self.non_production_count()
   154→
   155→    def non_production_count(self) -> int:
   156→        """Count files in excluded zones (test/config/generated/vendor)."""
   157→        return sum(1 for z in self._map.values() if z in EXCLUDED_ZONES)
   158→
   159→    def all_files(self) -> list[str]:
   160→        """Return all classified file paths."""
   161→        return list(self._map.keys())
   162→
   163→    def items(self) -> list[tuple[str, Zone]]:
   164→        """Return all (path, zone) pairs."""
   165→        return list(self._map.items())
   166→
   167→
   168→# ── Zone detection policies ────────────────────────────────
   169→
   170→@dataclass
   171→class ZonePolicy:
   172→    """Per-zone detection policy.
   173→
   174→    skip_detectors: detectors to skip entirely for this zone.
   175→    downgrade_detectors: detectors whose confidence is downgraded to "low".
   176→    exclude_from_score: whether findings in this zone are excluded from scoring.
   177→    """
   178→    skip_detectors: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
   179→    downgrade_detectors: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
   180→    exclude_from_score: bool = False
   181→
   182→
   183→ZONE_POLICIES: dict[Zone, ZonePolicy] = {
   184→    Zone.PRODUCTION: ZonePolicy(),
   185→    Zone.TEST: ZonePolicy(
   186→        skip_detectors={"dupes", "single_use", "orphaned", "coupling", "facade",
   187→                        "dict_keys", "test_coverage"},
   188→        downgrade_detectors={"smells", "structural"},
   189→        exclude_from_score=True,
   190→    ),
   191→    Zone.CONFIG: ZonePolicy(
   192→        skip_detectors={"smells", "structural", "dupes", "naming",
   193→                        "single_use", "orphaned", "coupling", "facade",
   194→                        "dict_keys", "test_coverage"},
   195→        exclude_from_score=True,
   196→    ),
   197→    Zone.GENERATED: ZonePolicy(
   198→        skip_detectors={"unused", "logs", "exports", "deprecated", "structural",
   199→                        "props", "smells", "react", "dupes", "single_use",
   200→                        "orphaned", "coupling", "facade", "naming", "patterns",
   201→                        "cycles", "flat_dirs", "dict_keys", "test_coverage",
   202→                        "security"},
   203→        exclude_from_score=True,
   204→    ),
   205→    Zone.VENDOR: ZonePolicy(
   206→        skip_detectors={"unused", "logs", "exports", "deprecated", "structural",
   207→                        "props", "smells", "react", "dupes", "single_use",
   208→                        "orphaned", "coupling", "facade", "naming", "patterns",
   209→                        "cycles", "flat_dirs", "dict_keys", "test_coverage",
   210→                        "security"},
   211→        exclude_from_score=True,
   212→    ),
   213→    Zone.SCRIPT: ZonePolicy(
   214→        skip_detectors={"coupling", "single_use", "orphaned", "facade"},
   215→        downgrade_detectors={"structural"},
   216→    ),
   217→}
   218→
   219→
   220→# ── Helpers for phase runners ─────────────────────────────
   221→
   222→def adjust_potential(zone_map, total: int) -> int:
   223→    """Subtract non-production files from a potential count.
   224→
   225→    Uses the zone map's own file list — no need to pass files separately.
   226→    No-op if zone_map is None (backward compat).
   227→    """
   228→    if zone_map is None:
   229→        return total
   230→    return max(total - zone_map.non_production_count(), 0)
   231→
   232→
   233→def should_skip_finding(zone_map, filepath: str, detector: str) -> bool:
   234→    """Check if a finding should be skipped based on zone policy.
   235→
   236→    Returns True if the file's zone policy says to skip this detector.
   237→    """
   238→    if zone_map is None:
   239→        return False
   240→    zone = zone_map.get(filepath)
   241→    policy = ZONE_POLICIES.get(zone)
   242→    return policy is not None and detector in policy.skip_detectors
   243→
   244→
   245→def filter_entries(zone_map, entries: list[dict], detector: str,
   246→                   file_key: str = "file") -> list[dict]:
   247→    """Filter detector entries by zone policy. No-op if zone_map is None.
   248→
   249→    If file_key points to a list (e.g. cycle entries with "files"), checks
   250→    the first element.
   251→    """
   252→    if zone_map is None:
   253→        return entries
   254→
   255→    def _get_path(entry):
   256→        val = entry[file_key]
   257→        return val[0] if isinstance(val, list) else val
   258→
   259→    return [e for e in entries
   260→            if not should_skip_finding(zone_map, _get_path(e), detector)]
   261→
```

```
1→"""Persistent state management for desloppify findings (.desloppify/state.json)."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import fnmatch
     6→import json
     7→import os
     8→import sys
     9→import tempfile
    10→from datetime import datetime, timezone
    11→from pathlib import Path
    12→from typing import NotRequired, TypedDict
    13→
    14→from .scoring import TIER_WEIGHTS
    15→from .utils import PROJECT_ROOT, rel, matches_exclusion
    16→
    17→
    18→class Finding(TypedDict):
    19→    """The central data structure — a normalized finding from any detector."""
    20→    id: str
    21→    detector: str
    22→    file: str
    23→    tier: int              # 1-4
    24→    confidence: str        # "high" | "medium" | "low"
    25→    summary: str
    26→    detail: dict
    27→    status: str            # "open" | "resolved" | "wontfix" | "false_positive"
    28→    note: str | None
    29→    first_seen: str
    30→    last_seen: str
    31→    resolved_at: str | None
    32→    reopen_count: int
    33→    # Stamped post-creation in plan.py:
    34→    lang: NotRequired[str]
    35→    zone: NotRequired[str]
    36→
    37→STATE_DIR = PROJECT_ROOT / ".desloppify"
    38→STATE_FILE = STATE_DIR / "state.json"
    39→
    40→CURRENT_VERSION = 1
    41→
    42→
    43→def _now() -> str:
    44→    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
    45→
    46→
    47→def _empty_state() -> dict:
    48→    return {
    49→        "version": CURRENT_VERSION,
    50→        "created": _now(),
    51→        "last_scan": None,
    52→        "scan_count": 0,
    53→        "config": {"ignore": []},
    54→        "score": 0,
    55→        "stats": {},
    56→        "findings": {},
    57→    }
    58→
    59→
    60→def load_state(path: Path | None = None) -> dict:
    61→    """Load state from disk, or return empty state.
    62→
    63→    Handles corruption gracefully: tries backup, then starts fresh.
    64→    """
    65→    p = path or STATE_FILE
    66→    if not p.exists():
    67→        return _empty_state()
    68→
    69→    try:
    70→        data = json.loads(p.read_text())
    71→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as e:
    72→        # Try backup
    73→        backup = p.with_suffix(".json.bak")
    74→        if backup.exists():
    75→            try:
    76→                data = json.loads(backup.read_text())
    77→                print(f"  \u26a0 State file corrupted ({e}), loaded from backup.", file=sys.stderr)
    78→                return data
    79→            except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError):
    80→                pass
```

```
1→"""Persistent state management for desloppify findings (.desloppify/state.json)."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import fnmatch
     6→import json
     7→import os
     8→import sys
     9→import tempfile
    10→from datetime import datetime, timezone
    11→from pathlib import Path
    12→from typing import NotRequired, TypedDict
    13→
    14→from .scoring import TIER_WEIGHTS
    15→from .utils import PROJECT_ROOT, rel, matches_exclusion
    16→
    17→
    18→class Finding(TypedDict):
    19→    """The central data structure — a normalized finding from any detector."""
    20→    id: str
    21→    detector: str
    22→    file: str
    23→    tier: int              # 1-4
    24→    confidence: str        # "high" | "medium" | "low"
    25→    summary: str
    26→    detail: dict
    27→    status: str            # "open" | "resolved" | "wontfix" | "false_positive"
    28→    note: str | None
    29→    first_seen: str
    30→    last_seen: str
    31→    resolved_at: str | None
    32→    reopen_count: int
    33→    # Stamped post-creation in plan.py:
    34→    lang: NotRequired[str]
    35→    zone: NotRequired[str]
    36→
    37→STATE_DIR = PROJECT_ROOT / ".desloppify"
    38→STATE_FILE = STATE_DIR / "state.json"
    39→
    40→CURRENT_VERSION = 1
    41→
    42→
    43→def _now() -> str:
    44→    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
    45→
    46→
    47→def _empty_state() -> dict:
    48→    return {
    49→        "version": CURRENT_VERSION,
    50→        "created": _now(),
    51→        "last_scan": None,
    52→        "scan_count": 0,
    53→        "config": {"ignore": []},
    54→        "score": 0,
    55→        "stats": {},
    56→        "findings": {},
    57→    }
    58→
    59→
    60→def load_state(path: Path | None = None) -> dict:
    61→    """Load state from disk, or return empty state.
    62→
    63→    Handles corruption gracefully: tries backup, then starts fresh.
    64→    """
    65→    p = path or STATE_FILE
    66→    if not p.exists():
    67→        return _empty_state()
    68→
    69→    try:
    70→        data = json.loads(p.read_text())
    71→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as e:
    72→        # Try backup
    73→        backup = p.with_suffix(".json.bak")
    74→        if backup.exists():
    75→            try:
    76→                data = json.loads(backup.read_text())
    77→                print(f"  \u26a0 State file corrupted ({e}), loaded from backup.", file=sys.stderr)
    78→                return data
    79→            except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError):
    80→                pass
    81→        print(f"  \u26a0 State file corrupted ({e}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    82→        try:
    83→            p.rename(p.with_suffix(".json.corrupted"))
    84→        except OSError:
    85→            pass
    86→        return _empty_state()
    87→
    88→    # Version check
    89→    version = data.get("version", 1)
    90→    if version > CURRENT_VERSION:
    91→        print(f"  \u26a0 State file version {version} is newer than supported ({CURRENT_VERSION}). "
    92→              f"Some features may not work correctly.", file=sys.stderr)
    93→    return data
    94→
    95→
    96→def _json_default(obj):
    97→    """JSON serializer that handles known types and rejects unknowns."""
    98→    if isinstance(obj, set):
    99→        return sorted(obj)
   100→    if isinstance(obj, Path):
   101→        return str(obj)
   102→    if hasattr(obj, "isoformat"):
   103→        return obj.isoformat()
   104→    raise TypeError(f"Object of type {type(obj).__name__} is not JSON serializable: {obj!r}")
   105→
   106→
   107→def save_state(state: dict, path: Path | None = None):
   108→    """Recompute stats/score and save to disk atomically."""
   109→    _recompute_stats(state)
   110→    p = path or STATE_FILE
   111→    p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   112→
   113→    content = json.dumps(state, indent=2, default=_json_default) + "\n"
   114→
   115→    # Atomic write: temp file + rename
   116→    try:
   117→        fd, tmp_path = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=str(p.parent), suffix=".tmp")
   118→        try:
   119→            os.write(fd, content.encode())
   120→            os.fsync(fd)
   121→        finally:
   122→            os.close(fd)
   123→
   124→        # Keep a backup of the previous state
   125→        if p.exists():
   126→            backup = p.with_suffix(".json.bak")
   127→            try:
   128→                import shutil
   129→                shutil.copy2(str(p), str(backup))
   130→            except OSError:
   131→                pass
   132→
   133→        os.replace(tmp_path, str(p))
   134→    except OSError:
   135→        # Fallback to direct write if atomic write fails
   136→        try:
   137→            os.unlink(tmp_path)
   138→        except (OSError, UnboundLocalError):
   139→            pass
   140→        try:
   141→            p.write_text(content)
   142→        except OSError as e:
   143→            import sys
   144→            print(f"  \u26a0 Could not save state: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
   145→
   146→
   147→
   148→_EMPTY_COUNTERS = ("open", "fixed", "auto_resolved", "wontfix", "false_positive")
   149→
   150→
   151→def _count_findings(findings: dict) -> tuple[dict[str, int], dict[int, dict[str, int]]]:
   152→    """Tally per-status counters and per-tier breakdowns."""
   153→    counters = dict.fromkeys(_EMPTY_COUNTERS, 0)
   154→    tier_stats: dict[int, dict[str, int]] = {}
   155→    for f in findings.values():
   156→        s, tier = f["status"], f.get("tier", 3)
   157→        counters[s] = counters.get(s, 0) + 1
   158→        ts = tier_stats.setdefault(tier, dict.fromkeys(_EMPTY_COUNTERS, 0))
   159→        ts[s] = ts.get(s, 0) + 1
   160→    return counters, tier_stats
   161→
   162→
   163→def _weighted_progress(findings: dict) -> tuple[float, float]:
   164→    """Compute weighted addressed% and strict-fixed%. Returns (score, strict_score)."""
   165→    total_w = addressed_w = fixed_w = 0
   166→    for f in findings.values():
   167→        w = TIER_WEIGHTS.get(f.get("tier", 3), 2)
   168→        total_w += w
   169→        if f["status"] != "open":
   170→            addressed_w += w
   171→        if f["status"] in ("fixed", "auto_resolved", "false_positive"):
   172→            fixed_w += w
   173→    if total_w == 0:
   174→        return 100.0, 100.0
   175→    return round((addressed_w / total_w) * 100, 1), round((fixed_w / total_w) * 100, 1)
   176→
   177→
   178→def _update_objective_health(state: dict, findings: dict):
   179→    """Compute dimension-based objective health scores from potentials."""
   180→    pots = state.get("potentials", {})
   181→    if not pots:
   182→        return
   183→    from .scoring import merge_potentials, compute_dimension_scores, compute_objective_score
   184→    merged = merge_potentials(pots)
   185→    if not merged:
   186→        return
   187→    ds = compute_dimension_scores(findings, merged, strict=False)
   188→    ss = compute_dimension_scores(findings, merged, strict=True)
   189→    state["dimension_scores"] = {
   190→        n: {"score": ds[n]["score"], "strict": ss[n]["score"], "checks": ds[n]["checks"],
   191→            "issues": ds[n]["issues"], "tier": ds[n]["tier"], "detectors": ds[n].get("detectors", {})}
   192→        for n in ds}
   193→    state["objective_score"] = round(compute_objective_score(ds), 1)
   194→    state["objective_strict"] = round(compute_objective_score(ss), 1)
   195→
   196→
   197→def _recompute_stats(state: dict):
   198→    """Recompute stats, progress scores, and objective health scores from findings."""
   199→    findings = state["findings"]
   200→    counters, tier_stats = _count_findings(findings)
   201→    score, strict_score = _weighted_progress(findings)
   202→    state["stats"] = {
   203→        "total": sum(counters.values()),
   204→        **counters,
   205→        "by_tier": {str(t): ts for t, ts in sorted(tier_stats.items())},
   206→    }
   207→    state["score"] = score
   208→    state["strict_score"] = strict_score
   209→    _update_objective_health(state, findings)
   210→
   211→
   212→def is_ignored(finding_id: str, file: str, ignore_patterns: list[str]) -> bool:
   213→    """Check if a finding matches any ignore pattern (glob, ID prefix, or file path)."""
   214→    for pat in ignore_patterns:
   215→        if "*" in pat:
   216→            target = finding_id if "::" in pat else file
   217→            if fnmatch.fnmatch(target, pat):
   218→                return True
   219→        elif "::" in pat:
   220→            if finding_id.startswith(pat):
   221→                return True
   222→        elif file == pat or file == rel(pat):
   223→            return True
   224→    return False
   225→
   226→
   227→def add_ignore(state: dict, pattern: str) -> int:
   228→    """Add an ignore pattern. Removes matching findings from state. Returns count removed."""
   229→    config = state.setdefault("config", {})
   230→    ignores = config.setdefault("ignore", [])
   231→    if pattern not in ignores:
   232→        ignores.append(pattern)
   233→
   234→    to_remove = [fid for fid, f in state["findings"].items()
   235→                 if is_ignored(fid, f["file"], [pattern])]
   236→    for fid in to_remove:
   237→        del state["findings"][fid]
   238→    return len(to_remove)
   239→
   240→
   241→def make_finding(detector: str, file: str, name: str, *,
   242→                 tier: int, confidence: str, summary: str,
   243→                 detail: dict | None = None) -> Finding:
   244→    """Create a normalized finding dict with a stable ID."""
   245→    rfile = rel(file)
   246→    fid = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{name}" if name else f"{detector}::{rfile}"
   247→    now = _now()
   248→    return {"id": fid, "detector": detector, "file": rfile, "tier": tier,
   249→            "confidence": confidence, "summary": summary, "detail": detail or {},
   250→            "status": "open", "note": None, "first_seen": now, "last_seen": now,
   251→            "resolved_at": None, "reopen_count": 0}
   252→
   253→
   254→def _find_suspect_detectors(
   255→    existing: dict, current_by_detector: dict[str, int], force_resolve: bool,
   256→    ran_detectors: set[str] | None = None,
   257→) -> set[str]:
   258→    """Detectors that had open findings but didn't actually run this scan.
   259→
   260→    A detector is suspect only if:
   261→    1. It had open findings before
   262→    2. It returned 0 findings now
   263→    3. It's NOT in ran_detectors (i.e., it didn't run — tool missing, skipped, errored)
   264→
   265→    If ran_detectors is provided (from potentials), a 0-finding result from a
   266→    detector that DID run is trusted — the user likely fixed everything.
   267→    """
   268→    if force_resolve:
   269→        return set()
   270→    prev: dict[str, int] = {}
   271→    for f in existing.values():
   272→        if f["status"] == "open":
   273→            det = f.get("detector", "unknown")
   274→            prev[det] = prev.get(det, 0) + 1
   275→
   276→    suspect = set()
   277→    for det, n in prev.items():
   278→        if current_by_detector.get(det, 0) > 0:
   279→            continue  # Detector produced findings — not suspect
   280→        if ran_detectors is not None and det in ran_detectors:
   281→            continue  # Detector ran and found nothing — legitimate
   282→        # Detector had findings before, returned 0 now, and didn't appear in
   283→        # potentials. Only flag if it had a meaningful number of findings.
   284→        if n >= 3:
   285→            suspect.add(det)
   286→    return suspect
   287→
   288→
   289→def _auto_resolve_disappeared(
   290→    existing: dict, current_ids: set[str], suspect_detectors: set[str],
   291→    now: str, *, lang: str | None, scan_path: str | None,
   292→    exclude: tuple[str, ...] = (),
   293→) -> tuple[int, int, int]:
   294→    """Auto-resolve open/wontfix findings absent from scan. Returns (resolved, skip_lang, skip_path)."""
   295→    resolved = skip_lang = skip_path = 0
   296→    for fid, old in existing.items():
   297→        if fid in current_ids or old["status"] not in ("open", "wontfix"):
   298→            continue
   299→        if lang and old.get("lang") and old["lang"] != lang:
   300→            skip_lang += 1; continue
   301→        if scan_path and not old["file"].startswith(scan_path.rstrip("/") + "/") and old["file"] != scan_path:
   302→            skip_path += 1; continue
   303→        if exclude and any(matches_exclusion(old["file"], ex) for ex in exclude):
   304→            continue
   305→        if old.get("detector", "unknown") in suspect_detectors:
   306→            continue
   307→        prev = old["status"]
   308→        old["status"] = "auto_resolved"
   309→        old["resolved_at"] = now
   310→        old["note"] = ("Fixed despite wontfix — disappeared from scan (was wontfix)"
   311→                       if prev == "wontfix" else "Disappeared from scan — likely fixed")
   312→        resolved += 1
   313→    return resolved, skip_lang, skip_path
   314→
   315→
   316→def _upsert_findings(
   317→    existing: dict, current_findings: list[dict], ignore: list[str],
   318→    now: str, *, lang: str | None,
   319→) -> tuple[set[str], int, int, dict[str, int]]:
   320→    """Insert new findings and update existing ones. Returns (ids, new, reopened, by_detector)."""
   321→    current_ids: set[str] = set()
   322→    new_count = reopened = 0
   323→    by_detector: dict[str, int] = {}
   324→    for f in current_findings:
   325→        fid = f["id"]
   326→        if is_ignored(fid, f["file"], ignore):
   327→            continue
   328→        current_ids.add(fid)
   329→        det = f.get("detector", "unknown")
   330→        by_detector[det] = by_detector.get(det, 0) + 1
   331→        if lang:
   332→            f["lang"] = lang
   333→        if fid in existing:
   334→            old = existing[fid]
   335→            old.update(last_seen=now, tier=f["tier"], confidence=f["confidence"],
   336→                       summary=f["summary"], detail=f.get("detail", {}))
   337→            if "zone" in f:
   338→                old["zone"] = f["zone"]
   339→            if lang and not old.get("lang"):
   340→                old["lang"] = lang
   341→            if old["status"] in ("fixed", "auto_resolved"):
   342→                prev = old["status"]
   343→                old["reopen_count"] = old.get("reopen_count", 0) + 1
   344→                old.update(status="open", resolved_at=None,
   345→                           note=f"Reopened (×{old['reopen_count']}) — reappeared in scan (was {prev})")
   346→                reopened += 1
   347→        else:
   348→            existing[fid] = f
   349→            new_count += 1
   350→    return current_ids, new_count, reopened, by_detector
   351→
   352→
   353→def merge_scan(state: dict, current_findings: list[dict], *,
   354→               lang: str | None = None, scan_path: str | None = None,
   355→               force_resolve: bool = False, exclude: tuple[str, ...] = (),
   356→               potentials: dict[str, int] | None = None,
   357→               codebase_metrics: dict | None = None,
   358→               include_slow: bool = True) -> dict:
   359→    """Merge a fresh scan into existing state. Returns diff summary."""
   360→    from .utils import compute_tool_hash
   361→    now = _now()
   362→    state["last_scan"] = now
   363→    state["scan_count"] = state.get("scan_count", 0) + 1
   364→    state["tool_hash"] = compute_tool_hash()
   365→    if potentials is not None and lang:
   366→        state.setdefault("potentials", {})[lang] = potentials
   367→    if codebase_metrics is not None and lang:
   368→        state.setdefault("codebase_metrics", {})[lang] = codebase_metrics
   369→    if lang:
   370→        state.setdefault("scan_completeness", {})[lang] = "full" if include_slow else "fast"
   371→
   372→    existing = state["findings"]
   373→    ignore = state.get("config", {}).get("ignore", [])
   374→    current_ids, new_count, reopened_count, current_by_detector = _upsert_findings(
   375→        existing, current_findings, ignore, now, lang=lang)
   376→    # Detectors that appear in potentials actually ran — trust their 0-finding results
   377→    ran_detectors = set(potentials.keys()) if potentials else None
   378→    suspect_detectors = _find_suspect_detectors(
   379→        existing, current_by_detector, force_resolve, ran_detectors)
   380→    auto_resolved, skipped_lang, skipped_path = _auto_resolve_disappeared(
   381→        existing, current_ids, suspect_detectors, now,
   382→        lang=lang, scan_path=scan_path, exclude=exclude)
   383→    _recompute_stats(state)
   384→
   385→    # Append scan history entry for trajectory tracking
   386→    history = state.setdefault("scan_history", [])
   387→    history.append({
   388→        "timestamp": now,
   389→        "lang": lang,
   390→        "objective_strict": state.get("objective_strict"),
   391→        "objective_score": state.get("objective_score"),
   392→        "open": state["stats"]["open"],
   393→        "diff_new": new_count,
   394→        "diff_resolved": auto_resolved,
   395→        "dimension_scores": {
   396→            name: {"score": ds["score"], "strict": ds.get("strict", ds["score"])}
   397→            for name, ds in state.get("dimension_scores", {}).items()
   398→        } if state.get("dimension_scores") else None,
   399→    })
   400→    if len(history) > 20:
   401→        state["scan_history"] = history[-20:]
   402→
   403→    # Detect chronic reopeners (findings that keep bouncing between resolved and open)
   404→    chronic = [f for f in existing.values()
   405→               if f.get("reopen_count", 0) >= 2 and f["status"] == "open"]
   406→
   407→    return {
   408→        "new": new_count, "auto_resolved": auto_resolved,
   409→        "reopened": reopened_count, "total_current": len(current_ids),
   410→        "suspect_detectors": sorted(suspect_detectors) if suspect_detectors else [],
   411→        "chronic_reopeners": chronic,
   412→        "skipped_other_lang": skipped_lang, "skipped_out_of_scope": skipped_path,
   413→    }
   414→
   415→
   416→def _matches_pattern(fid: str, f: dict, pattern: str) -> bool:
   417→    """Check if a finding matches: exact ID, glob, ID prefix, detector name, or file path."""
   418→    if fid == pattern:
   419→        return True
   420→    if "*" in pattern:
   421→        return fnmatch.fnmatch(fid, pattern)
   422→    if "::" in pattern:
   423→        return fid.startswith(pattern)
   424→    return f.get("detector") == pattern or f["file"] == pattern or f["file"].startswith(pattern.rstrip("/") + "/")
   425→
   426→
   427→def match_findings(state: dict, pattern: str, status_filter: str = "open") -> list[dict]:
   428→    """Return findings matching *pattern* with the given status."""
   429→    return [f for fid, f in state["findings"].items()
   430→            if (status_filter == "all" or f["status"] == status_filter)
   431→            and _matches_pattern(fid, f, pattern)]
   432→
   433→
   434→def resolve_findings(state: dict, pattern: str, status: str,
   435→                     note: str | None = None) -> list[str]:
   436→    """Resolve findings matching pattern. Returns list of resolved IDs."""
   437→    now = _now()
   438→    resolved = []
   439→    for f in match_findings(state, pattern, status_filter="open"):
   440→        f.update(status=status, note=note, resolved_at=now)
   441→        resolved.append(f["id"])
   442→    _recompute_stats(state)
   443→    return resolved
   444→
```

```
    23→# Extra exclusions set via --exclude CLI flag, applied to all file discovery

    25→
    26→
    27→def set_exclusions(patterns: list[str]):
    28→    """Set global exclusion patterns (called once from CLI at startup)."""
    29→    global _extra_exclusions
    30→    _extra_exclusions = tuple(patterns)
    31→    _find_source_files_cached.cache_clear()
    32→
    33→
    34→# ── File content cache (opt-in, scan-scoped) ─────────────
    35→
    36→_file_cache: dict[str, str | None] = {}
    37→_cache_enabled = False
    38→
    39→
    40→def enable_file_cache():
    41→    """Enable scan-scoped file content cache."""
    42→    global _cache_enabled
    43→    _cache_enabled = True
    44→    _file_cache.clear()
    45→
    46→
    47→def disable_file_cache():
    48→    """Disable file content cache and free memory."""
    49→    global _cache_enabled
    50→    _cache_enabled = False
    51→    _file_cache.clear()
    52→
    53→
    54→# ── Atomic file writes ─────────────────────────────────────
    55→
    56→import tempfile
    57→
    58→
    59→def safe_write_text(filepath: str | Path, content: str) -> None:
    60→    """Atomically write text to a file using temp+rename."""
    61→    p = Path(filepath)
    62→    p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    63→    fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=p.parent, suffix=".tmp")
    64→    try:
    65→        with os.fdopen(fd, "w") as f:
    66→            f.write(content)
    67→        os.replace(tmp, str(p))
    68→    except BaseException:
    69→        try:
    70→            os.unlink(tmp)
    71→        except OSError:
    72→            pass
    73→        raise
    74→
    75→
    76→# ── Cross-platform grep replacements ────────────────────────
    77→
    78→
    79→def _read_file_text(filepath: str) -> str | None:
    80→    """Read a file as text, with optional caching."""
    81→    if _cache_enabled:
    82→        if filepath in _file_cache:
    83→            return _file_cache[filepath]
    84→    try:
    85→        content = Path(filepath).read_text(errors="replace")
    86→    except OSError:
    87→        content = None
    88→    if _cache_enabled:
    89→        _file_cache[filepath] = content
    90→    return content
    91→
    92→
    93→def grep_files(pattern: str, file_list: list[str], *,
    94→               flags: int = 0) -> list[tuple[str, int, str]]:
    95→    """Search files for a regex pattern. Returns list of (filepath, lineno, line_text).
    96→
    97→    Cross-platform replacement for ``grep -rn -E <pattern> <path>``.
    98→    """
    99→    compiled = re.compile(pattern, flags)
   100→    results: list[tuple[str, int, str]] = []
   101→    for filepath in file_list:
   102→        abs_path = filepath if os.path.isabs(filepath) else str(PROJECT_ROOT / filepath)
   103→        content = _read_file_text(abs_path)
   104→        if content is None:
   105→            continue
   106→        for lineno, line in enumerate(content.splitlines(), 1):
   107→            if compiled.search(line):
   108→                results.append((filepath, lineno, line))
   109→    return results
   110→
   111→
   112→def grep_files_containing(names: set[str], file_list: list[str], *,
   113→                          word_boundary: bool = True) -> dict[str, set[str]]:
   114→    r"""Find which files contain which names. Returns {name: set(filepaths)}.
   115→
   116→    Cross-platform replacement for ``grep -rlFw -f patternfile <path>``
   117→    followed by per-file ``grep -oFw``.
   118→    """
   119→    if not names:
   120→        return {}
   121→    if word_boundary:
   122→        escaped = sorted(names, key=len, reverse=True)
   123→        combined = re.compile(r"\b(?:" + "|".join(re.escape(n) for n in escaped) + r")\b")
   124→    else:
   125→        escaped = sorted(names, key=len, reverse=True)
   126→        combined = re.compile("|".join(re.escape(n) for n in escaped))
   127→
   128→    name_to_files: dict[str, set[str]] = {}
   129→    for filepath in file_list:
   130→        abs_path = filepath if os.path.isabs(filepath) else str(PROJECT_ROOT / filepath)
   131→        content = _read_file_text(abs_path)
   132→        if content is None:
   133→            continue
   134→        found = set(combined.findall(content))
   135→        for name in found & names:
   136→            name_to_files.setdefault(name, set()).add(filepath)
   137→    return name_to_files
   138→
   139→
   140→def grep_count_files(name: str, file_list: list[str], *,
   141→                     word_boundary: bool = True) -> list[str]:
   142→    """Return list of files containing name. Replacement for ``grep -rl -w name``."""
   143→    if word_boundary:
   144→        pat = re.compile(r"\b" + re.escape(name) + r"\b")
   145→    else:
   146→        pat = re.compile(re.escape(name))
   147→    matching: list[str] = []
   148→    for filepath in file_list:
   149→        abs_path = filepath if os.path.isabs(filepath) else str(PROJECT_ROOT / filepath)
   150→        content = _read_file_text(abs_path)
   151→        if content is None:
   152→            continue
   153→        if pat.search(content):
   154→            matching.append(filepath)
   155→    return matching
   156→
   157→
   158→LOC_COMPACT_THRESHOLD = 10000  # Switch from "1,234" to "1K" format
   159→
   160→COLORS = {
   161→    "reset": "\033[0m",
   162→    "bold": "\033[1m",
   163→    "dim": "\033[2m",
   164→    "red": "\033[31m",
   165→    "green": "\033[32m",
   166→    "yellow": "\033[33m",
   167→    "blue": "\033[34m",
   168→    "cyan": "\033[36m",
   169→}
   170→
   171→NO_COLOR = os.environ.get("NO_COLOR") is not None
   172→
   173→
   174→def c(text: str, color: str) -> str:
   175→    if NO_COLOR or not sys.stdout.isatty():
   176→        return str(text)
   177→    return f"{COLORS.get(color, '')}{text}{COLORS['reset']}"
   178→
   179→
   180→def log(msg: str):
   181→    """Print a dim status message to stderr."""
   182→    print(c(msg, "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
   183→
   184→
   185→def print_table(headers: list[str], rows: list[list[str]], widths: list[int] | None = None):
   186→    if not rows:
   187→        return
   188→    if not widths:
   189→        widths = [max(len(str(h)), *(len(str(r[i])) for r in rows)) for i, h in enumerate(headers)]
   190→    header_line = "  ".join(h.ljust(w) for h, w in zip(headers, widths))
   191→    print(c(header_line, "bold"))
   192→    print(c("─" * (sum(widths) + 2 * (len(widths) - 1)), "dim"))
   193→    for row in rows:
   194→        print("  ".join(str(v).ljust(w) for v, w in zip(row, widths)))
   195→
   196→
   197→def display_entries(args, entries, *, label, empty_msg, columns, widths, row_fn,
   198→                    json_payload=None, overflow=True):
   199→    """Standard JSON/empty/table display for detect commands.
   200→
   201→    Handles the three-branch pattern shared by most cmd wrappers:
   202→    1. --json → dump payload  2. empty → green message  3. table → header + rows + overflow.
   203→    Returns True if entries were displayed, False if empty.
   204→    """
   205→    import json as _json
   206→    if getattr(args, "json", False):
   207→        payload = json_payload or {"count": len(entries), "entries": entries}
   208→        print(_json.dumps(payload, indent=2))
   209→        return True
   210→    if not entries:
   211→        print(c(empty_msg, "green"))
   212→        return False
   213→    print(c(f"\n{label}: {len(entries)}\n", "bold"))
   214→    top = getattr(args, "top", 20)
   215→    rows = [row_fn(e) for e in entries[:top]]
   216→    print_table(columns, rows, widths)
   217→    if overflow and len(entries) > top:
   218→        print(f"\n  ... and {len(entries) - top} more")
   219→    return True
   220→
   221→
   222→def rel(path: str) -> str:
   223→    try:
   224→        return str(Path(path).resolve().relative_to(PROJECT_ROOT)).replace("\\", "/")
   225→    except ValueError:
   226→        return path
   227→
   228→
   229→def resolve_path(filepath: str) -> str:
   230→    """Resolve a filepath to absolute, handling both relative and absolute."""
   231→    p = Path(filepath)
   232→    if p.is_absolute():
   233→        return str(p.resolve())
   234→    return str((PROJECT_ROOT / filepath).resolve())
   235→
   236→
   237→def matches_exclusion(rel_path: str, exclusion: str) -> bool:
   238→    """Check if a relative path matches an exclusion pattern (path-component aware).
   239→
   240→    Matches if exclusion is a path component (e.g. "test" matches "test/foo.py"
   241→    or "src/test/bar.py") or a directory prefix (e.g. "src/test" matches
   242→    "src/test/bar.py"). Does NOT do substring matching — "test" will NOT match
   243→    "testimony.py".
   244→    """
   245→    parts = Path(rel_path).parts
   246→    # Direct component match
   247→    if exclusion in parts:
   248→        return True
   249→    # Directory prefix match (e.g. "src/test" matches "src/test/bar.py")
   250→    if "/" in exclusion or os.sep in exclusion:
   251→        normalized = exclusion.rstrip("/").rstrip(os.sep)
   252→        return rel_path.startswith(normalized + "/") or rel_path.startswith(normalized + os.sep)
   253→    return False
   254→
   255→
   256→def _is_excluded_dir(name: str, rel_path: str, extra: tuple[str, ...]) -> bool:
   257→    """Check if a directory should be pruned during traversal."""
   258→    if name in DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS or name.endswith(".egg-info"):
   259→        return True
   260→    if extra and any(matches_exclusion(rel_path, ex) or ex == name for ex in extra):
   261→        return True
   262→    return False
   263→
   264→
   265→@lru_cache(maxsize=16)
   266→def _find_source_files_cached(path: str, extensions: tuple[str, ...],
   267→                               exclusions: tuple[str, ...] | None = None,
   268→                               extra_exclusions: tuple[str, ...] = ()) -> tuple[str, ...]:
   269→    """Cached file discovery using os.walk — cross-platform, prunes during traversal."""
   270→    root = Path(path)
   271→    if not root.is_absolute():
   272→        root = PROJECT_ROOT / root
   273→    all_exclusions = (exclusions or ()) + extra_exclusions
   274→    ext_set = set(extensions)
   275→    files: list[str] = []
   276→    for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(root):
   277→        # Prune excluded directories in-place (prevents descending into them)
   278→        rel_dir = os.path.relpath(dirpath, PROJECT_ROOT).replace("\\", "/")
   279→        dirnames[:] = sorted(
   280→            d for d in dirnames
   281→            if not _is_excluded_dir(d, rel_dir + "/" + d, all_exclusions)
   282→        )
   283→        for fname in filenames:
   284→            if any(fname.endswith(ext) for ext in ext_set):
   285→                full = os.path.join(dirpath, fname)
   286→                rel_file = os.path.relpath(full, PROJECT_ROOT).replace("\\", "/")
   287→                if all_exclusions and any(matches_exclusion(rel_file, ex) for ex in all_exclusions):
   288→                    continue
   289→                files.append(rel_file)
   290→    return tuple(sorted(files))
   291→
   292→
   293→def find_source_files(path: str | Path, extensions: list[str],
   294→                      exclusions: list[str] | None = None) -> list[str]:
   295→    """Find all files with given extensions under a path, excluding patterns."""
   296→    # Pass _extra_exclusions as part of the cache key so changes invalidate cached results
   297→    return list(_find_source_files_cached(
   298→        str(path), tuple(extensions), tuple(exclusions) if exclusions else None,
   299→        _extra_exclusions))
   300→
   301→
   302→def find_ts_files(path: str | Path) -> list[str]:
   303→    """Find all .ts and .tsx files under a path."""
   304→    return find_source_files(path, [".ts", ".tsx"])
   305→
   306→
   307→def find_tsx_files(path: str | Path) -> list[str]:
   308→    """Find all .tsx files under a path."""
   309→    return find_source_files(path, [".tsx"])
   310→
   311→
   312→def find_py_files(path: str | Path) -> list[str]:
   313→    """Find all .py files under a path."""
   314→    return find_source_files(path, [".py"])
   315→
   316→
   317→TOOL_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
   318→
   319→
   320→def compute_tool_hash() -> str:
   321→    """Compute a content hash of all .py files in the desloppify package.
   322→
   323→    Changes to any tool source file produce a different hash, enabling
   324→    staleness detection for scan results.
   325→    """
   326→    h = hashlib.sha256()
   327→    for py_file in sorted(TOOL_DIR.rglob("*.py")):
   328→        try:
   329→            h.update(str(py_file.relative_to(TOOL_DIR)).encode())
   330→            h.update(py_file.read_bytes())
   331→        except OSError:
   332→            continue
   333→    return h.hexdigest()[:12]
   334→
   335→
   336→def check_tool_staleness(state: dict) -> str | None:
   337→    """Return a warning string if tool code has changed since last scan, else None."""
   338→    stored = state.get("tool_hash")
   339→    if not stored:
   340→        return None
   341→    current = compute_tool_hash()
   342→    if current != stored:
   343→        return (f"Tool code changed since last scan (was {stored}, now {current}). "
   344→                f"Consider re-running: desloppify scan")
   345→    return None
   346→
   347→
   348→def get_area(filepath: str) -> str:
   349→    """Derive an area name from a file path (generic: first 2 path components)."""
   350→    parts = filepath.split("/")
   351→    return "/".join(parts[:2]) if len(parts) > 1 else parts[0]
   352→
```

```
1→"""Narrative orchestrator — compute_narrative() entry point."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from ._constants import STRUCTURAL_MERGE
     6→
     7→from .phase import _detect_phase, _detect_milestone
     8→from .dimensions import _analyze_dimensions, _analyze_debt
     9→from .actions import _compute_actions, _compute_tools
    10→from .headline import _compute_headline
    11→from .reminders import _compute_reminders
    12→from .strategy import _compute_strategy
    13→
    14→
    15→def _count_open_by_detector(findings: dict) -> dict[str, int]:
    16→    """Count open findings by detector, merging structural sub-detectors."""
    17→    by_det: dict[str, int] = {}
    18→    for f in findings.values():
    19→        if f["status"] != "open":
    20→            continue
    21→        det = f.get("detector", "unknown")
    22→        if det in STRUCTURAL_MERGE:
    23→            det = "structural"
    24→        by_det[det] = by_det.get(det, 0) + 1
    25→    return by_det
    26→
    27→
    28→def _compute_badge_status() -> dict:
    29→    """Check if scorecard.png exists and whether README references it."""
    30→    from ..utils import PROJECT_ROOT
    31→
    32→    scorecard_path = PROJECT_ROOT / "scorecard.png"
    33→    generated = scorecard_path.exists()
    34→
    35→    in_readme = False
    36→    if generated:
    37→        for readme_name in ("README.md", "readme.md", "README.MD"):
    38→            readme_path = PROJECT_ROOT / readme_name
    39→            if readme_path.exists():
    40→                try:
    41→                    in_readme = "scorecard.png" in readme_path.read_text()
    42→                except OSError:
    43→                    pass
    44→                break
    45→
    46→    recommendation = None
    47→    if generated and not in_readme:
    48→        recommendation = 'Add to README: <img src="scorecard.png" width="400">'
    49→
    50→    return {
    51→        "generated": generated,
    52→        "in_readme": in_readme,
    53→        "path": "scorecard.png",
    54→        "recommendation": recommendation,
    55→    }
    56→
    57→
    58→def compute_narrative(state: dict, *, diff: dict | None = None,
    59→                      lang: str | None = None,
    60→                      command: str | None = None) -> dict:
    61→    """Compute structured narrative context from state data.
    62→
    63→    Returns a dict with: phase, headline, dimensions, actions, tools, debt, milestone.
    64→
    65→    Args:
    66→        state: Current state dict.
    67→        diff: Scan diff (only present after a scan).
    68→        lang: Language name (e.g. "python", "typescript").
    69→        command: The command that triggered this (e.g. "scan", "fix", "resolve").
    70→    """
    71→    raw_history = state.get("scan_history", [])
    72→    # Filter history to current language to avoid mixing trajectories.
    73→    # Include entries without a lang field (pre-date this feature) for backward compat.
    74→    history = ([h for h in raw_history if h.get("lang") in (lang, None)]
    75→               if lang else raw_history)
    76→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
    77→    stats = state.get("stats", {})
    78→    obj_strict = state.get("objective_strict")
    79→    obj_score = state.get("objective_score")
    80→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
    81→
    82→    by_det = _count_open_by_detector(findings)
    83→    badge = _compute_badge_status()
    84→
    85→    phase = _detect_phase(history, obj_strict)
    86→    dimensions = _analyze_dimensions(dim_scores, history, state)
    87→    debt = _analyze_debt(dim_scores, findings, history)
    88→    milestone = _detect_milestone(state, diff, history)
    89→    actions = _compute_actions(by_det, dim_scores, state, debt, lang)
    90→    strategy = _compute_strategy(findings, by_det, actions, phase, lang)
    91→    tools = _compute_tools(by_det, lang, badge)
    92→    headline = _compute_headline(phase, dimensions, debt, milestone, diff,
    93→                                 obj_strict, obj_score, stats, history,
    94→                                 open_by_detector=by_det)
    95→    reminders, updated_reminder_history = _compute_reminders(
    96→        state, lang, phase, debt, actions, dimensions, badge, command)
    97→
    98→    return {
    99→        "phase": phase,
   100→        "headline": headline,
   101→        "dimensions": dimensions,
   102→        "actions": actions,
   103→        "strategy": strategy,
   104→        "tools": tools,
   105→        "debt": debt,
   106→        "milestone": milestone,
   107→        "reminders": reminders,
   108→        "reminder_history": updated_reminder_history,
   109→    }
   110→
```

```
1→"""Zone classification system — deterministic file intent classification.
     2→
     3→Classifies files into zones (production, test, config, generated, script, vendor)
     4→based on path patterns. Zone metadata flows through findings, scoring, and the LLM.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import os
    10→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    11→from enum import Enum
    12→
    13→
    14→class Zone(str, Enum):
    15→    """File intent zone — determines scoring and detection policy."""
    16→    PRODUCTION = "production"
    17→    TEST = "test"
    18→    CONFIG = "config"
    19→    GENERATED = "generated"
    20→    SCRIPT = "script"
    21→    VENDOR = "vendor"
    22→
    23→
    24→# Zones excluded from the health score
    25→EXCLUDED_ZONES = {Zone.TEST, Zone.CONFIG, Zone.GENERATED, Zone.VENDOR}
    26→
    27→# String values for quick lookup in scoring (findings store zone as string)
    28→EXCLUDED_ZONE_VALUES = {z.value for z in EXCLUDED_ZONES}
    29→
    30→
    31→@dataclass
    32→class ZoneRule:
    33→    """A classification rule: zone + list of path patterns.
    34→
    35→    Patterns are matched against relative file paths. First matching rule wins.
    36→    Pattern types (auto-detected from shape):
    37→      - "/dir/"   → substring match on full path (directory marker)
    38→      - ".ext"    → basename ends-with (suffix/extension, e.g. ".d.ts", ".test.")
    39→      - "prefix_" → basename starts-with (trailing underscore)
    40→      - "name.py" → basename exact match (has extension, no /)
    41→      - fallback  → substring on full path
    42→    """
    43→    zone: Zone
    44→    patterns: list[str]
    45→
    46→
    47→def _match_pattern(rel_path: str, pattern: str) -> bool:
    48→    """Match a zone pattern against a relative file path.
    49→
    50→    See ZoneRule docstring for pattern type conventions.
    51→    """
    52→    basename = os.path.basename(rel_path)
    53→
    54→    # Directory pattern: "/dir/" → substring on padded path
    55→    if pattern.startswith("/") and pattern.endswith("/"):
    56→        return pattern in ("/" + rel_path + "/")
    57→
    58→    # Suffix/extension pattern: starts with "." → contains on basename
    59→    if pattern.startswith("."):
    60→        return pattern in basename
    61→
    62→    # Prefix pattern: ends with "_" → basename starts-with
    63→    if pattern.endswith("_"):
    64→        return basename.startswith(pattern)
    65→
    66→    # Suffix pattern: starts with "_" → basename ends-with (_test.py, _pb2.py)
    67→    if pattern.startswith("_"):
    68→        return basename.endswith(pattern)
    69→
    70→    # Exact basename: has a proper file extension (1-5 chars after last dot),
    71→    # no "/" → exact basename match (config.py, setup.py, conftest.py)
    72→    if "/" not in pattern and "." in pattern:
    73→        ext = pattern.rsplit(".", 1)[-1]
    74→        if ext and len(ext) <= 5 and ext.isalnum():
    75→            return basename == pattern
    76→
    77→    # Fallback: substring on full path (vite.config, tsconfig, eslint, etc.)
    78→    return pattern in rel_path
    79→
    80→
    81→# ── Common zone rules (shared across languages) ──────────
    82→
    83→COMMON_ZONE_RULES = [
    84→    ZoneRule(Zone.VENDOR, ["/vendor/", "/third_party/", "/vendored/"]),
    85→    ZoneRule(Zone.GENERATED, ["/generated/", "/__generated__/"]),
    86→    ZoneRule(Zone.TEST, ["/tests/", "/test/", "/fixtures/"]),
    87→    ZoneRule(Zone.SCRIPT, ["/scripts/", "/bin/"]),
    88→]
    89→
    90→
    91→def classify_file(rel_path: str, rules: list[ZoneRule],
    92→                  overrides: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> Zone:
    93→    """Classify a file by its relative path. Overrides take priority."""
    94→    if overrides:
    95→        override = overrides.get(rel_path)
    96→        if override:
    97→            try:
    98→                return Zone(override)
    99→            except ValueError:
   100→                pass  # Invalid zone value — fall through to rules
   101→    for rule in rules:
   102→        for pattern in rule.patterns:
   103→            if _match_pattern(rel_path, pattern):
   104→                return rule.zone
   105→    return Zone.PRODUCTION
   106→
   107→
   108→class FileZoneMap:
   109→    """Cached zone classification for a set of files.
   110→
   111→    Built once per scan from file list + zone rules.
   112→    """
   113→
   114→    def __init__(self, files: list[str], rules: list[ZoneRule],
   115→                 rel_fn=None, overrides: dict[str, str] | None = None):
   116→        """Build zone map.
   117→
   118→        Args:
   119→            files: List of file paths (absolute or relative).
   120→            rules: Ordered zone rules (first match wins).
   121→            rel_fn: Optional function to convert paths to relative.
   122→            overrides: Manual zone overrides {rel_path: zone_value}.
   123→        """
   124→        self._map: dict[str, Zone] = {}
   125→        self._overrides = overrides
   126→        for f in files:
   127→            rp = rel_fn(f) if rel_fn else f
   128→            self._map[f] = classify_file(rp, rules, overrides)
   129→
   130→    def get(self, path: str) -> Zone:
   131→        """Get zone for a file path. Returns PRODUCTION if not classified."""
   132→        return self._map.get(path, Zone.PRODUCTION)
   133→
   134→    def exclude(self, files: list[str], *zones: Zone) -> list[str]:
   135→        """Return files NOT in the given zones."""
   136→        zone_set = set(zones)
   137→        return [f for f in files if self._map.get(f, Zone.PRODUCTION) not in zone_set]
   138→
   139→    def include_only(self, files: list[str], *zones: Zone) -> list[str]:
   140→        """Return files that ARE in the given zones."""
   141→        zone_set = set(zones)
   142→        return [f for f in files if self._map.get(f, Zone.PRODUCTION) in zone_set]
   143→
   144→    def counts(self) -> dict[str, int]:
   145→        """Return file count per zone."""
   146→        counts: dict[str, int] = {}
   147→        for zone in self._map.values():
   148→            counts[zone.value] = counts.get(zone.value, 0) + 1
   149→        return counts
   150→
   151→    def production_count(self) -> int:
   152→        """Count files classified as production."""
   153→        return len(self._map) - self.non_production_count()
   154→
   155→    def non_production_count(self) -> int:
   156→        """Count files in excluded zones (test/config/generated/vendor)."""
   157→        return sum(1 for z in self._map.values() if z in EXCLUDED_ZONES)
   158→
   159→    def all_files(self) -> list[str]:
   160→        """Return all classified file paths."""
   161→        return list(self._map.keys())
   162→
   163→    def items(self) -> list[tuple[str, Zone]]:
   164→        """Return all (path, zone) pairs."""
   165→        return list(self._map.items())
   166→
   167→
   168→# ── Zone detection policies ────────────────────────────────
   169→
   170→@dataclass
   171→class ZonePolicy:
   172→    """Per-zone detection policy.
   173→
   174→    skip_detectors: detectors to skip entirely for this zone.
   175→    downgrade_detectors: detectors whose confidence is downgraded to "low".
   176→    exclude_from_score: whether findings in this zone are excluded from scoring.
   177→    """
   178→    skip_detectors: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
   179→    downgrade_detectors: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
   180→    exclude_from_score: bool = False
   181→
   182→
   183→ZONE_POLICIES: dict[Zone, ZonePolicy] = {
   184→    Zone.PRODUCTION: ZonePolicy(),
   185→    Zone.TEST: ZonePolicy(
   186→        skip_detectors={"dupes", "single_use", "orphaned", "coupling", "facade",
   187→                        "dict_keys", "test_coverage"},
   188→        downgrade_detectors={"smells", "structural"},
   189→        exclude_from_score=True,
   190→    ),
   191→    Zone.CONFIG: ZonePolicy(
   192→        skip_detectors={"smells", "structural", "dupes", "naming",
   193→                        "single_use", "orphaned", "coupling", "facade",
   194→                        "dict_keys", "test_coverage"},
   195→        exclude_from_score=True,
   196→    ),
   197→    Zone.GENERATED: ZonePolicy(
   198→        skip_detectors={"unused", "logs", "exports", "deprecated", "structural",
   199→                        "props", "smells", "react", "dupes", "single_use",
   200→                        "orphaned", "coupling", "facade", "naming", "patterns",
   201→                        "cycles", "flat_dirs", "dict_keys", "test_coverage",
   202→                        "security"},
   203→        exclude_from_score=True,
   204→    ),
   205→    Zone.VENDOR: ZonePolicy(
   206→        skip_detectors={"unused", "logs", "exports", "deprecated", "structural",
   207→                        "props", "smells", "react", "dupes", "single_use",
   208→                        "orphaned", "coupling", "facade", "naming", "patterns",
   209→                        "cycles", "flat_dirs", "dict_keys", "test_coverage",
   210→                        "security"},
   211→        exclude_from_score=True,
   212→    ),
   213→    Zone.SCRIPT: ZonePolicy(
   214→        skip_detectors={"coupling", "single_use", "orphaned", "facade"},
   215→        downgrade_detectors={"structural"},
   216→    ),
   217→}
   218→
   219→
   220→# ── Helpers for phase runners ─────────────────────────────
   221→
   222→def adjust_potential(zone_map, total: int) -> int:
   223→    """Subtract non-production files from a potential count.
   224→
   225→    Uses the zone map's own file list — no need to pass files separately.
   226→    No-op if zone_map is None (backward compat).
   227→    """
   228→    if zone_map is None:
   229→        return total
   230→    return max(total - zone_map.non_production_count(), 0)
   231→
   232→
   233→def should_skip_finding(zone_map, filepath: str, detector: str) -> bool:
   234→    """Check if a finding should be skipped based on zone policy.
   235→
   236→    Returns True if the file's zone policy says to skip this detector.
   237→    """
   238→    if zone_map is None:
   239→        return False
   240→    zone = zone_map.get(filepath)
   241→    policy = ZONE_POLICIES.get(zone)
   242→    return policy is not None and detector in policy.skip_detectors
   243→
   244→
   245→def filter_entries(zone_map, entries: list[dict], detector: str,
   246→                   file_key: str = "file") -> list[dict]:
   247→    """Filter detector entries by zone policy. No-op if zone_map is None.
   248→
   249→    If file_key points to a list (e.g. cycle entries with "files"), checks
   250→    the first element.
   251→    """
   252→    if zone_map is None:
   253→        return entries
   254→
   255→    def _get_path(entry):
   256→        val = entry[file_key]
   257→        return val[0] if isinstance(val, list) else val
   258→
   259→    return [e for e in entries
   260→            if not should_skip_finding(zone_map, _get_path(e), detector)]
   261→
```

```
1→"""Multi-line smell detection helpers (brace-tracked).
     2→
     3→Extracted from smells.py to keep file sizes manageable.
     4→All helpers are imported into smells.py for use in detect_smells().
     5→"""
     6→
     7→import re
     8→
     9→_MAX_CATCH_BODY = 1000  # max characters to scan for catch block body
    10→
    11→
    12→def _scan_code(text: str, start: int = 0, end: int | None = None):
    13→    """Yield (index, char, in_string) tuples, handling escapes correctly.
    14→
    15→    Skips escaped characters (\\x) by advancing +2 instead of +1.
    16→    Tracks single-quoted, double-quoted, and template literal strings.
    17→    Correct for \\\\\" (escaped backslash before quote) where prev_ch pattern fails.
    18→    """
    19→    i = start
    20→    limit = end if end is not None else len(text)
    21→    in_str = None
    22→    while i < limit:
    23→        ch = text[i]
    24→        if in_str:
    25→            if ch == '\\' and i + 1 < limit:
    26→                yield (i, ch, True)
    27→                i += 1
    28→                yield (i, text[i], True)
    29→                i += 1
    30→                continue
    31→            if ch == in_str:
    32→                in_str = None
    33→            yield (i, ch, in_str is not None)
    34→        else:
    35→            if ch in ("'", '"', '`'):
    36→                in_str = ch
    37→                yield (i, ch, True)
    38→            else:
    39→                yield (i, ch, False)
    40→        i += 1
    41→
    42→
    43→def _strip_ts_comments(text: str) -> str:
    44→    """Strip // and /* */ comments while preserving strings."""
    45→    result: list[str] = []
    46→    i = 0
    47→    in_str = None
    48→    while i < len(text):
    49→        ch = text[i]
    50→        if in_str:
    51→            if ch == '\\' and i + 1 < len(text):
    52→                result.append(text[i:i + 2])
    53→                i += 2
    54→                continue
    55→            if ch == in_str:
    56→                in_str = None
    57→            result.append(ch)
    58→            i += 1
    59→        elif ch in ('"', "'", '`'):
    60→            in_str = ch
    61→            result.append(ch)
    62→            i += 1
    63→        elif ch == '/' and i + 1 < len(text):
    64→            if text[i + 1] == '/':
    65→                nl = text.find('\n', i)
    66→                if nl == -1:
    67→                    break
    68→                i = nl
    69→            elif text[i + 1] == '*':
    70→                end = text.find('*/', i + 2)
    71→                if end == -1:
    72→                    break
    73→                i = end + 2
    74→            else:
    75→                result.append(ch)
    76→                i += 1
    77→        else:
    78→            result.append(ch)
    79→            i += 1
    80→    return ''.join(result)
    81→
    82→
    83→def _ts_match_is_in_string(line: str, match_start: int) -> bool:
    84→    """Check if a match position falls inside a string literal or comment on a single line.
    85→
    86→    Mirrors Python's _match_is_in_string but for TS syntax (', ", `, //).
    87→    """
    88→    i = 0
    89→    in_str = None
    90→
    91→    while i < len(line):
    92→        if i == match_start:
    93→            return in_str is not None
    94→
    95→        ch = line[i]
    96→
    97→        # Escape sequences inside strings
    98→        if in_str and ch == "\\" and i + 1 < len(line):
    99→            i += 2
   100→            continue
   101→
   102→        if in_str:
   103→            if ch == in_str:
   104→                in_str = None
   105→            i += 1
   106→            continue
   107→
   108→        # Line comment — everything after is non-code
   109→        if ch == "/" and i + 1 < len(line) and line[i + 1] == "/":
   110→            return match_start > i
   111→
   112→        if ch in ("'", '"', '`'):
   113→            in_str = ch
   114→            i += 1
   115→            continue
   116→
   117→        i += 1
   118→
   119→    return False
```
