## Answers to Your Questions

### What function-level patterns should the review tool detect that it currently does not?

1. **Return type annotation lies.** When a function's actual return type does not match its annotation, and the callers destructure correctly (proving the annotation is wrong, not the code). This requires comparing the return statements against the annotation.

2. **Display + Compute conflation.** Functions whose names start with `show_`/`display_`/`print_` but whose return values are depended upon by callers. This is a naming violation that requires checking callsites.

3. **Dead accumulator writes.** Values assigned into dict keys that are overwritten before being read. The `priority` counter in `_compute_actions` is a clear example -- it accumulates values that are immediately discarded by the re-sort.

4. **Duplicate schema mining.** Two or more functions that extract data from the same JSON shape (task params, orchestrator payload) using different field lists. This requires cross-file analysis of string literal property access patterns.

5. **Synchronous functions dressed as async.** Functions declared `async` that never `await` anything. While `require-await` exists as an ESLint rule, the review tool should flag this as a design issue when it occurs in a family of functions where some are genuinely async and some are not.

6. **setTimeout-as-synchronization.** Any `setTimeout` inside a Promise that is clearly waiting for React state to settle. Pattern: `setX(value)` followed by `setTimeout(() => { /* use X */ }, N)`.

7. **Implicit ordering dependencies.** Functions like `_detect_phase` where the correctness depends on the order of `if/elif` branches, and where re-ordering would silently change behavior. This is detectable by finding if/elif chains where multiple branches could be true simultaneously for the same input.

### What context data would help?

- **All callers of a function:** Essential for judging return type fitness, display-vs-compute conflation, and whether a function's error signaling matches what callers check.
- **Sibling functions in the same module:** Needed for convention_outlier detection (do all other functions in this module use the same error pattern? same async pattern? same naming convention?).
- **Functions that access the same schema fields:** Needed for duplicate-schema-mining detection. Cross-file analysis of property access chains on untyped records.
- **The full type hierarchy of return values:** If a function returns `dict` but callers destructure it as a tuple, the type annotation is lying.
- **Call graph depth:** Functions that are called only once might warrant inlining; functions called from many sites warrant stricter interface design.

### Python-specific vs TypeScript-specific patterns

**Python-specific:**
- Return type annotations that lie (Python does not enforce them at runtime, so they drift silently).
- Multiple `import` statements inside function bodies (Python's lazy import pattern). The desloppify codebase has these throughout (e.g., `scan.py` lines 40, 141, 170, 192, 202). While intentional to break import cycles, a reviewer should check whether the cycle could be broken architecturally instead.
- `dict` parameters where a TypedDict or dataclass would enforce structure. The narrative module passes `state: dict`, `diff: dict`, `by_det: dict` everywhere. The type is always `dict` but the required keys differ per function.

**TypeScript-specific:**
- Optional callbacks in large interfaces (the ApplyContext pattern). Python does not have this problem because it uses keyword arguments.
- Async functions that never await. Python's `async def` that never uses `await` raises a warning in some checkers.
- Type assertions (`as Record<string, unknown>`) that bypass the type system. These are concentrated in the task-utils.ts and applySettingsService.ts files -- they indicate the type system has given up on modeling the JSON shape.
- Query key construction inconsistency (`[...queryKeys.resources.all, type, 'v2']` vs `[...queryKeys.resources.all, data.type]`). This is a TS/React-Query-specific pattern where stale query key construction causes cache bugs.

### What makes a function "review-worthy" vs "fine as-is"?

Heuristics for prioritizing review:

1. **Parameter count >= 5.** The more inputs, the more the function's contract matters and the more likely it is that some parameters are redundant or redundantly constrained.

2. **Return type is `dict` / `Record<string, unknown>` / any unstructured type.** These functions have implicit contracts that are not enforced by the type system.

3. **The function has both side effects and a return value.** Pure functions and pure-side-effect functions are straightforward. Functions that do both (like `_show_post_scan_analysis`) are where naming and contract issues hide.

4. **The function is longer than ~50 lines with multiple conditional branches.** `_compute_headline_inner` (80 lines, 8+ branches) is borderline. `useUpdateResource` (110 lines, 4 queries, multiple error paths) is clearly review-worthy.

5. **The function accesses deeply nested dict/JSON paths.** `deriveInputImages`, `extractSettings`, and `extractPairShotGenerationId` all do deep property mining on untyped JSON. Each access is a potential bug if the schema changes.

6. **The function is duplicated or near-duplicated.** The pagination loops in `useListPublicResources`/`useListResources` and the cumulative-position calculation in `applyFramePositionsToExistingImages`/`replaceImagesIfRequested` are exact duplicates.

7. **The function uses magic numbers tied to external constraints.** `100`ms timeout, `20000` safety limit, `1000` page size, `81` base max gap -- these are "review-worthy" because a reviewer should ask "is this documented? will it need to change?"

**Functions that are "fine as-is":**
- Pure mappers under ~15 lines with clear names (`getVariantLabel`, `getTimeAgo`, `pixelToFrame`).
- Functions whose name completely describes their behavior and whose parameters are typed (`_strip_test_markers`, `_hash_file`).
- Standard CRUD hooks that follow the exact same pattern as their siblings (`useDeleteResource` is fine -- it is the simplest version of the pattern).

   - **`tests/test_review.py`** — NEW FILE: 53 tests
     - TestBuildReviewContext (7 tests): empty files, naming vocab, error conventions, existing findings, codebase stats, module patterns, import graph, zone distribution
     - TestSelectFilesForReview (7 tests): production files, max_files limit, cache skip fresh, cache refresh stale, content hash change, force refresh, priority ordering

4. Errors and Fixes:

   - **Dep graph lookup returning 0 importers**: Dep graph keys are absolute paths (`/Users/.../desloppify/utils.py`) but lookups used `rel()` which returns relative paths (`desloppify/utils.py`). Fixed by adding `_dep_graph_lookup()` that tries `resolve_path(filepath)` first, then `rel(filepath)`.

   - **Module patterns using TS-specific regex for Python**: `export default` and `export function|const|class` regexes were used for all languages. Fixed by checking `lang.name == "typescript"` and using Python-specific patterns (`def`, `__all__`, `class`) for Python.

   - **Import graph summary showing absolute paths**: `rel()` was not applied to dep graph keys in context builder. Fixed by wrapping keys with `rel(f)`.

   - **Types/constants files over-represented in TS (17/50)**: No deprioritization for declaration-only files. Fixed by adding `_LOW_VALUE_NAMES` regex pattern and reducing their priority score to `score // 3`.

   - **Tiny files selected (<20 LOC)**: No minimum LOC filter. Fixed by adding `_MIN_REVIEW_LOC = 20` and returning `-1` priority for files below threshold.

   - **Test failures after priority changes**: Tests used fake paths or tiny content that didn't pass the new 20 LOC minimum. Fixed by creating real files in tmp_path with `"content\n" * 25`.

   - **Zone mock matching pytest temp paths**: `if "test" in filepath` matched `/private/var/folders/.../pytest.../` paths, classifying all files as test zone. Fixed to check `fname.endswith(".test.ts")` or `fname.startswith("test_")` or `"__tests__" in filepath`.

   - **Content assertion exact match**: `assert data["files"][0]["content"] == "export function getData() { return 42; }"` failed after content was repeated 25 times. Fixed to use `in` operator.

   - **Headline None + review_suffix potential TypeError**: The low-abstraction agent identified that if `security_prefix` is `""` and `headline is None`, `result = "" or None` evaluates to `None`, and `None + review_suffix` would raise TypeError. Currently unreachable due to business logic constraints but fragile.

   - Verified the review system works end-to-end for both Python and TypeScript

   - Confirmed review findings persist through normal scans (suspect detector protection)
   - Verified file selection quality improved dramatically: TS types files dropped from 17/50 to 1/50

   - `_detect_async_no_await` uses raw string search bypassing `_scan_code`
   - `_detect_error_no_throw` doesn't use `_ts_match_is_in_string` from same file
   - `build_review_context` reads all files 3-4x without enabling file cache
   - Duplicated `_now()` function in review.py and state.py

   141→class TestBuildReviewContext:
   142→    def test_empty_files(self, mock_lang, empty_state):
   143→        from desloppify.review import build_review_context
   144→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[])
   145→        ctx = build_review_context(Path("/tmp"), mock_lang, empty_state)
   146→        assert ctx.naming_vocabulary == {}

   148→
   149→    def test_naming_vocabulary_extraction(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   150→        from desloppify.review import build_review_context
   151→        (tmp_path / "foo.ts").write_text("function getData() {}\ndef setName(): pass\nclass UserService {}")
   152→        (tmp_path / "bar.ts").write_text("function getUser() {}\nasync function handleClick() {}")
   153→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[
   154→            str(tmp_path / "foo.ts"), str(tmp_path / "bar.ts")
   155→        ])
   156→        ctx = build_review_context(tmp_path, mock_lang, empty_state)
   157→        assert ctx.naming_vocabulary["total_names"] == 5
   158→        assert ctx.naming_vocabulary["prefixes"]["get"] == 2
   159→        assert ctx.naming_vocabulary["prefixes"]["set"] == 1
   160→        assert ctx.naming_vocabulary["prefixes"]["handle"] == 1
   161→
   162→    def test_error_convention_detection(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   163→        from desloppify.review import build_review_context
   164→        (tmp_path / "foo.ts").write_text("try { x } catch(e) {}\nreturn null;")
   165→        (tmp_path / "bar.ts").write_text("throw new Error('fail')")
   166→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[
   167→            str(tmp_path / "foo.ts"), str(tmp_path / "bar.ts")

   172→        assert ctx.error_conventions.get("throws") == 1

   225→class TestSelectFilesForReview:
   226→    def test_selects_production_files(self, mock_lang_with_zones, empty_state, tmp_path):
   227→        from desloppify.review import select_files_for_review
   228→        # Create real files with enough content to pass min LOC filter
   229→        src = tmp_path / "src"
   230→        src.mkdir()
   231→        (src / "foo.ts").write_text("export function foo() {}\n" * 25)
   232→        (src / "bar.ts").write_text("export function bar() {}\n" * 25)
   233→        tests = src / "__tests__"
   234→        tests.mkdir()
   235→        (tests / "foo.test.ts").write_text("test('x', () => {})\n" * 25)
   236→        foo_path = str(src / "foo.ts")
   237→        bar_path = str(src / "bar.ts")
   238→        test_path = str(tests / "foo.test.ts")
   239→        mock_lang_with_zones.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[
   240→            foo_path, bar_path, test_path,

   242→        files = select_files_for_review(mock_lang_with_zones, tmp_path, empty_state)

   247→    def test_max_files_limit(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):

   260→    def test_cache_skip_fresh_files(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   261→        from desloppify.review import select_files_for_review, _hash_file
   262→        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   263→        # Create a real file for hashing
   264→        real_file = tmp_path / "cached.ts"
   265→        real_file.write_text("cached content")
   266→        content_hash = _hash_file(str(real_file))
   267→
   268→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[str(real_file)])
   269→        state = dict(empty_state)
   270→        rpath = str(real_file.relative_to(Path.cwd())) if real_file.is_relative_to(Path.cwd()) else str(real_file)
   271→        # We need to patch rel() to return a stable path
   272→        with patch("desloppify.review.rel", return_value="cached.ts"):
   273→            state["review_cache"] = {
   274→                "files": {
   275→                    "cached.ts": {
   276→                        "content_hash": content_hash,
   277→                        "reviewed_at": now,
   278→                        "finding_count": 0,
   279→                    }
   280→                }
   281→            }
   282→            files = select_files_for_review(mock_lang, tmp_path, state, max_age_days=30)
   283→        assert len(files) == 0

   285→    def test_cache_refresh_stale_files(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   286→        from desloppify.review import select_files_for_review, _hash_file
   287→        old_time = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(days=60)).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   288→        real_file = tmp_path / "stale.ts"
   289→        real_file.write_text("stale content\n" * 25)  # >= _MIN_REVIEW_LOC
   290→        content_hash = _hash_file(str(real_file))
   291→
   292→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[str(real_file)])
   293→        state = dict(empty_state)
   294→        with patch("desloppify.review.rel", return_value="stale.ts"):
   295→            state["review_cache"] = {
   296→                "files": {
   297→                    "stale.ts": {
   298→                        "content_hash": content_hash,
   299→                        "reviewed_at": old_time,
   300→                        "finding_count": 0,
   301→                    }
   302→                }
   303→            }
   304→            files = select_files_for_review(mock_lang, tmp_path, state, max_age_days=30)
   305→        assert len(files) == 1

   307→    def test_content_hash_change_triggers_review(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   308→        from desloppify.review import select_files_for_review
   309→        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")

   313→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[str(real_file)])

   316→            state["review_cache"] = {
   317→                "files": {
   318→                    "changed.ts": {
   319→                        "content_hash": "old_hash_different",
   320→                        "reviewed_at": now,
   321→                        "finding_count": 0,
   322→                    }
   323→                }
   324→            }
   325→            files = select_files_for_review(mock_lang, tmp_path, state, max_age_days=30)
   326→        assert len(files) == 1

   351→    def test_priority_ordering_by_importers(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   352→        from desloppify.review import select_files_for_review
   353→        # Create real files so _read_file_text works (need >= _MIN_REVIEW_LOC)
   354→        src = tmp_path / "src"
   355→        src.mkdir()
   356→        (src / "popular.ts").write_text("export function foo() {}\n" * 30)
   357→        (src / "lonely.ts").write_text("export function bar() {}\n" * 30)
   358→        pop_abs = str(src / "popular.ts")
   359→        lon_abs = str(src / "lonely.ts")
   360→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[pop_abs, lon_abs])
   361→        mock_lang._dep_graph = {
   362→            pop_abs: {"importers": {"a", "b", "c", "d", "e"}, "imports": set()},
   363→            lon_abs: {"importers": set(), "imports": set()},
   364→        }
   365→        files = select_files_for_review(mock_lang, tmp_path, empty_state)
   366→        assert files[0] == pop_abs

   371→class TestPrepareReview:
   372→    def test_basic_prepare(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   373→        from desloppify.review import prepare_review
   374→        f = tmp_path / "foo.ts"
   375→        f.write_text("export function getData() { return 42; }\n" * 25)
   376→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[str(f)])
   377→
   378→        data = prepare_review(tmp_path, mock_lang, empty_state)
   379→        assert data["command"] == "review"
   380→        assert data["total_candidates"] == 1
   381→        assert data["dimensions"] == [
   382→            "naming_quality", "comment_quality", "error_consistency",
   383→            "convention_outlier", "abstraction_fitness",
   384→            "logic_clarity", "contract_coherence", "initialization_coupling",
   385→        ]
   386→        assert "system_prompt" in data
   387→        assert len(data["files"]) == 1
   388→        assert "export function getData() { return 42; }" in data["files"][0]["content"]
   389→
   390→    def test_custom_dimensions(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   391→        from desloppify.review import prepare_review
   392→        f = tmp_path / "foo.ts"
   393→        f.write_text("export function bar() { return 1; }\n" * 25)
   394→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[str(f)])
   395→
   396→        data = prepare_review(tmp_path, mock_lang, empty_state,
   397→                              dimensions=["naming_quality", "comment_quality"])
   398→        assert data["dimensions"] == ["naming_quality", "comment_quality"]
   399→        assert len(data["dimension_prompts"]) == 2
   400→
   401→    def test_file_neighbors_included(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   402→        from desloppify.review import prepare_review
   403→        f = tmp_path / "foo.ts"
   404→        f.write_text("export function bar() {}\n" * 25)
   405→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[str(f)])
   406→        mock_lang._dep_graph = {
   407→            "foo.ts": {"imports": {"bar.ts"}, "importers": {"baz.ts", "qux.ts"}},
   408→        }
   409→
   410→        with patch("desloppify.review.rel", return_value="foo.ts"):
   411→            data = prepare_review(tmp_path, mock_lang, empty_state)
   412→        if data["files"]:
   413→            neighbors = data["files"][0]["neighbors"]
   414→            if neighbors:  # dep graph lookup may not match the patched rel
   415→                assert "imports" in neighbors

   420→class TestImportReviewFindings:
   421→    def test_import_valid_findings(self, empty_state, sample_findings_data):
   422→        from desloppify.review import import_review_findings
   423→        diff = import_review_findings(sample_findings_data, empty_state, "typescript")
   424→        assert diff["new"] == 3
   425→        # Check findings were added to state
   426→        findings = empty_state["findings"]
   427→        assert len(findings) == 3
   428→        # Check finding IDs follow the pattern
   429→        ids = list(findings.keys())
   430→        assert any("naming_quality" in fid for fid in ids)
   431→        assert any("comment_quality" in fid for fid in ids)
   432→        assert any("error_consistency" in fid for fid in ids)
   433→
   434→    def test_import_skips_malformed_findings(self, empty_state):
   435→        from desloppify.review import import_review_findings
   436→        data = [
   437→            {"file": "foo.ts"},  # Missing required fields
   438→            {"dimension": "naming_quality"},  # Missing file
   439→            {  # Valid
   440→                "file": "src/foo.ts",
   441→                "dimension": "naming_quality",
   442→                "identifier": "foo",
   443→                "summary": "test",
   444→                "confidence": "low",
   445→            },
   446→        ]
   447→        diff = import_review_findings(data, empty_state, "typescript")
   448→        assert diff["new"] == 1

   475→    def test_import_updates_review_cache(self, empty_state, sample_findings_data, tmp_path):
   476→        from desloppify.review import import_review_findings
   477→        # Create actual files so hashing works

   480→            (tmp_path / "src" / "foo.ts").write_text("content")

   486→    def test_import_merges_with_state(self, state_with_findings, sample_findings_data):
   487→        from desloppify.review import import_review_findings
   488→        diff = import_review_findings(sample_findings_data, state_with_findings, "typescript")

   493→    def test_import_preserves_wontfix_findings(self, empty_state, sample_findings_data):
   494→        from desloppify.review import import_review_findings
   495→        # First import
   496→        import_review_findings(sample_findings_data, empty_state, "typescript")
   497→        # Mark one as wontfix
   498→        for f in empty_state["findings"].values():
   499→            if "naming_quality" in f["id"]:
   500→                f["status"] = "wontfix"
   501→                f["note"] = "intentionally generic"
   502→                break
   503→        # Second import with same findings
   504→        import_review_findings(sample_findings_data, empty_state, "typescript")
   505→        # Wontfix should NOT be auto-resolved (it's still in current findings)
   506→        wontfix = [f for f in empty_state["findings"].values()
   507→                    if f["status"] == "wontfix"]
   508→        # The finding still exists
   509→        assert any("naming_quality" in f["id"] for f in empty_state["findings"].values())
   510→

   533→class TestScoringIntegration:
   534→    def test_review_findings_appear_in_scoring(self, empty_state, sample_findings_data):
   535→        from desloppify.review import import_review_findings
   536→        from desloppify.scoring import compute_dimension_scores
   537→        import_review_findings(sample_findings_data, empty_state, "typescript")
   538→
   539→        potentials = {"review": 2}  # 2 files reviewed
   540→        dim_scores = compute_dimension_scores(
   541→            empty_state["findings"], potentials)
   542→        assert "Design quality" in dim_scores
   543→        assert dim_scores["Design quality"]["issues"] > 0

   545→    def test_review_findings_not_auto_resolved_by_scan(self, empty_state, sample_findings_data):
   546→        from desloppify.review import import_review_findings
   547→        from desloppify.state import merge_scan
   548→        # Import review findings
   549→        import_review_findings(sample_findings_data, empty_state, "typescript")
   550→        review_ids = {f["id"] for f in empty_state["findings"].values()
   551→                      if f["detector"] == "review"}
   552→
   553→        # Simulate a normal scan with no review detector in potentials
   554→        merge_scan(empty_state, [],
   555→                   lang="typescript",
   556→                   potentials={"unused": 10, "smells": 50})
   557→
   558→        # Review findings should still be open (not auto-resolved)
   559→        for fid in review_ids:
   560→            if fid in empty_state["findings"]:
   561→                assert empty_state["findings"][fid]["status"] == "open"

   576→# ── Staleness tests ───────────────────────────────────────────────
   577→
   578→class TestStaleness:
   579→    def test_stale_after_max_age(self):
   580→        from desloppify.review import _count_stale, _count_fresh
   581→        old = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(days=60)).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   582→        state = {
   583→            "review_cache": {
   584→                "files": {
   585→                    "foo.ts": {"content_hash": "abc", "reviewed_at": old, "finding_count": 0},
   586→                }
   587→            }
   588→        }
   589→        assert _count_stale(state, 30) == 1
   590→        assert _count_fresh(state, 30) == 0
   591→
   592→    def test_fresh_within_max_age(self):
   593→        from desloppify.review import _count_stale, _count_fresh
   594→        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   595→        state = {
   596→            "review_cache": {
   597→                "files": {
   598→                    "foo.ts": {"content_hash": "abc", "reviewed_at": now, "finding_count": 0},
   599→                }
   600→            }
   601→        }
   602→        assert _count_stale(state, 30) == 0
   603→        assert _count_fresh(state, 30) == 1
   604→
   605→    def test_mixed_fresh_and_stale(self):
   606→        from desloppify.review import _count_stale, _count_fresh
   607→        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   608→        old = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(days=60)).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   609→        state = {
   610→            "review_cache": {
   611→                "files": {
   612→                    "fresh.ts": {"content_hash": "abc", "reviewed_at": now, "finding_count": 0},
   613→                    "stale.ts": {"content_hash": "def", "reviewed_at": old, "finding_count": 1},
   614→                }
   615→            }
   616→        }
   617→        assert _count_fresh(state, 30) == 1
   618→        assert _count_stale(state, 30) == 1
   619→

   623→class TestNarrativeIntegration:
   624→    def test_review_staleness_reminder(self):

   848→                "confidence": "low",
   849→            },
   850→        ]
   851→        diff = import_review_findings(data, empty_state, "python")

   900→class TestSiblingConventions:
   901→    def test_sibling_conventions_populated(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   902→        from desloppify.review import build_review_context
   903→        hooks = tmp_path / "hooks"
   904→        hooks.mkdir()
   905→        for i in range(4):
   906→            (hooks / f"hook{i}.ts").write_text(
   907→                f"export function useHook{i}() {{}}\n"
   908→                f"function handleEvent{i}() {{}}\n"
   909→            )
   910→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[
   911→            str(hooks / f"hook{i}.ts") for i in range(4)
   912→        ])
   913→        ctx = build_review_context(tmp_path, mock_lang, empty_state)
   914→        assert "hooks/" in ctx.sibling_conventions
   915→        assert "use" in ctx.sibling_conventions["hooks/"]
   916→        assert "handle" in ctx.sibling_conventions["hooks/"]
   917→
   918→    def test_sibling_conventions_serialized(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   919→        from desloppify.review import build_review_context, _serialize_context
   920→        hooks = tmp_path / "hooks"

   925→            str(hooks / f"hook{i}.ts") for i in range(4)
   926→        ])
   927→        ctx = build_review_context(tmp_path, mock_lang, empty_state)
   928→        serialized = _serialize_context(ctx)
   929→        assert "sibling_conventions" in serialized

   934→class TestFileCache:
   935→    def test_build_context_uses_file_cache(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   936→        """build_review_context should enable file cache for performance."""

   938→        from desloppify import utils as _utils_mod

   940→        f.write_text("function getData() {}\nclass Foo {}")
   941→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[str(f)])
   942→
   943→        # Cache should be disabled before and after
   944→        assert not _utils_mod._cache_enabled
   945→        build_review_context(tmp_path, mock_lang, empty_state)
   946→        assert not _utils_mod._cache_enabled  # Cleaned up after

## test_review.py Analysis

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tests/test_review.py`
**Lines**: 946 | **Classes**: 15 | **Test Functions**: 65

### Test Categories

**1. UNIT TESTS – Review Context Building (TestBuildReviewContext, 6 tests)**
- Tests for extracting naming vocabulary from source files
- Error convention detection (try/catch vs throws vs null returns)
- Codebase statistics computation (LOC, file counts, averages)
- Module pattern discovery
- Import graph summaries
- Zone distribution analysis

**2. UNIT TESTS – File Selection Logic (TestSelectFilesForReview, 7 tests)**
- Production vs test zone filtering
- Maximum files limit enforcement
- Cache hit detection (fresh files skipped)
- Cache stale detection (old reviews re-triggered)
- Content hash change detection
- Force refresh override
- Priority ordering by import popularity

**3. UNIT TESTS – Review Preparation (TestPrepareReview, 3 tests)**
- Building review payload with system prompts
- Custom dimension selection
- Neighbor file inclusion from dependency graph

**4. UNIT TESTS – Finding Import & Validation (TestImportReviewFindings, 9 tests)**
- Valid finding import and state merge
- Malformed finding rejection
- Confidence level validation with fallback
- Dimension whitelist validation
- Review cache updates
- State merging with existing detectors
- Wontfix status preservation
- Language tag setting
- Tier classification (all review findings = tier 3)
- Detail field storage (suggestion, dimension, reasoning)

**5. INTEGRATION TESTS – Scoring (TestScoringIntegration, 4 tests)**
- Review findings counted in scoring dimensions
- Review findings NOT auto-resolved by normal scans
- "review" detector registered in file-based detectors
- "Design quality" dimension (tier 4) contains review detector

**6. UNIT TESTS – Staleness Tracking (TestStaleness, 3 tests)**
- Age-based staleness detection with configurable max_age_days
- Fresh files (within threshold) identified
- Mixed fresh/stale analysis

**7. INTEGRATION TESTS – Narrative (TestNarrativeIntegration, 4 tests)**
- Staleness reminder generation ("review_stale" type)
- No reminder when cache is fresh
- No reminder when cache missing
- Headline customization with review counts during maintenance phase

**8. INTEGRATION TESTS – Registry (TestRegistry, 2 tests)**
- "review" detector in canonical registry with "Design quality" dimension
- "review" in display order

**9. UNIT TESTS – Constants & Prompts (TestDimensionPrompts, 2 tests)**
- All default dimensions have complete prompt definitions
- System prompt completeness

**10. UNIT TESTS – File Hashing (TestHashFile, 3 tests)**
- Hash consistency for unchanged content
- Hash change detection on file modification
- Graceful handling of missing files

**11. CLI TESTS – Argument Parsing (TestCLI, 6 tests)**
- `review --prepare` flag parsing
- `review --import <file>` argument
- `review --max-age <days>` numeric argument
- `review --max-files <count>` numeric argument
- `review --refresh` boolean flag
- `review --dimensions <csv>` comma-separated list

**12. UNIT TESTS – New Dimensions (TestNewDimensions, 5 tests)**
- logic_clarity dimension structure validation
- contract_coherence dimension structure validation
- initialization_coupling dimension structure validation
- All new dimensions in DEFAULT_DIMENSIONS list
- Import accepts all new dimension types

**13. UNIT TESTS – Language Guidance (TestLangGuidance, 4 tests)**
- Python guidance patterns/naming/conventions present
- TypeScript guidance patterns/naming/conventions present
- prepare_review includes lang_guidance in payload
- Language-specific guidance for Python vs TypeScript

**14. UNIT TESTS – Sibling Conventions (TestSiblingConventions, 2 tests)**
- Sibling conventions extraction (naming prefixes within same directory)
- Serialization of context with sibling conventions

**15. INTEGRATION TESTS – File Cache (TestFileCache, 1 test)**
- File cache lifecycle (enabled during build, cleaned up after)

- **Unit tests**: ~47 tests (builder, selection, import, validation, hashing, constants, dimensions)
- **Integration tests**: ~15 tests (scoring, narrative, registry, language guidance, file cache)

### Coverage Focus
The test_review.py file comprehensively tests the subjective code review system's infrastructure: context building, file selection heuristics, finding import/validation pipeline, scoring integration, narrative reminders, CLI interface, and dimension prompts for 8 review dimensions (naming_quality, comment_quality, error_consistency, convention_outlier, abstraction_fitness, logic_clarity, contract_coherence, initialization_coupling).
